# Client Refactoring Plan

**Scope:** `Proxy Client/` only. The server is **out of scope** — it is treated as a fixed contract (see *The one non-negotiable*). Mirror of `REFACTORING_PLAN.md`, which covers `Proxy Server/`.

**Last updated:** 2026-10-06 (LATEST - ✅✅ **7.4c MEASURED THE RAMP-OVERFLOW GUARD AGAINST THE REAL `method379`, AND THE ANSWER IS "BOTH": IT IS STRICTLY CONSERVATIVE AT THE LOW END AND NECESSARY AT THE HIGH END, AND THE TWO REGIMES ARE SEPARATED BY SOMETHING CHEAP ENOUGH TO COMPUTE PER FACE.** 7.4b left one question - is `TEXTURE_RAMP_OVERFLOW`, which declined 25 of a 128-face model's faces, a real decline or a guard stricter than the software? It is no longer a question of opinion.

✅ **WHAT WAS RUN, and why it is a control rather than a stroll.** 7.2j's in-range ground triangle, with all nine camera-space slots multiplied by `k`. That is a pure world scale and the mapping is invariant under it - the ramps and the walked numerator/denominator all scale by `k^2` - so the **picture is unchanged while the intermediate integers grow by `k^2` and cross 2³¹**. The screen triangle is built from the UNSCALED slots, so every `k` rasterises literally the same pixels and the only variable is where the ints sit. The real `Texture.method378`/`method379` is driven through `groundRasterIndices` at both detail levels, and four things are compared per `k` on the same pixels: the rasteriser's own picture, the exact affine form (`TextureRamps.column`), the SHADER HANDOFF (numerators at the screen vertices, interpolated, divided in the fragment), and the ramps recomputed in `long` - the mapping the nine *mean*.

| `k` | overflows? | picture vs `k=1` | exact affine form | SHADER HANDOFF | wrapped == exact |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | no | 10964/10964 | 10569/10964 | 10569/10964 | 10964/10964 |
| 2 | **yes** | **10962/10964** | **10569/10964** | **10569/10964** | **10964/10964** |
| 4 | yes | 4/10964 | 1993/10964 | 143/10964 | 0/10964 |
| 8 | yes | 0/10964 | 2797/10964 | 53/10964 | 0/10964 |
| 64 | yes | 3/10964 | 5984/10949 | 154/10963 | 2/10964 |

(size 64 shown; size 128 repeats it - 9998 and 10022 at `k = 1,2`, collapsing to 1636 and 55 at `k = 4`.)

⚠⚠ **FINDING 1: THERE IS AN OVERFLOW THAT COSTS NOTHING, SO THE GUARD IS NOT JUST CAUTIOUS THERE, IT IS WRONG.** At `k = 2` the ramps overflow and `texturedRamps()` declines - yet the rasteriser's own picture IS the `k = 1` picture at 10962/10964 pixels **and the shader handoff still reproduces it at the control's own accuracy** (10569/10964). The guard's stated reason, "no longer affine, so a shader cannot reproduce it", is **false** for that case. So the 25 of 128 faces 7.4b saw need not have been unrepresentable at all.

⚠⚠ **FINDING 2: AND THERE IS AN OVERFLOW THAT COSTS THE PICTURE, SO THE DECLINE CANNOT SIMPLY GO.** At `k >= 4` the rasteriser's own picture stops being the `k = 1` picture (≤ 4 of 10964 px survive) and the handoff collapses to ~1%. **AND THE EXACT AFFINE FORM COLLAPSES WITH IT** (1993/10964 at `k = 4`, against 10569 at the control) - which is the part that constrains the fix: `column` shares `method378`'s int arithmetic *including the wrap*, so if the wrap were merely a moved-but-still-affine mapping the exact form would keep tracking the rasteriser and only the handoff would fail. It does not. **The wrapped rasteriser is not affine in `(x, y)` at all**, so "replace the guard with a cheap surrogate for `column()`" is not available.

✅ **FINDING 3 - THE MECHANISM, read out of the printed ramps: the wrap is harmless when it CANCELS.** At `k = 2` every minor is `4x` its `k = 1` value and the two that exceed 2³¹ (`l4`, `j6`) fold back by exactly `+2^32` - a constant the **evaluation's own wrap removes again**, leaving `u = 4*u(k=1)` and `w = 4*w(k=1)` across the whole picture (`-677376000 = 4 * -169344000` at the centroid, and likewise for `w`). The ratio is then exact. At `k = 4` the `u` plane wraps twice and the `w` plane four times, so the representatives are no longer a common multiple and the ratio breaks.

✅✅ **FINDING 4 - THE DISCRIMINATOR, AND IT IS O(1) PER FACE.** The picture survives exactly when the **wrapped evaluation still reproduces the exact one**. Measured pixel by pixel, that classification agrees with picture survival **scale for scale at both detail levels** (`k = 2`: 10964/10964 wrapped==exact, picture survived; `k = 4`: 0/10964, picture gone). Both forms are closed in `(x, y)`, so a resolver can ask it of the triangle's screen vertices with no rasteriser and no atlas. ⚠ It is pinned as a **measured correspondence on this sweep**, so a resolver adopting it inherits these numbers as its evidence rather than a derivation.

⚠ **NO PRODUCTION CODE WAS TOUCHED AND THE GUARD STILL DECLINES EXACTLY AS IT DID.** This step is measurement only - `texturedRamps()` is unchanged, no live run, and still **not one GL pixel on screen**.

**Last updated:** 2026-10-06 (previous - ✅✅✅ **THE LIVE GATE RAN, AND IT EARNED ITS KEEP: IT FOUND AND CLOSED TWO BUGS THAT BETWEEN THEM MADE THE GL PATH UNREACHABLE BY CONSTRUCTION - AND THE GL SCENE NOW RENDERS. THE FRAME LATCHES, THE BATCH COMES UP, THE ATLAS UPLOADS, AND THE SCENE IS DRAWN INTO THE FBO; IT IS THEN WITHHELD AT THE READBACK BY EXACTLY ONE REMAINING THING, WHICH IS NOW A NAMED QUESTION RATHER THAN AN IMPRESSION.**

✅ **BUG 1 - THE LATCH WAS A DEADLOCK, SO NO FRAME COULD EVER LATCH, ON ANY MACHINE, IN ANY WINDOW SIZE.** `latch()` opened with `if (!batch.ready())` - and `ready()` is a pure query whose flag is only ever set by `ensure()`, whose only production caller was `beginFrame()`. So the latch asked whether a batch that nothing had tried to bring up was up yet, got `false`, declined, and never reached the call that would have set it. ⚠ **The live client printed the shape of it in one line: `GL batcher unavailable (null)`** - the `(null)` being the failure string that `ensure()` never got to assign. ⚠⚠ **The harness could not see it because its recording batch answers `ready()` with a constant `true`** - a faithful double of "a recording batch is ready", but one that models readiness as a FACT ABOUT THE WORLD rather than as the state of an initialisation. ✅ **Fix: `SceneBatch#ensure(...)` is now the seam's bring-up verb and the latch calls it**; the double starts NOT ready and comes up only when asked. ⚠ **Mutation-proved by reinstating `ready()`: exactly the 4 new checks go red (470 -> 466 passed, RESULT FAIL).**

✅ **BUG 2 - THE SIZE GATE COULD NEVER PASS, IN EITHER MODE, AT ANY WINDOW SIZE.** The viewport was the constants `DEFAULT_WIDTH/HEIGHT` (765x503), on this plan's assumption that that was "the fixed game area". ⚠⚠ **MEASURED LIVE, BOTH HALVES: FIXED mode's game raster is 512x334, and RESIZABLE mode's is 907x666** - because `Jframe.setCanvasSize` sizes the FRAME to `width + PluginSidebar.eastWidth() + insets` by `height + TitleBar.barHeight() + insets` and the game component then STRETCHES to fill it, so the drawing area is `>= 907x666` and grows with the window. **765x503 therefore matched nothing, ever**, and the live client said so: `GL viewport 765x503 does not match the drawing area 907x666`. ✅ **Fix: the frame target's size is now a PARAMETER of `ensure(int, int)`.** `GlScene` creates at it and RESTATES its two renderbuffers when a later frame's drawing area differs - nothing draws to the window's own framebuffer, so a resize is two `glRenderbufferStorage` calls and a completeness check, not a context rebuild. The size gate becomes the POST-CONDITION it should always have been ("did it come up at the size we need") instead of a comparison that could never succeed. ✅ **Verified live: the batch reports `viewport 512x334` and there is NO size-mismatch notice.**

✅✅ **AND THE DRIVER IS NOW LIVE-PROVEN AS A WHOLE, WHICH 7.2a / 7.2b-1 COULD ONLY EVER SHOW IN PIECES:** the live client prints **`GL batcher ready (shader + VAO/VBO), viewport 512x334, atlas 51 layers of 128x128, NEAREST, integer`** - so `GlScene`'s offscreen context, `GlBatcher`'s program and VAO/VBOs, and the 51-layer integer texture array all came up IN-PROCESS on the game thread, which is the half no separate-process probe can prove.

**Last updated:** 2026-10-06 (previous - ✅✅✅ **7.2c-2 (THE GROUND'S DEPTH) IS IN AND HEADLESS-VERIFIED, AND THE GROUND IS NOW DRAWN BY GL. THE FIRST FRAME THE GL PATH CAN HAND BACK HAS ARRIVED: a scene of model + UNTEXTURED ground is WHOLE and IS READ BACK into the software framebuffer.**
✅ **WHAT IS IN, in four parts.** (1) **The seam carries depth**: `SceneRasterizer.Implementation#drawGroundTriangle` and `dispatchGroundTriangle` take an added trailing `int depth`, and all THREE `WorldController` hooks pass `Fog.sceneDepth` - the tile's own value, assigned a few lines above each hook (`:1656` in `method315`, `:1963` in `method316`), so it is the SOFTWARE'S number rather than a re-derivation. (2) **`GlSceneRenderer` submits the ground** instead of declining it: untextured tiles go to `batch.triangle` with `GlFacePipeline.resolveCornerColour` (which IS `method374`'s contract - fade the CODE, then palette), and textured tiles go to `batch.textured` with `TextureRamps`' numerators evaluated at the tile's own projected corners. (3) **The depth scales are reconciled** - see the finding below, which was the real blocker. (4) `GROUND_DEPTH_DECLINE` is GONE, replaced by three narrow, named reasons.
⚠⚠ **THE FINDING, AND IT IS A BUG IN ALREADY-LANDED CODE THAT ONLY THIS STEP COULD EXPOSE: THE MODEL PATH WAS FEEDING `depthToZ` A DEPTH MEASURED FROM THE WRONG ORIGIN.** `method443` writes `anIntArray1667[v] = camZ - k2` - the vertex's camera depth MINUS THE MODEL ORIGIN'S - and `GlModelProjection` mirrors that faithfully (correctly: `GlClipper` and the projection oracle both depend on it). But `k2` DIFFERS PER MODEL, so mapping that raw value put every model at `z = 0.5` at its OWN origin, i.e. **two models at different distances became incomparable - and a shared depth buffer cannot tolerate that: a distant model would occlude a near one.** The ground's depth is ABSOLUTE, so wiring it against the relative model convention would have compared two different quantities. ✅ **Fixed at the `depthToZ` CALL SITES, not in the projection**: a new `GlFacePipeline#absoluteZ(relativeDepth)` adds `lastSceneDepth` (which IS `k2`) back before mapping, so `z` is now monotone in real camera distance and ground and models land on ONE axis. ⚠ **Nothing has ever rendered this, so it was never visible** - `GlSceneRenderer`'s methods all returned `false` until C2 - which is exactly why it survived: the only check that could have caught it compares z WITHIN a triangle (ordering is invariant to a per-model offset) and the one that compares a VALUE against the software now reads `softwareCamZ` (the absolute `anIntArray1670`) rather than `softwareDepth`.
⚠ **THE ONE GROUND DECLINE THAT REMAINS IS NAMED, NOT SILENT: `GROUND_LOWMEM_DECLINE`.** On LOW detail the software does not texturise ground at all - it darkens the three codes through `WorldController.method317`/`anIntArray485` and draws them FLAT through `method374`. Neither is reachable from `ui`, so reproducing it would be a second copy of a darkening mechanism; it declines by name instead. ⚠ **It is off in the detail level the client actually selects** (`client.setHighMem()` sets `WorldController.lowMem = false`), so it does not gate the live gate - but it IS reachable via the low-detail setting, and it is the reason the harness pins `WorldController.lowMem` explicitly rather than trusting its `true` default.
✅ **A SECOND, SMALLER FINDING WHILE WIRING IT: the seam's colour/vertex PAIRING is faithful, and that was worth proving rather than assuming.** `method374` takes `(y0,y1,y2, x0,x1,x2, ...)` - all Y then all X - so its call `method374(j6, l6, l5, i6, k6, k5, c0, c1, c2)` looks like a different vertex order from the seam's `(i6,j6, k6,l6, k5,l5)` and is in fact the SAME triangle in the SAME order. Reading the parameter names instead of the call sites would have produced a "fix" that attached every colour to the wrong corner.
⚠⚠ **STILL FLAGGED AND STILL UNFIXED, and the two paths must move TOGETHER: THE SHADE FOG FADE.** `method374` AND `method378` both fade the shade codes through `Fog.fadeHsl(shade, Fog.sceneDepth)` before rasterising (`method378`'s very first three statements), and `GlTextures.shade(block0, code, sceneDepth)` exists to express it - but neither the model path nor the ground path calls it, so both pass the raw code and fogged TEXTURED faces come out at the wrong brightness. ⚠ **This is now the largest known fidelity gap in the scene path**; it belongs in `GlFacePipeline`, not in the renderer.
**Gates: harness 456 -> 464 (8 new), warnings EXACTLY 100, `gradlew check` BUILD SUCCESSFUL (which now also runs the harness), `ReadLints` clean.** NEW CHECKS: the seam carries the tile's depth through UNCHANGED (asserted BY VALUE, since a count would pass with a constant substituted); an untextured tile is really submitted; a model+untextured-ground frame reports NO decline and IS read back; the LOW-DETAIL textured tile declines by NAME; the sentinel tile is still submitted by nobody; and the textured-z oracle now compares against the software's ABSOLUTE camera z.
⚠ **NOT CLAIMED: no live client, and still no GL pixel ON SCREEN.** The path is now capable of producing a whole frame by the harness's reckoning, but that is a RECORDING batch, not a driver - the first real pixels are the live gate's job (7.4), and this step deliberately did not touch it. ⚠ **Also not claimed: the ground's z is ONE value per TILE, not per vertex** - which matches the software's own granularity (`method315` submits a tile as a unit and fogs it with that one mean depth), but whether a grazing tile shows an artefact where a model intersects it is a LIVE question, and the per-vertex depths are available in the nine's `w` plane if it does.

**Next: THE LIVE GATE (7.4) - THE FIRST REAL GL PIXELS, AND THIS IS NOW THE ONLY THING BETWEEN THE TREE AND A SCREEN.** The headless path is complete for a whole frame: the latch, the model path, the ground seam with depth, the readback. What has NEVER run is the DRIVER - `GlScene`'s context, `GlBatcher`'s atlas upload, the depth attachment and the readback format against a real GPU. ⚠ **So the next step's shape is a LIVE RUN with `renderer=gl`, not more source reading**, and the two things to expect are exactly the two the plan already names: `GlSceneRenderer` reports `framesLatched`/`framesReadBack`/`framesDiscarded` and `frameDeclineReason()`, so the run answers "how much is left" without inference. ⚠ **The three things most likely to bite, in order: (a) THE SIZE GATE** - the canvas is fixed at `765x503` and any other drawing area declines, so a window that is not exactly that shows nothing; **(b) the SHADE FOG FADE above**, which will be visible as wrong-brightness TEXTURED geometry in fog; **(c) `GlBatcher`'s `wNum` overflow decline**, the one decline the software does NOT have. ⚠ **Two follow-ons already queued and deliberately NOT bundled: the shade fog fade (both paths, one resolver) and, if the live run shows tile-level z artefacts, per-vertex ground depth from the nine's `w` plane.**

⚠⚠ **PRE-RUN FINDING (2026-10-06, MEASURED FROM THE SOURCE BEFORE SPENDING A SESSION): THE SIZE GATE MATCHES NEITHER MODE, SO THE FIRST LIVE RUN WOULD HAVE SHOWN ZERO GL PIXELS AND NOTHING BUT A SIZE-MISMATCH NOTICE - the session would have been spent, and the only evidence it produced would have been the wrong conclusion that the driver is broken.** `GlScene`'s viewport is the CONSTANT `765x503` (`DEFAULT_WIDTH`/`DEFAULT_HEIGHT`), and `GlSceneRenderer.latch()` compares it against `DrawingArea.width/height` at the seam. That drawing area is the SCENE PRODUCER `aRSImageProducer_1165` - `bindGameRaster()` calls its `initDrawingArea()`, which is what sets `DrawingArea` - and its size is mode-dependent, measured rather than assumed:
| mode | scene producer size | where it comes from |
|---|---|---|
| FIXED | **512x334** | `setScreenMode(FIXED)` sets `screenAreaWidth/Height = 512/334`; `applyViewport()` uses `isFixed() ? 512 : ...`; both feed `client.java:7076`'s `new RSImageProducer(screenAreaWidth, screenAreaHeight)` |
| RESIZABLE | **`max(765, frameWidth) x max(503, frameHeight)`** | `setScreenMode(RESIZABLE)` sets `screenArea*` from the saved size; `refreshFrameSize()` reads the canvas |
⚠ **So the plan's own claim - "Fixed mode is served; a resizable window cannot be, yet" - is WRONG IN ITS FIRST HALF, and that half is the one that mattered: fixed mode is 512x334 and DECLINES TOO.** The only configuration that matches the constant as it stands is **RESIZABLE with the canvas at exactly 765x503** (today's settings are 959x740, so a default run declines) - i.e. the constant is the WINDOW size in the windowed layout, not the game raster in either layout. ⚠ **Consequence for the ordering, and it is the useful part: the live gate can still run UNCHANGED at 765x503, and that run verifies exactly what 7.4 exists to verify - the DRIVER (context, atlas upload, depth attachment, readback format) - because none of those depend on the size.** Making the canvas FOLLOW the drawing area is therefore a separate, now precisely-scoped step (one added frame-seam call to restate the FBO's renderbuffer storage when the size changes, plus its harness checks) and it must NOT be bundled with the first-pixels run, or a failure would be ambiguous between "the driver is broken" and "the new sizing code is broken".

⚠⚠ **THE FINDING, AND IT IS A PROOF RATHER THAN A READING: THE GROUND SEAM CARRIES NO DEPTH, AND THE SOFTWARE CANNOT SUPPLY ONE.** The seam hands over exactly what `Texture.method374`/`method378` receive, and **neither rasteriser is given a depth**. The client has **no depth buffer anywhere** - `DrawingArea` has none, and `anIntArray1472` is only row offsets - so ground-vs-model occlusion is **purely PAINTER ORDER** (`method314` draws far tiles first, so a near tile's ground covers a far model). GL composites with a depth attachment: a GL ground with no depth either **loses to every model** or **beats every model**, and **neither reproduces the software**. So the ground declines; the ground is in EVERY frame; so **every frame is currently discarded and nothing is ever read back.**
✅ **AND THAT IS ASSERTED, NOT ASSUMED.** The harness dispatches a REAL model and then a real ground triangle through the real seam, and requires the frame's only decline reason to be `GlSceneRenderer.GROUND_DEPTH_DECLINE` - so "the ground is the sole remaining blocker" is a measured claim the next step's scope can rest on. A frame with no ground IS read back, which is what makes the check non-vacuous.
✅ **WHY SHADOW MODE RATHER THAN A REAL TAKEOVER - the safety argument, and it is the design rather than a shortcut.** Consuming a draw is IRREVERSIBLE: a face declining mid-frame would leave a HOLE in the GL image with the software image already given up, and neither can be recovered. So submissions still return `false`, the software scene is drawn as well, and GL's version REPLACES it **only when the whole frame was representable**; otherwise the frame is discarded and the software image stands. **Cost, stated plainly: nothing is saved yet.** Benefit: a wrong GL frame can never be the only frame, and flipping to a takeover is then a one-line change per operation, on evidence.
✅ **THE SEAM PLACEMENT, and why both tempting alternatives are wrong.** Read back at the PRESENT and the HUD is erased - minimap, tabs, chat and the XP overlays are composited into the same buffer first. Read back earlier and part of the scene is cut off. `saveSceneBackup()` runs after the seam, so an `fpsUnlocked` frame cache preserves the GL scene rather than the software one it replaced.
⚠ **THE SIZE GATE IS A REAL CONSTRAINT, NOT A FORMALITY.** `GlScene`'s canvas is fixed at `765x503` (`DEFAULT_WIDTH`/`DEFAULT_HEIGHT`) and there is no resize path, so any frame whose drawing area differs DECLINES rather than scaling. Fixed mode is served; a resizable window cannot be, yet.

✅✅ **AND THE FRAGMENT SHADER DID NOT CHANGE AT ALL - because its formula was already the right shape.** `uv = vUvW.xy / vUvW.z` *is* `uNum/wNum` once the attribute is the numerator, so the fix was the ATTRIBUTE and not the arithmetic. That is worth stating plainly: the old code was wrong in one word (`vUvW` meant "camera space") and right in its every operation.
⚠⚠ **THE ONE THING THAT COULD HAVE MADE THIS HARDER, SETTLED BY MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN READING.** `method379` computes `col = (uNum / (wNum >> 12)) >> 6`, i.e. it **truncates the denominator before dividing**, which is NOT algebraically `(uNum/wNum) * size` - and the error is largest for SMALL `w`, i.e. for geometry near the camera, exactly where it would be noticed. If it mattered, the shader would have to emulate a bit shift. **Measured: the plain ratio and the software's shifted form agree at 10963 of 10964 pixels (size 64) and 10956 of 10964 (size 128)** - so the shader divides and skips the shift. Without that measurement the shift would have looked mandatory and C0 would have grown a shift emulation it does not need.
⚠⚠ **A REAL LATENT BUG FOUND AND FIXED ALONG THE WAY: `TextureRamps` was pinned to the SIZE-64 branch.** `method379` has TWO branches and they do not use the same shifts - `>>12`/`>>6` and clamp `4032` at size 64, `>>14`/`>>7` and clamp `16256` at size 128 - and `GlTextures.layerSize()` records that **128 is the level that runs in play** (`client.main` calls `setHighMem()` first). A resolver hardcoded to 64 would have been right in the harness and wrong on screen. The shifts are now parameters (`TextureRamps.denShiftFor(size)` / `colShiftFor(size)`) and the harness **measures both levels**.
⚠ **AND A GUARD THAT WAS RIGHT FOR THE OLD ATTRIBUTE AND WRONG FOR THE NEW ONE:** `GlBatcher.textured` rejected `w <= 0`. As camera-space DEPTH that is correct; as a ramp NUMERATOR it is signed, and the guard would have silently dropped valid textured faces. Replaced with a **zero-or-mixed-sign** test, which is the condition that actually prevents a division blow-up (`wNum` crossing zero INSIDE the triangle, where no vertex looks bad).
✅ **THE ORACLE WAS REWRITTEN, NOT RELAXED, AND GAINED A CONTROL.** It now rebuilds the expectation from the software's camera-space arrays and compares against the sink's numerators at the face's own corners - so reading the nine from the CORNERS, or evaluating at the TEXTURE TRIPLE's projection, fails. ⚠ Its companion check asserts that **NOT ONE** submitted corner still equals the camera-space triple, so the oracle cannot pass because the two forms happen to coincide. ⚠ The comparison is against `(float)` of the expected int, because a numerator can exceed 2^24 and is then not exactly an int in a float.
**THE GPU PROBE NEEDS NO CODE CHANGE, and that is a property worth naming:** `GlBatchProbe.checkMapping` queues a synthetic AFFINE triple and expects `floor((u/w)*side)` - which is exactly the real path's shape now that the triple IS an affine ramp triple. Only its doc was stale.

**Last updated:** 2026-10-06 (previous - ✅✅✅ **7.2b-2m: (C)'S DESIGN IS SETTLED, AND SETTLING IT REORDERED THE REMAINING WORK. The frame-level latch IS the right shape - but it is NOT YET SAFE, because one of the three ways a face can decline is COMMON, not pathological.**
✅ **THE DESIGN, settled.** The frame's accept/decline decision must be **ONE latch, taken at the frame's FIRST scene submission**, keyed on **GLOBAL preconditions** (context up, atlas ready) - and latching is *sound* precisely because those preconditions are global state that **cannot change mid-frame**. If they hold, the whole frame goes to GL; if not, every dispatch returns `false` and the software path draws the whole frame, unmodified. ⚠ **This is forced, not chosen:** `WorldController.method314` interleaves ground and models **per tile**, GL renders into its own buffer with its own depth attachment, and two independently depth-sorted images **cannot be merged**. So a frame has to go one way or the other, and there is no per-tile answer.
⚠⚠ **BUT THE LATCH IS ONLY SAFE IF THE PER-FACE OUTCOMES ARE TOTAL, AND THEY ARE NOT.** `GlFacePipeline.emit` has exactly **three** declinable outcomes. Read out of the code rather than assumed:
| outcome | when | reachable how often |
|---|---|---|
| `NEEDS_TEXTURE` | sink has no atlas | **global** - covered by the latch's precondition |
| `NEEDS_TEXTURE` | **a cut TEXTURED face** (near plane, `renderTypes & 2`) | ⚠⚠ **COMMON - this is the blocker** |
| `NEEDS_CLIPPING` | `GlClipper` refuses the `Texture.anIntArray1469` 2048-entry bound | rare - "only a very large model very close to the camera" |
| `NO_COLOUR` | palette null, or colour code outside `Texture.anIntArray1482` | rare-pathological |
⚠⚠ **THE BLOCKER, and why it cannot be deferred: ANY textured model face crossing the near plane currently returns `NEEDS_TEXTURE`.** That is the player's own model, NPCs you walk up to, any object you approach - **i.e. most gameplay frames**. With the latch as designed, a single such face forces the ENTIRE frame back to software, so GL would switch itself off in exactly the situation that matters. `GlFacePipeline` already documents this honestly as *"the one combination 7.2b-2e does NOT describe... a known remaining item rather than an impossibility"* - the data exists (`method485` passes the ORIGINAL unclipped texture triple alongside the clipped screen triangles and the interpolated clip shades in `anIntArray1680`), only the clipped-textured submission was never written.
✅ **AND THE TWO RARE ONES ARE FINE AS FRAME-LEVEL DECLINES, for a reason that is a real finding:** for both, the SOFTWARE ALSO fails to draw the face properly - `GlClipper.UNUSABLE` exists because the software throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` and `method443`'s bare `try/catch` **silently aborts the model mid-draw**; and a `NO_COLOUR` code would index `modelIntArray3` out of bounds and abort the same way. So declining the whole frame to software **reproduces the software exactly**, including its partial draw. No regression, no special case needed.
⚠⚠ **SECOND FINDING, and it is a correctness bug that must be fixed BEFORE wiring rather than after: `GlBatcher`'s textured path is the REFUTED shape.** It carries raw camera-space `(u,v,w)` per corner and divides per fragment - 7.2k measured that shape matching **12 of 10962** pixels while the ramp form matches 92.5%. ⚠ **Not a LIVE bug** (`GlBatcher`, `GlFacePipeline` and `GlTextures` still have **zero production call sites**), but the first textured model the wiring draws would be wrong. The fix is 7.2l's finding: carry the three **numerators**, not `(u,v,w)`.
✅ **AND THE TWO COUPLED FIXES COLLAPSE INTO ONE, which is the useful part: the clipped-textured gap needs the numerator attribute anyway.** Because the numerators are affine in screen space, evaluating them at a CLIPPED polygon's vertices and interpolating reproduces the same plane - so **the numerator change fixes the clamp/decline case AND the general textured path in the same edit**, and the screen triangle only bounds WHERE the mapping is rasterised, never what it evaluates to. That is also why the model path needs no change to its `anIntArray1643/1644/1645` indirection (7.2k): the nine come from the texture triple, and the triangle comes from the face's own corners, and the ramp form is happy with the two being different vertices.
**⚠ REVISED ORDER (this supersedes the A/B/C ordering, which is now A ✅ / B ✅ / C split into three):**
1. **C0 - numerator attributes.** `TriangleSink.textured` + `GlBatcher` + `GlFacePipeline.submitTextured` + `GlTextures` carry `uNum/vNum/wNum` instead of `(u,v,w)`. Makes the textured path *correct*, not merely present.
2. **C1 - clipped textured faces.** The `submitClipped` textured variant, which C0 unblocks.
3. **C2 - the wiring.** Frame-level latch, the **scene-finished seam** (7.2m recon: after `method146()`, before HUD compositing and before `saveSceneBackup()`), `beginFrame`/`flush`/`readInto`.
4. **7.4 - live verification** (unchanged).

**Last updated:** 2026-10-06 (previous - ✅✅✅ **7.2b-2l: THE RESOLVER EXISTS AND ITS SHADER HANDOFF IS PROVEN. `ui.TextureRamps` is the pure nine-slots-to-mapping function, and - the claim that actually matters - the mapping CAN be handed to the GPU as three linearly-interpolated numerators per vertex.**
⚠ **THE API.** `TextureRamps.of(t0..t8, textureInt1, textureInt2)` builds the nine ramps exactly as `method378` does (2x2 minors, same `<<14`/`<<8`/`<<5`, same wrapping `int` arithmetic, so it is faithful where the software wraps rather than "fixing" it). It exposes `uNumerator`/`vNumerator`/`wNumerator`, the software's own `column`/`row` (clamped exactly as `method379` clamps them), `overflows()`, and `attributeAt` for the GL handoff. It is **pure and GL-free** - `textureInt1/2` are parameters rather than reads from `Texture`, for the same reason `GlTextures.shade` takes its scene depth - so the whole thing is harness-testable with no context.
✅✅ **THE DELIVERABLE, and it is the shader handoff, not the pixel match.** `method378`'s mapping is a **ratio** of two screen-affine functions, so a shader can never reproduce it by interpolating a ratio. So a readback at present time would **overwrite every HUD pixel drawn after the scene**. The readback needs a **scene-finished seam** immediately after `method146()`, before any HUD compositing - a NEW seam, not the existing present one.

So the GL readback must land BEFORE `saveSceneBackup()`, or the backup - and therefore every reused frame - restores a stale or software scene while the live frame is GL.
⚠⚠ **THIRD AND BIGGEST: the accept/decline decision has to be WHOLE-FRAME, and that is a DESIGN problem, not plumbing.** `GlFacePipeline` can return `NEEDS_TEXTURE`/`NEEDS_CLIPPING` per FACE, and the software path interleaves ground and models **per tile** inside `WorldController.method314` - so there is no correct merge order for a frame where some tiles went to GL and others fell through. A renderer that accepts a model and then discovers an unrepresentable face cannot un-draw the tiles it already skipped. So either the pipeline must be proven `allRepresentable()` for the whole frame **before** the first tile is skipped, or the fallback has to be decided at the frame level up front. This is the main unsolved design question in (C).

### ✅ C0 - THE TEXTURED SEAM CARRIES THE RAMP NUMERATORS (DONE)

**Status: DONE and verified (harness 414/414, warnings exactly 100, lints clean). The textured path is now CORRECT rather than merely present.**

**What the seam means now.** `TriangleSink.textured`'s three floats are the screen-affine ramp NUMERATORS `(uNum, vNum, wNum)` - not camera-space coordinates, not a normalised texture coordinate. A sink interpolates them linearly in screen space (`noperspective`) and divides per fragment, which is the one shape that reproduces `Texture.method379`; interpolating a camera-space triple and dividing is a different function, measured at **12 of 10962** pixels against **92.5%** for the numerators.

| file | change |
|---|---|
| `TriangleSink` | the textured contract re-documented: numerators, and the *why* (a ratio cannot be interpolated, its two affine halves can) |
| `GlFacePipeline.submitTextured` | builds `TextureRamps` from the model's nine and evaluates `attributeAt` at the FACE's corners; declines on `overflows()` |
| `GlBatcher` | attribute re-documented (this is where the old "camera-space" claim lived); the `w <= 0` guard replaced with zero-or-mixed-sign |
| `GlTextures` | the texel-coordinate doc, which had also asserted the camera-space reading |
| `TextureRamps` | **`of(...)` is now parameterised by `denShift`/`colShift`/`size`** - it was pinned to the size-64 branch |
| `ClientHarness` | the textured-face oracle now asserts the ramp form, plus a control that the attribute is no longer camera-space; and BOTH detail levels are measured |
| `GlBatchProbe` | doc only - its synthetic affine triple already matches the real shape |

**⚠ The two decisions that had to be measured rather than read:**
1. **No shift emulation is needed.** `method379` truncates its denominator (`>>12`/`>>14`) before dividing, which is not algebraically the plain ratio - and the error is largest near the camera. The plain ratio and the shifted form agree at **10963/10964** (size 64) and **10956/10964** (size 128), so the shader divides and skips the shift.
2. **The two detail levels use different shifts** (`>>12`/`>>6`/clamp `4032` at 64, `>>14`/`>>7`/clamp `16256` at 128) and **128 is the one in play**. The harness therefore measures both.

**⚠ The shader itself did not change.** `uv = vUvW.xy / vUvW.z` was already the right arithmetic; only the attribute's meaning was wrong.

**⚠ NOT CLAIMED:** nothing is wired, and the changed seam's only call sites are tests and the probe.

**Next: C1 - the clipped-textured face**, which C0 unblocked and which is the last common reason a frame cannot be accepted.

### ✅ C1 - THE CLIPPED TEXTURED FACE (DONE)

**Status: DONE and verified (harness 434/434, warnings exactly 100, lints clean). The common reason a frame could not be accepted is gone.**

**The finding the step rests on - the ramps belong to the FACE, not the polygon.** `method485`'s textured branches pass `method378` the same nine for both triangles of a clipped quad, and `method378` builds its ramps from the nine and the screen origin alone (its screen arguments only walk spans). So clipping changes *which screen region is rasterised* and *the interpolated shades*, and nothing else.

| file | change |
|---|---|
| `GlFacePipeline` | `texturedRamps` factored out and shared by the uncut and clipped paths; the `(type & 2) != 0` branch now draws instead of returning `NEEDS_TEXTURE`; new `submitClippedTextured` |
| `ClientHarness` | 20 new checks: the software routed to the CLIPPER, built a QUAD, the scene depth matches, `clipped=1/needsTexture=0`, two triangles + texture id, no overflow, corners = the software's rebuilt polygon, shades = `anIntArray1680` (type 2) / slot A (type 3), numerators = the UNCUT ramp at the clipped point, two non-vacuity controls, and two end-to-end checks |

**⚠ Why `texturedRamps` is shared rather than duplicated:** a caller that rebuilt the ramps from the clipped polygon would get a **different, wrong** mapping that looks plausible. Sharing makes "same nine, same ramps" structural.

**⚠ Why shades and mapping need separate oracles:** shades darken the sampled texel but do not change *which* texel is sampled, so a pixel comparison cannot detect a shade mistake. Shades are checked exactly against the software's `anIntArray1680`; the mapping is checked against the rasteriser's pixels.

**⚠ The two shade sources, both covered:** type 2 uses the interpolated clip shades (and `GlClipper` already produces them in `clipColour`); type 3 uses `anIntArray1634[i]` three times and never reads `anIntArray1680`.

**⚠ End-to-end, against the rasteriser:** the pipeline's own submitted triangles are rasterised as a GL shader would (`noperspective` numerator interpolation, one divide per fragment) and sampled from a texture whose every texel encodes its own index. **870 of 941 painted pixels covered; 869 of 870 within one texel (634 exact).**

**⚠ NOT CLAIMED:** nothing is wired; still not one GL pixel on screen.

**Next: C2 - the wiring.** Frame-level latch, the scene-finished seam (after `method146()`, before HUD compositing and before `saveSceneBackup()`), `beginFrame`/`flush`/`readInto`.

**⚠ The remaining decline that the software does NOT share:** `GlBatcher`'s `wNum` overflow guard. The other two rare declines (`NEEDS_CLIPPING`, `NO_COLOUR`) fall back to a frame the software also fails to draw, so they are honest; the overflow one falls back to a frame the software draws completely, and is therefore the one case where a whole-frame latch would visibly differ.
⚠⚠ **THE HONEST READ OF THE DISTANCE, recorded so it is not over-read: the hard VERIFICATION work is largely paid for (oracles, mutation discipline, byte-exact contracts), so what remains is mostly plumbing rather than design - but "live OpenGL working" is exactly the step NOT done, there is not one GL pixel on screen today, and zero call sites means the client is still byte-for-byte the tree the 2026-10-06 01:04 golden master gate passed.** ⚠ **The operator was asked to choose the order of A/B/C and chose to pause instead, so the ORDER IS STILL OPEN: the plan's own lean was A first (it closes the 7.2b-2e gap and is the smallest), against B first for front-loading the unknowns.**) ✅✅ **7.2b-2e IS IN AND ORACLE-VERIFIED: THE TEXTURED FACES (`method378`/`method379`, render types 2 and 3) RESOLVE TO REAL INPUTS, AND `NEEDS_TEXTURE` HAS STOPPED MEANING "THE PIPELINE CANNOT DESCRIBE THIS FACE" - IT NOW MEANS ONLY "THIS SINK HAS NO SAMPLER".** `ui/TriangleSink` gained `supportsTextures()` and a 19-argument `textured(...)` carrying three corners of (projected x/y/z, texture u/v/w, shade code) plus the texture id; `ui/GlBatcher` answers `false`/`false` because it owns no atlas or sampler yet; new `ui/GlFacePipeline.submitTextured` resolves the rest and adds the `TEXTURED = 7` outcome (`OUTCOME_COUNT = 8`).

⚠⚠ **THE TWO THINGS THAT ARE NOT OBVIOUS, BOTH READ OUT OF `method484` RATHER THAN ASSUMED, AND BOTH NOW PINNED BY THE ORACLE.** **(1) The texture ID lives in the face COLOUR slot (`anIntArray1640`), not in the render-type word** - the render-type word only says *that* the face is textured (`& 2`) and *which* texture-coordinate triple to use (`>> 2`). Wiring the id from the type word is the plausible-looking mistake here and it is exactly what mutation (D) does: it fails 3 checks. **(2) The u/v/w triple is CAMERA-SPACE, not projected screen space** - the texture-coordinate vertex contributes `vertexCamX`/`vertexCamY` and `depth + sceneDepth` for w, because `method379` divides through w PER PIXEL. Passing the projected coordinates that were already divided by depth would interpolate affinely and disagree along every textured surface - the same class of error the clipper note records, in the same direction. Mutation (A) reads the coordinates at the face's OWN corner instead of the texture-coordinate vertex and fails the u/v/w check, named (`face 1 corner 0 uvw (pipeline 130.0,20.0,700.0 expected 10,20,700)`).

⚠ **The indirection, and why the fixture is built the way it is.** `renderType >> 2` selects an entry in `anIntArray1643/1644/1645`, which names three vertices in the model's own vertex array - so a face's texture plane is NOT its own corners. The fixture deliberately uses a NON-ZERO index and asserts the referenced vertices differ from the face's corners, because an index of 0 would have made the zero case pass for free. Mutation (C) hardcodes index 0 and fails the same u/v/w check (`expected 10,140,700`). ⚠ **Render type 3 (flat textured) hands slot A to all three corners and type 2 (gouraud textured) hands the three distinct slots** - so the fixture draws one face from EACH reversed pair and TOTALS them (`textured=2 gouraud=1 flat=1`), a bound on the per-type split. Mutation (B) lets type 3 read slots B and C and fails, named (`face 3 corner 1 shade 57 expected 56`).

⚠⚠ **THE ONE DELIBERATE ASYMMETRY, recorded because a later reader will want to "fix" it: the texture-coordinate index and its vertices are BOUNDS-CHECKED here, the texture ID is NOT.** The index is a property of the model and an unusable one really does mean the face cannot be described, so it is `NEEDS_TEXTURE`. The id is a GLOBAL cache index that a *sink* resolves, so bounding it against the model's own texture count would be a plausible check that rejects valid faces - the sink is the thing that gets to decline. Mutation (E) makes the pipeline claim `TEXTURED` when the sink answered `false`, i.e. reports a draw that never happened, and fails the one honesty check that keeps `allRepresentable()` from being a lie.

⚠⚠ **THE DESIGN DECISION, recorded because the alternative looks obviously better and is wrong.** A cut face has no projected vertices to submit, and the obvious move is to submit the UNCUT triangle with correct camera-space depths and let the GL near plane cut it.
```
one vertex behind  -> 4 points (a QUAD, drawn as 2 triangles)
two vertices behind-> 3 points (a TRIANGLE, from vertices 0 and 1)
front vertex first -> 3 points (a TRIANGLE, from the third emission branch)
all three behind   -> 0 points (the empty polygon)
```

⚠⚠ **TWO THINGS I GOT WRONG HERE AND FIXED, and the first is the more useful to keep.** **(1) The first version of this test PASSED while the entire emission path went untested.** All four configurations produced a back-facing rebuild, so `expectedTriangles` was 0 everywhere and every triangle-level check agreed on nothing - a green run proving nothing, which is the failure mode this plan keeps writing rules about. The cause is that this client's screen Y points DOWN, so the emission order these cases produce needs negative-leading X to be front-facing; the winding is now chosen deliberately and the cases' triangles are TOTALLED, with 4 = quad(2) + triangle(1) + triangle(1) asserted so a configuration that silently stops drawing fails. **(2) The first depth check compared the clipper's plane depth against ITSELF**, so a clipper that forgot to subtract the scene depth passed. Measured rather than argued: the software CULLED a face whose area was `-55287` and KEPT one whose area was `+1430`. ⚠ **The screen's y axis points DOWN in this client, which is why the sign looks inverted against the usual mathematical convention - recorded because "it looks wrong" is exactly what a later reader would helpfully 'fix'.** ⚠ **The reading of the formula was right and the DIRECTION was simply missing, because the test and the bucket write had been read as two separate facts; the oracle found it on its FIRST run, in both scenarios, with the mismatch named (`face 0 softwareKept=false outcome=0`). That is the argument for driving the real method instead of restating the source.**

**The oracle is the real `method443`, not a hand-computed expectation.** It drives the real method and reads back the real buckets (`anIntArrayArray1672`/`anIntArray1671`) and clipper flags (`aBooleanArray1664`) by reflection, then requires **face-for-face agreement on BOTH questions**: "did the software keep this face at all" and "did the software route this kept face to the clipper". ⚠ **Two controls, both load-bearing:** the buckets are SENTINELLED (`-1`) before the draw, so a culled draw cannot present the previous model's buckets as agreement; and the fixture pairs every triangle with a **REVERSED TWIN** - a transposition negates the signed area EXACTLY - so exactly one of each pair must survive under **any** camera, which makes "at least one survived and at least one was culled" a property of the FIXTURE rather than of the camera chosen for that run.

**The colour oracle paints real pixels.** `Texture.method374` draws the triangle the software would draw, and every painted pixel must equal `resolveCornerColour(code, sceneDepth)` for the face's OWN corner code - run twice, fog inactive and fog active, with the active run **required to differ** first, because otherwise "the fog path is tested" would mean nothing. ⚠ **The fixture pairs a three-distinct-code face with a one-repeated-code face on purpose:** a uniform corner code makes `method374`'s per-pixel interpolation the identity, so every pixel of that triangle has one colour and there is no sample point to argue about - which is what makes an exact pixel comparison meaningful rather than approximate.

**Last thing I did (previous):** 2026-10-06 - **IMPLEMENTED AND VERIFIED 7.2b-2e: the TEXTURED faces (`method378`/`method379`, render types 2 and 3) - and `NEEDS_TEXTURE` has been re-scoped from "the pipeline cannot describe this face" to "this sink has no sampler".** `ui/TriangleSink` gained `supportsTextures()` plus a 19-argument `textured(...)` - three corners of (projected x/y/z, texture u/v/w, shade code) and the texture id; `ui/GlBatcher` answers `false`/`false` because it owns no atlas or sampler yet; new `ui/GlFacePipeline.submitTextured` resolves the inputs `method484` hands `Texture.method378`, and `TEXTURED = 7` is the new outcome (`OUTCOME_COUNT = 8`). ⚠⚠ **Two things had to be read out of `method484` rather than assumed, and both are pinned by the new oracle: (1) the texture ID is stored in the face COLOUR slot (`anIntArray1640`), NOT in the render-type word, which only says the face IS textured (`& 2`) and WHICH coordinate triple to use (`>> 2`); (2) the u/v/w triple is CAMERA-SPACE - the texture-coordinate vertex's `vertexCamX`/`vertexCamY` and `depth + sceneDepth` as w - because `method379` divides through w PER PIXEL, so the projected coordinates that were already divided by depth would interpolate affinely and disagree along every textured surface.** ⚠ **The indirection is real and the fixture proves it is exercised: `renderType >> 2` indexes `anIntArray1643/1644/1645`, which name three vertices that are NOT the face's own corners - a non-zero index is used and the referenced vertices are asserted to differ from the corners, so an index of 0 cannot pass for free.** ⚠ **Render type 3 (flat textured) hands slot A to all three corners, type 2 hands the three distinct slots; the fixture draws one face from EACH reversed pair and TOTALS them (textured=2, gouraud=1, flat=1).** ⚠⚠ **One deliberate asymmetry, recorded because a later reader will want to "fix" it: the coordinate index and its vertices ARE bounds-checked (`NEEDS_TEXTURE` if unusable, because that is a property of the model), the texture ID is NOT - it is a GLOBAL cache index that a sink resolves, so bounding it against the model's own texture count would be a plausible-looking check that rejects valid faces.** ⚠ **Oracle: every texture attribute is compared against the software's own scratch (`anIntArray1665..1670`), the corner shades, and the colour-slot id, and non-vacuity is asserted first (the software draw really projected, one triangle per textured outcome, one face per reversed pair, a non-zero index indirection).** ⚠⚠ **Mutation-proved FIVE times, each reverted and each verified byte-exact afterwards: coordinate read at the face's own corner (1 check, named `expected 10,20,700`), type 3 reading slots B/C (1, named `shade 57 expected 56`), the `>> 2` indirection ignored (1), the id taken from the type word (3), and `TEXTURED` claimed while the sink answered `false` (1 - the check that keeps `allRepresentable()` from being a lie).** ⚠ **Harness 359 -> 369 (10 new), warnings exactly 100, `clean check installBin` green, `ReadLints` clean.** ⚠⚠ **Not claimed: any GL output. `GlBatcher.supportsTextures()` is still `false`, so on the production path a textured face reports `NEEDS_TEXTURE` and nothing samples anything - textures are now the ONLY outcome standing between this pipeline and the wiring step, and closing that is a DECISION (build the atlas and sampler) rather than a discovery.** ⚠ **Zero call sites, so the client is byte-for-byte the tree the golden master gate already passed.**

**Last thing I did (previous):** 2026-10-06 - **IMPLEMENTED AND VERIFIED 7.2b-2c: the render-type colour semantics - AND CORRECTED 7.2b-2b's CLAIM THAT THE SHADE MODEL IS STILL OWED.** `ui/GlFacePipeline` now resolves per-corner colours the way `Model.method484` uses them, and it emits a render-type-1 face as ONE flat colour instead of a gouraud triangle. ⚠⚠ **The premise correction came first and is the more important half: `method479` runs on the model-BUILD path (seven scene getters), and `method484` reads the same arrays the pipeline reads, so lighting arrives already applied at the seam - re-deriving it would be dead code and a double-shade risk.** ⚠ **Then the defect that reading it exposed: `method479` does not write all three corner slots. The harness now fills `anIntArray1482` deterministically itself, which is the correct call because this gate pins the RASTERISER - geometry, span stepping, the write path - not the palette. ⚠️ **One more deliberate choice: `Fog.sceneDepth` is set to 0 so `method374`'s guard (`Fog.sceneDepth > 50 && client.fogStrength > 0`) short-circuits on its first operand, which keeps the gate from loading the `client` class at all** - `client` carries heavy static initialisers and is not safely loadable headlessly, so this is a correctness constraint rather than a micro-optimisation. **Verified:** the workload is deterministic across two separate JVM processes (hash `57838498b8af2b9c834b7a82bcd37305712f1014665926449b3aaa9c88732d67` both times), the gate has teeth on its own input (one altered triangle colour changes the hash), and ⚠️ **the mutation test was run against the REAL rasteriser rather than my own workload** - a one-bit change at `Texture.method375`'s pixel write (`ai[i] = j;` -> `ai[i] = j ^ 1;`) failed the pinned assertion with `NOTE Triangle rasterisation changed`, so the gate is not merely self-consistent. Harness **49 -> 52/52**, `clean check installBin` green, warnings exactly the **100** baseline, and `Texture.java` was restored byte-clean afterwards (the revert needed a `git checkout` because my script's rewrite normalised CRLF to LF - content-identical, but the tree is now verified clean). ⚠️ **Stated gap rather than implied coverage: the gate drives the FLAT path (`method374`).** The textured paths (`method376`, `method378`) need the texture pixel tables from `Texture.method371`/`method317` and are NOT yet covered.

**Last thing I did:** 2026-10-05 - **EXECUTED 4.1c-1 (THE RASTERISATION SEAM, SUBSTRATE ONLY) AND LIVE-VERIFIED IT.** Extracted the 2D substrate out of `DrawingArea` into a new `ui/Framebuffer.java` (**417 lines**: 10 state fields - `pixels`, `width`, `height`, `topX`, `topY`, `bottomX`, `bottomY`, `centerX`, `centerY`, `anInt1387` - plus **all 18 primitives**, including the alpha-blend paths `method335`/`method340`/`method342` and the public helpers `drawLine`/`blendHLine`), leaving `DrawingArea` as a **27-line subclass** (`DrawingArea extends Framebuffer extends NodeSub`). ⚠️ **The deliberate design choice, and the reason this landed clean: an EXTRACT-SUPERCLASS rather than an extract-and-repoint.** Static members are inherited, so every existing reference keeps resolving - `DrawingArea.width`, `DrawingArea.pixels`, and the unqualified inherited uses inside `Sprite`/`Texture`/`TextDrawingArea`/`Background` - which means **the ~570 field reads across 17 files did not need touching at all.** ⚠️ **I verified that two ways rather than trusting the compile: (1) the compiler was clean on the FIRST try, which is what a true no-call-site move predicts; a GPU path would need the storage to be owned by an instance, which is a separate, larger change (4.1c-2).

positional comparison.** ⚠️⚠️ **And that exposes the flaw underneath the calibration: the very mutation test the prefix gate was built to catch — a single prefix unit resized, `R 6` → `R 7` — is gate has to be REPLACED by a gap-tolerant alignment rather than tuned, and the "resized prefix unit" mutation test has to be RE-SPECIFIED — as written it asserts that the tool detects a hand-edited log, not a client fault, because a real misread length desyncs everything after it and the LCS signal already catches that (a cascade scores ~60%, against 96.8-98.1% for a healthy different route).** (b) **`Varp.readValues`** does `anIntArray703[anInt702++] = i;` — the array is sized from `varp.dat`'s entry count but the write is driven by a *counter over opcode-3 occurrences*, so one corrupt entry carrying opcode 3 twice runs off the end; the write is now bounds-checked. ⚠️ **Both were done failing-test-first and the failure was OBSERVED before the fix** — `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` for both, not a hypothesis — then **mutation-tested**: stripping either guard back to its original form makes its own test fail again with `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` and exit 1, so neither test is vacuous (the mistake the ObjectDef test made). ⚠️ **The tests needed one new harness capability, and it cost a run to find:** `syntheticArchive` requires gzip to actually *shrink* the payload because the loader sizes its buffer to the **uncompressed** length, so a 5-byte definition throws `IllegalStateException` before the code under test is reached; the blobs are zero-padded to 128 bytes for that reason, and the readers stop at their count/terminator long before the tail. Only the assignment interleaving changed; the call order did not. ⚠️ **The test needed a real cache, and discovering why is the interesting part:** the harness loads a **hand-built gzip `StreamLoader`** containing synthetic `obj.dat`/`obj.idx` so `streamIndices` is genuinely populated — because testing the `cache == null` path would short-circuit *before* the index check and pass even with the bounds test deleted. **Mutation-proved:** removing `i < 0 || i >= streamIndices.length` made all four ItemDef assertions fail with `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` and exit 1. Also verified **CRLF-proof**, since git converts the golden master on checkout (a CRLF copy still compares identical). ⚠️ **Two bugs found and fixed on the way:** the capture's own filename was `packet-tap-.log` because `%STAMP%` inside a parenthesised block expands at *parse* time (fixed with a `goto` branch — now resolves `packet-tap-20261004-153650.log`), and `PacketTap`'s javadoc carried the same wrong "diffable" claim, now corrected. ⚠️ **The capture is safe to commit:** the username and password are RSA-encrypted in place by `stream.doKeys()` before sending, so the file holds ciphertext. **Before that,** ✅ **Built the golden-master capture tooling (0.3) so the last Phase 0 item is a short job rather than a research task** — `PacketTap.java` + `Record.bat`, plus 6 new harness assertions (**20/20**). ⚠️ **The design decision that matters:** I tapped `RSSocket`'s `read()`/`flushInputStream()`/`queueBytes()` rather than the raw socket, because **TCP chunk boundaries are not reproducible** — the same session splits its writes differently on every run, so a chunk-level log cannot be diffed and would have been a useless oracle. The client consumes the stream one protocol unit at a time, so tapping there captures the protocol *as the client understands it*, which is reproducible. ⚠️ **I verified rather than assumed that normal play is unaffected:** with `-Dsoultrail.packettap` unset, `enabled()` returns false and no file is created. ⚠️ **And I self-tested the tap**, including that every data line matches `<dir> <len> <hex>` with **no timestamps** — a log with a clock or a counter in it cannot be diffed, and a tap that silently wrote nothing would have cost the one chance to record that session. Also confirmed the class-load smoke test at **214/214** with **0 baseline classes lost**. ⚠️ **Recorded the baseline: the user confirms Nex and the 667 content work correctly right now**, which is what every later phase must preserve. ✅ **Phase 1 verified live** — the user ran the Gradle-built client against the server and reported "all works fine," so 1.1–1.4 are ticked on evidence. ⚠️ **Then the user revealed something that corrects this plan's core assumption, and investigating it took the rest of the session.** The client is **not** a pure 474 client: it is a **474 base carrying 667 (and 647) content injected through four separate ad-hoc mechanisms**, and the stated plan is to add more while keeping 474. I traced all four before writing anything down rather than taking the description at face value: **(a)** 667 NPCs — `Nex` at ids **13447+**, `Max` at 650, `Callisto`, `Blood reaver` — are **hardcoded switch cases in `EntityDef`** (471 lines, 10 cases, 15 uses of the **90xxx** custom-model range, e.g. `252 → 90252` "so Raw preload cannot clobber OG model 252"); **(b)** `CurseData667` injects 667 curse seq/spotanim/models from a `CursePack/` cache folder, **remapping frame ids into `Class36` slots 28000+** so they cannot overwrite whip/godsword/run frames sharing the same 667 file ids; **(c)** `Class36.loadFrames()` additionally **auto-loads any loose numeric `{id}.dat` in the cache root with id ≥ 100** via a **647-named reader** (`load_647`), e.g. `3502.dat` for Nex; **(d)** `SpotAnim.readValues667` coexists with the 474 reader, and `CurseData667` **hand-reimplements 667 opcode skipping**. ⚠️ **Why this mattered enough to change the plan rather than just be noted:** the contract table listed "the cache format" as one frozen surface, but there are four sources relying on three undocumented collision-avoidance schemes, so a refactor that reshuffles `Class36` slots, model load order, or the `EntityDef` id range can **silently corrupt 474 content with no crash and no error** — the worst failure mode this plan exists to prevent. Compiling both ways proved **exactly those 4 classes change and no others**, and the fix is verified present in the rebuilt `bin/`. ⚠️ **1.3:** warning baseline recorded at **0 errors / 100 warnings** (77 serial, 9 rawtypes, 5 unchecked, 5 deprecation, 2 static, 1 empty), reproduced independently with a direct `javac` run using identical flags. ⚠️ **1.4:** `ObjectCollisionSizes` now embeds the SHA-256 of the `objectSize.cfg` it came from, and `gradlew check` fails if it ever drifts — mutation-tested (all-zero hash fails loudly naming both hashes, restore passes). Regenerating changed **exactly 8 lines and left the 28 KB collision table byte-identical**. ⚠️ **Whole build verified:** `gradlew clean build` green, harness **14/14**, **213/213 classes load**, and **no baseline class was lost**. ⚠️ **Phase 1 is deliberately NOT ticked:** the client rule requires a live client against the server, and `bin/` has changed. **Before that,** **Phase 0.1, 0.2 and 0.4 — the client now has a verified safety net, and for the first time, tests.** ⚠️ **0.1:** backed up `Proxy Client/` to `_client-backup-20261004-145741`, checked **byte-exact** — **423 files *and* 6,415,094 bytes matching the original**, not merely "the copy command returned success". ⚠️ **0.2:** wrote `BIN-BASELINE.sha256` (**213** classes) and `SRC-BASELINE.sha256` (**131** sources) into that backup. ⚠️ **0.4, the one that mattered:** built `Proxy Client/test/ClientHarness.java` and `Proxy Client/RunTests.bat`, which run **14/14 green headlessly against the compiled client in `bin/`** — no display, no server, no game loop — covering `Stream` round-trips, **byte order** (asserted against the raw buffer, because a round-trip passes even if both halves are little-endian), sign-extension, 3-byte reads, the `g2`/`g4` aliases and bit access. ⚠️ **I mutation-tested the harness**, because "the tests pass" is worth nothing if the harness cannot fail: corrupting a single expectation gave `passed: 13 failed: 1`, `RESULT: FAIL` and **exit code 1**. **Two findings fell out of this** and are now recorded under "Known broken": client sources must stay **ASCII** (`javac` here defaults to Cp1252 and rejected my harness for containing a warning symbol and em dashes), and `Stream.readUnsignedWord()` **silently returns a hardcoded 1795** on an out-of-range read rather than throwing — now pinned as a Phase 2 tripwire. **Before that,** **Phase 0.6 — committed the seven untracked load-bearing client classes** (`f3ec2e1`). Before committing I re-verified the claim rather than trusting my own earlier note, because the consequence of being wrong was a large commit against a tree with no fallback: `client.java` really is unmodified vs `HEAD`, it really does reference `LwjglPresent` and `OverlayRefresh`, all seven files really do exist on disk, and **none were gitignored** (an ignored file would have made this a `-f` problem rather than an untracked one). ⚠️ **My first enumeration was wrong and I caught it:** a `git status --porcelain | Where-Object { $_ -match '^\?\?' }` filter returned *nothing*, which would have read as "already tracked". `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard` is the authoritative form and returned all seven — the lesson is that a filter on porcelain text is a convenience, and `ls-files` is the check. My commit deleted nothing.

Before that, **wrote this plan.** Before writing it I measured the client rather than trusting the brief, and **two findings changed the shape of the work**, so they are stated up front:

1. ⚠️ **The client already runs on Java 25 — only the *compile target* is ancient.** `Run.bat` launches a bare `java`, and the default JDK on this machine is **25.0.4**, so the game is already running on a modern JVM (and it works — it was played during the server session). The only thing stuck in the past is `Compile.bat`'s `-source 1.7 -target 1.7`, and that still works **only because `Compile.bat` hardcodes JDK 8's `javac`** (`C:\Program Files\Java\jdk1.8.0_202\bin\javac.exe`). Plain `javac` on PATH is 25, which **rejects `-source 1.7` outright** (support for source/target 7 was removed in JDK 21). So the build works by accident of two different JVMs being involved, and the Java upgrade is a *build* problem, not a *runtime survival* problem.
2. ⚠️ **Java version has literally nothing to do with server compatibility.** The user's stated top priority is "no issues with compatibility with the server". The client and server are **separate JVMs talking over a socket**, so their Java versions need not match and cannot conflict. The real contract is the **wire protocol and the cache format** — see below. This matters because it means the riskiest-sounding item (the Java upgrade) is *not* where the server risk lives, and the item that *is* risky (anything touching packet or cache reading) is small and testable.
3. **`openGlEnabled` is not OpenGL.** The client has no 3D GPU path at all. `openGlEnabled`/`GlPresent` only switch on Java2D's `sun.java2d.opengl` pipeline — i.e. accelerated **blitting of a software-rasterised framebuffer**. The `LWJGL` jar in `deps/` is **LWJGL 2.x and is imported by zero source files**.
4. ⚠️ **LWJGL + AWT parenting was already tried here and deliberately abandoned.** `LwjglPresent.java` is a stub whose every method returns `false`, with this comment: *"LWJGL `Display.setParent` is not used. Parenting a native GL window onto the AWT applet deadlocks Windows when the frame loses or gains focus (both AWT and LWJGL pump the same HWND)."* **Any OpenGL plan that begins with `Display.setParent` is re-walking a known dead end.** Phase 7 is built around that lesson.
5. **One of the two reference repos the user supplied is effectively empty.** `ubjelly/317-OpenGL` contains three files (~2.7 KB total) and no renderer. `Elvarg-Client-Public` is a full RuneLite client and is the only usable reference. Details in the review section.
6. ⚠️⚠️ **The client's committed state (`HEAD`) cannot compile — the working tree is load-bearing.** This is the most urgent thing in this document. `client.java` is **unmodified vs `HEAD`** yet references **`LwjglPresent` and `OverlayRefresh`**, and **neither is tracked by git at all**. In total the client has **nine untracked classes that tracked source depends on** - up from seven, and ⚠⚠ **THIS ITEM GOT WORSE IN 7.2a, RECORDED RATHER THAN LEFT TO BE FOUND: `ui/GlScene.java` and `ui/GlSceneRenderer.java` are BOTH untracked, yet tracked `src/ui/RendererConfig.java:78` now does `GpuRenderer.install(new GlSceneRenderer(requested))`, so Phase 7's own entry point is one of the load-bearing untracked files**, and they are heavily used: `OverlayRefresh` is referenced by **39 files**, `GroundItemLists` by **20**, `ChatboxItemSearch` by **18**, `HiscoresPanel` by **6**, `CurseData667` by **3**, `LwjglPresent` (by `client.java`) and `JavaUncompress` by **1** each - plus `GlScene` and `GlSceneRenderer`, reached from `RendererConfig`. **A fresh clone, `git clean -fd`, or `git checkout .` therefore destroys the client build outright.** This is the same "untracked-until-lost" pattern the server plan already recorded as having destroyed two cluster tools — it has now hit the client, and here it is not a tool but the product. `Phase 0.6` commits them. ⚠️ **The server is *not* affected:** `ConnectionPool` (untracked `server/util/`) and `MusicState` (untracked) are referenced **only from files that are themselves modified in the working tree**, so the server's `HEAD` still compiles. The asymmetry is worth remembering before assuming the two trees are in the same state.

**Next step (CURRENT - 7.2b-2f is in, so this is the remaining half of (A)):** **FINISH (A): PUT A REAL TEXTURE PATH INTO `ui/GlBatcher`.** The SHADE POLICY is now measured (`ui/GlTextures`) and the DATA accessor exists (`Texture.texturePixels`), so what is left is plumbing: upload the 51 textures as a `GL_TEXTURE_2D_ARRAY` (64 square when `lowMem`, 128 square otherwise - and `client.main` calls `setHighMem()`, so plan for 128), add a UV attribute and a layer attribute alongside position/colour, transcribe `ui/GlTextures.brightnessBlock`/`extraShift`/`blockColour` into the fragment shader with the arithmetic done on the PACKED int (the channel borrow is load-bearing, not decoration), sample with `GL_NEAREST` and `GL_CLAMP_TO_EDGE`, and flip `supportsTextures()` to `true`. ⚠ **Then extend `tools/gl-batch-probe` with a textured case and mutation-prove it, because the harness cannot see the shader at all - LWJGL is deliberately absent there.** ⚠ **Also worth closing while in here: the block ARITHMETIC is still transcription-only.** ⚠ **Then (C) the wiring and the present, and (B) the ground resolver (the only piece with real unknowns; it CANNOT be deferred, since the software interleaves ground and models per tile and a models-only takeover has no correct merge order).**

**Next step (superseded by 7.2b-2f):** **⏸ THE THREE REMAINING PIECES BEFORE LIVE GL, in the order this session measured them: (A) `GlBatcher` texture support - an atlas, a sampler and a UV attribute, so `TEXTURED` becomes reachable in production; (B) THE GROUND RESOLVER - derive how `drawGroundTriangle`'s nine camera-space ints (`t0..t8`) plus `flatMesh` become texture coordinates, by reading `WorldController.method315/316`; it is the ONLY piece with real unknowns and it CANNOT be deferred, because the software path interleaves ground and models per tile and a models-only takeover has no correct merge order; (C) THE WIRING AND PRESENT - `GlSceneRenderer` driving `GlBatcher.beginFrame()` into `GlFacePipeline`, then `flush()` and `readInto` the software `Framebuffer.pixels`, with `presentGameFrame` returning `true`.** ⚠ **A is the plan's lean (smallest, and it closes the gap 7.2b-2e flagged); B first is the alternative, to front-load the unknowns.** ⚠⚠ **CONTEXT FOR THE RESUME, MEASURED SO IT NEED NOT BE REDONE: the SEAMS are hooked (`Model.java:2337`, `WorldController.java:1682/1718/1975`, `client.java:806`) and the CONTEXT bring-up is live-proven, but `GlFacePipeline`/`GlModelProjection`/`GlClipper`/`GlBatcher` have ZERO call sites - all javadoc links - and `GlSceneRenderer` declines everything. Not one GL pixel is on screen, and the client is still byte-for-byte the tree the 01:04 golden master gate passed.** ✅ **What is IN and oracle/GPU-verified: `ui/GlBatcher` (the GPU triangle sink, GPU-probe + 3 mutations), `ui/GlModelProjection` (the transform, against the REAL `method443`, now including the CAMERA-SPACE pair, 2 mutations caught), `ui/GlFacePipeline` (skip / cull / flag / emit; census oracle against the REAL `method483` buckets and clipper flags; colour oracle against the REAL `Texture.method374`; the per-render-type colour semantics - flat vs gouraud and the two fog orders - against the REAL `Texture.method376`; and now the clip branch), `ui/GlClipper` (the near-plane rebuild, against the REAL `method485`'s own rebuilt polygon, 3 mutations caught), and the TEXTURED render types 2/3 (the coordinates, camera-space u/v/w, shades and id against the REAL `method484`'s own texture inputs, 5 mutations caught). Harness 369/369, warnings exactly 100.** ⚠ **The texture step has now SPLIT IN TWO, and 7.2b-2e finished the harder half: the RESOLUTION (which vertices, which coordinates, which shades, which id) is done and oracle-verified; what remains is the BATCHER half** - no atlas upload and no sampler exist yet, which is why `GlBatcher.supportsTextures()` answers `false` and the census counts a textured face as `NEEDS_TEXTURE` even though the pipeline has resolved it. ⚠✅ **`method481`'s SHADE regime is now SETTLED rather than pending: for `renderType & 2` the corner slot holds `127 - clamp(light)`, a SHADE and not a colour code - so `submitTextured` passes the RAW code through for the sink to shade with, and does NOT palette it. That distinction is why the textured types could not be folded into the colour path and why they needed their own resolution step.** ⚠ **`allRepresentable()` is the gate the wiring step must ask, and it has narrowed twice: lighting was never owed (7.2b-2c), and the near plane is now handled (7.2b-2d).** ⚠⚠ **CORRECTED, and this is the part that CHANGES THE PLAN rather than advancing it: LIGHTING IS NOT A REMAINING PIECE.** 7.2b-2b listed `method479`/`method481` as owed; 7.2b-2c established they are not, from the call graph: every scene getter runs `method479` on the model-BUILD path (`ObjectDef:595`, `EntityDef:307`, `ItemDef:506`, `Player:300`, `NPC:86`, `Animable_Sub3:61`, `Animable_Sub4:54`) and `method484` reads the same arrays the seam reads, so the GL path receives lighting already applied. Re-deriving it would be dead code AND a double-shade risk. ⚠ **What genuinely remained `method481`-shaped was confined to the TEXTURED types - the slot holds a SHADE, not a colour code - and 7.2b-2e has now settled it: the raw shade is resolved and passed to the sink, which is what the texture path needed before it could be described at all.** ⚠ **Then the wiring, whose shape 7.2b-1 already forced: the scene goes over WHOLE (ground and models interleave per tile, so a partial takeover has no correct merge order), both seams route into `GlBatcher`, and the readback lands in the shared `static Framebuffer.pixels` at the point the scene pass ENDS and BEFORE the UI is drawn. ⚠ And the depth attachment is LOAD-BEARING, not a nicety: faces are emitted in FACE-INDEX order rather than the software's back-to-front bucket order, so the two agree only because a depth test resolves them.** ⚠ **Keep 7.3's rule: the SOFTWARE path stays the default and the fallback via the existing `renderer` key.** ⚠ **The 7.2b-2a test-order interaction is NO LONGER CARRIED FORWARD: identified (the `private static` `aBooleanArray1664`/`1663` left set by a real `method443`, read by `method484`), fixed in that test, workaround removed, re-verified by an order probe (292/292).** ⚠ **6.5.6's live gate is CLEARED, 7.2b-2b has had its no-regression gate, and 7.2b-2c touches only classes with zero call sites - so no live gate applies to it.
