**Verification:** ✅ **As of 2026-10-04 the client has a real build, headless tests, and a protocol oracle.** `gradlew clean build` is green: harness **46/46** (`gradlew harness`), stale-class report (`gradlew reportStaleClasses`), and cfg-freshness (`gradlew verifyObjectCollisionSizes`) all run as part of `gradlew check`. ⚠️ **Harness coverage moved from one class to ten (`Stream`, `PacketTap`, `ItemDef`, `EntityDef`, `ObjectDef`, `VarBit`, `Varp`, `IDK`, `ObjectManager`, `StreamLoader`) — but "ten of 131 sources" is still the honest framing**, and `client.java`, `Model` and the cache readers remain uncovered. ⚠️ **And the protocol oracle now refuses to be vacuous: `compare-capture.ps1` fails (exit 2) when the two captures hash the same, because that means it was handed the golden master twice and its "IDENTICAL" would have meant nothing** — which is exactly what happened on 2026-10-04 when no fresh capture existed, and is why a re-record is now a required step rather than a formality. ⚠️ **The synthetic-cache tests are the strongest ones so far, and the reason is worth keeping:** they build a **valid synthetic cache** instead of leaving the cache unloaded, because the unloaded path short-circuits before the check being tested — so they would have passed with the bug still present. ⚠️ **2.2 then added the sharper lesson: passing tests can still be vacuous.** My first `ObjectDef` test asserted the right property against the wrong input (`forID(-1)`) — because `ObjectDef`'s constructor defaults `type` to `-1`, the cache scan at the top of `forID` returns *before* the clamp — and it **passed with the clamp deleted**. Mutation testing is the only reason that was caught; the rewritten test (`forID(-2)`, then asserting it is the *same object* as `forID(0)`) is itself mutation-proven to fail with `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` and exit 1. ⚠️ **The harness tests `build/classes/java/main`, NOT `bin/`** — and `bin/` is what `Run.bat` plays. The two differ until `installBin` runs, which would mean *testing one thing and playing another*. That is not hypothetical: after the 2.1 edit `bin/ItemDef.class` was **stale**, so a live session at that moment would have exercised the old crashing code and produced a misleading result. Caught by disassembling `bin/` and `build/` and comparing; `installBin` was run and the two guard bodies are now byte-identical. **Run `installBin` (or a full `build`) before any live test.** ⚠️ **The 0.3 capture tooling is itself tested** (6 of those 20 assertions), including a check that its log has **no timestamps** (a clock or counter would defeat even the structural comparison, since every line would then differ) — and that `PacketTap` is inert when disabled. ⚠️ **Every one of those checks was mutation-tested** — the harness fails with exit code 1 on a wrong expectation, and the cfg check fails loudly on a tampered hash — because a check that has never been seen to fail is not evidence. Additional one-off verification: all **213/213 `bin/` classes load** (catches missing supertypes a green compile hides), and **no baseline class was lost** against the Phase 0.2 manifest. ⚠️ **But the harness still covers one class (`Stream`) out of 131 sources** — it proves the method, not the coverage. ✅ **`gradlew compareCapture` now DOES establish protocol compatibility — structurally.** It diffs a fresh capture against `golden-master/session-20261004.log` and is mutation-tested to fail on a deleted unit or on **a single resized unit**. ⚠️ **But it cannot see payload contents** (the same ISAAC/`Math.random()` encryption reason), so it cannot detect a wrong animation: ⚠️ **474 asset integrity is still NOT automatically protected**, and the screen-recording half of 0.3 is the only thing that covers it. ⚠️ **It also only means anything for the same route and roughly the same world state** — server tick timing and NPC positions legitimately shift units, so a dirty run is a signal to look, not automatic proof of a bug. ✅ **As of 2026-10-04 evening that "signal to look" is an actual verdict:** the tool scores **common-prefix length + LCS similarity** rather than raw positional divergence, because a fresh human session can never match the golden master positionally — one extra client packet shifts every unit after it. Calibrated on the real captures (89.1% same-protocol/different-route vs a 60-62% destroyed-structure floor; threshold 75%, plus a bounded 600-unit prefix ALIGNMENT — the earlier positional prefix gate was proven false on same-binary captures and replaced, see 0.5). ⚠️ **Stated limit: a small change confined to the route-dependent tail is indistinguishable from a different route and will pass; a break in the login/load prefix or any cascade (which is what a real desync is) will not.**

**Known broken / half-finished (found while writing this plan, not yet actioned):**
- ✅ ~~⚠️⚠️ **`HEAD` cannot compile the client — seven required classes are untracked.**~~ **FIXED (commit `f3ec2e1`, Phase 0.6).** `client.java` was unmodified vs `HEAD` yet referenced the untracked `LwjglPresent` and `OverlayRefresh`; six untracked classes were referenced across the tree (`OverlayRefresh` 39 files, `GroundItemLists` 20, `ChatboxItemSearch` 18, `HiscoresPanel` 6, `CurseData667` 3, `JavaUncompress` 1). **A fresh clone or `git clean -fd` would have destroyed the client build.** All seven are now tracked and `HEAD` compiles. (The server's `HEAD` was unaffected throughout — verified.)
- ⚠️ **A malformed noted-item definition hard-crashed the client** — `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException: Index -1 ... ItemDef.forID`. Reported live on 2026-10-04 while ranging Rock Crabs. `ItemDef.readValues` takes `certTemplateID` from opcode **98** and `certID` from opcode **97** *separately* and leaves both at `-1`; an entry with 98 but no 97 made `forID` call `toNote()`, which called `forID(certID)` = `forID(-1)`, and line 178 did `streamIndices[-1]`. **This was pre-existing and unrelated to the server refactor** (the server cannot send it: every ground-item send passes `ItemHandler.createGroundItem`'s `if (itemId > 0)` guard, and `ItemAssistant.dropAllItems`' `-1` is filtered there). 🟡 **GUARDED AND LIVE-VERIFIED (Phase 2.1, 2026-10-04)** — implemented, mutation-tested, and confirmed on a live session (logged in, played, exited code 0). `forID` now returns a shared defaulted stand-in for any out-of-cache id, so **every** caller is protected rather than just `toNote`, and `toNote` additionally bails out on a missing cert id or null/empty name. ⚠️ **Still worth knowing that nothing prevents the malformed definition itself** — the guard makes it harmless rather than impossible; the underlying `obj.dat` entry is unchanged.
- ⚠️ **The same shape was real elsewhere too — four instances found, fixed and pinned in Phase 2.2; some remain unaudited.** The pattern is any code that indexes an array or a `Stream` with a cache-derived value. ✅ **Fixed (2.2), all three crashing during cache load at startup:** **`VarBit.unpackConfig`** indexed `Varp.cache` with the target varp id read from `varbit.dat`; **`Varp.readValues`** wrote into its index table past the end when one definition carried opcode 3 twice; **`IDK.readValues`** wrote `anIntArray659/660/661[i - 40/50/60]` through opcode ranges ten slots wide into six/six/five-slot arrays, so opcodes 46-49, 56-59 and 65-69 crashed. ⚠️ **The `IDK` fix also had to preserve the read while skipping the store** — folding the read into the guard would desync `idk.dat` silently, and a dedicated test now pins that. ⚠️ **Also fixed (2.2): `ObjectManager`'s five `Flo.cache[id - 1]` sites** (`:113`, `:127`, `:189`, `:222`, `:243`), where `id` is an overlay byte from the landscape data and only `id > 0` was checked. ⚠️ **These did NOT crash — they failed SILENTLY**, because `method171` wraps its whole body (36-450) in a `try` whose `catch` is **empty**, so an out-of-range id abandoned the rest of that region's blending and lighting with no message, no log and no crash. **The plan's earlier "throws during region load" claim was wrong** and is corrected in the 2.2 section; the guard is now a single `boundedFloorId` clamp applied where each id is read. ⚠️ **Still outstanding:** the `streamIndices[i] == 0` case (an id in range that the cache never defined parses from `obj.dat` offset 0); ⚠️ `ObjectDef.forID(-1)` returns an uninitialized cache entry for the same reason (`type` defaults to `-1`); ⚠️ `CurseData667` indexes `Animation.anims[]` with an anim id derived from `spotanim.dat` (667 path — Phase 6.5). ⚠️ **`EntityDef`, `ObjectDef`, `Flo`, `Animation`, `SpotAnim`, `Class36.method531` and `client.java`'s `safeAnimation`/`safeGfxAnimation` were checked and are already guarded.**
- ✅ ~~⚠️ **The client's `-source 1.7` build is one PATH change away from failure.**~~ **FIXED (Phase 1.2, commit `7c8f1987`).** `-source/-target 1.7` is gone, replaced by `options.release = 8` through Gradle, so the build no longer depends on a hardcoded JDK 8 path and works from plain `javac` 25.
- ⚠️ **`Proxy Client/src/ObjectCollisionSizes.java` is a *generated* file** that must stay in sync with `Proxy Server/Data/objectSize.cfg` via `Proxy Client/tools/generate-object-collision-sizes.ps1`. Editing the cfg without regenerating silently drifts client collision from server collision. The generator validates that no footprint exceeds 4 bits per axis.
- ⚠️ **`deps/lwjgl.jar` (LWJGL 2.x, ~1.05 MB) and `deps/lwjgl-platform-natives-windows.jar` are dead weight** — no source imports `org.lwjgl`. Phase 8 decides whether LWJGL 3 replaces them (Phase 7) or they go.
- ⚠️ **`Proxy Client/dump4/` (dated 2014) and `Proxy Client/jdt-bin/` are stale scratch/build output** — Phase 8 cleanup.
- ⚠️ **The 474/667 revision chooser in `Loader` is inert, and its label contradicts its code.** It offers "474 characters" / "**634** characters", passes `"474"` / `"667"`, and `client.main` forwards the argument to `new Jframe(args)` — **whose constructor never reads it**, so both buttons launch a byte-identical client. ⚠️ **Left exactly as-is on purpose:** it looks like an obvious bug to "fix", but making the choices *do* something would change behaviour and could break the live client, so it is recorded rather than touched. It matters only because it must not be mistaken for a real revision switch when reasoning about the data model — 667 content is always loaded.
- ⚠️ **The 667/647 injection layers rely on undocumented ID-avoidance conventions** with nothing enforcing them — `Class36` slots **28000+** for CursePack frames, **90xxx** for remapped models, NPC ids **13447+**. Two silent-failure modes follow: a new injection colliding with 474 data, or Phase 8 deleting a "magic number" that is actually load-bearing. Added to the frozen-contract section above; Phase 6.5 is where this gets designed properly.
- ✅ ~~⚠️ **Client source files must stay ASCII.**~~ **This was WRONG and is corrected here.** Thirteen of the 131 sources have contained non-ASCII all along. The accurate finding is: **the build's source encoding is load-bearing, and it was silently wrong.** `Compile.bat` passes no `-encoding`, so `javac` uses the platform default (**Cp1252 here**) while the sources are **UTF-8**. In nine files those bytes sit only in comments, so bytecode was unaffected — which is why this went unnoticed. **Phase 1.2 fixes the build to read UTF-8**, which also matches `tools/generate-object-collision-sizes.ps1`, which already *writes* UTF-8. ⚠️ **The lesson is worth keeping:** I first read the symptom ("my file won't compile because of a warning symbol") as a rule to obey rather than a signal to investigate, and the real bug was in the shipped client, not in my file.
- ✅ ~~⚠️ **Four player-visible strings are compiled as mojibake.**~~ **FOUND AND FIXED (Phase 1.2, commit pending).** Reading UTF-8 bytes as Cp1252 turned each em dash (`E2 80 94`) into three wrong characters, and four of them were in **string literals**, so players saw the corruption:
  - `ChatboxItemSearch` — `"<mojibake> click an item"` → `"— click an item"`
  - `FriendListPlugin` — `"Friends <mojibake> 3/200"` → `"Friends — 3/200"`
  - `Interfaces` — `"Crisp edge smoothing only <mojibake> no screen blur"`
  - `PluginSidebar` — `"Crisp silhouette edges only <mojibake> no blur"`
  ⚠️ **The blast radius was measured before the fix, not assumed:** compiling the same sources both ways changed **exactly those 4 classes and no others**, and the new `bin/` was re-checked to confirm a real U+2014 is now present and the mojibake is gone. ⚠️ **This is a genuine behaviour change** — the first thing in this plan that alters what a player sees — which is exactly why Phase 1.1 is not marked done without a live session.
- ⚠️ **`bin/` carries 7 stale `.class` files that no source produces**: `Data667.class` (an orphan with **zero references anywhere** — `CurseData667` is a different, live class), `LwjglPresent$1..$5.class` (anonymous inner classes from before `LwjglPresent` was reduced to a stub, so they are unreachable), and `PluginSidebar$PluginRow$4.class` (same, for a `PluginRow` that now has fewer anonymous classes). Cause: `Compile.bat`'s `robocopy /E /IS /IT` **never purges**, exactly as the Phase 0.2 note predicted. ⚠️ **Deliberately NOT deleted by the new build**: purging changes what ships, and `LwjglPresent$1..$5` also corroborate that the abandoned LWJGL attempt was once real code. The Gradle build reports them (`gradlew reportStaleClasses`, wired into `check`) and Phase 8 removes them.
- ⚠️ **`Stream`'s out-of-range reads fail silently instead of throwing** — a desync hazard. ⚠️ **DELIBERATELY KEPT as of 2026-10-04, NOT a Phase 2 target** (see 2.3): these accessors read every packet field and every cache definition, so changing what an out-of-range read returns would change how a desync presents, which the frozen-contract rule forbids. `readUnsignedWord()` wraps its body in `catch (Exception e) { return readUnsignedWord2(); }`, and `readUnsignedWord2()` is a **hardcoded `return 1795;`** (`readUnsignedByte2()` likewise returns a hardcoded `-263`, and `method421()` returns `-1` on any exception). So reading past the end of the buffer does not raise an error — it yields a plausible-looking wrong number, which is harder to diagnose than a crash. Two assertions in `ClientHarness` pin the **current** behaviour as a tripwire, so any future change to the sentinel has to be deliberate and has to move them with it.

## The rule this repo must keep (mirrors `.cursor/rules/refactor-progress.mdc`)

- At the start of every task, read the "Current status" block above and continue from "Next step".
- After each completed step, update that block: "Last updated", "Last thing I did", "Next step", and any counts you verified.
- Before stopping mid-task, update the block first.
- Edit only the status block and the relevant phase section. Never rewrite the rest of the file.
- **Never mark a step done until it has been verified** — and for this client that means *verified on a live client against the real server*, not merely "it compiles".
- **Flag a phase the moment it is blocked or contradicted**, in the phase section and in the status block. A phase that turns out to be wrong is more useful recorded than silently dropped.

---

## The one non-negotiable: compatibility with the server

The user's stated highest priority. Restated precisely so it is testable rather than a feeling:

**The client must always be able to log into and play the *current, unmodified* server.** Concretely, the following are **frozen contracts** and every phase must leave them byte-identical unless the phase is explicitly about them:

| Contract | Where it lives | Why it breaks compatibility |
|---|---|---|
| **Packet opcodes and their payload layouts** | `client.java`'s incoming-packet switch, and `Stream` read helpers | A reordered read desyncs the stream and every later packet is garbage. This is the single most dangerous surface in the whole plan. |
| **Login handshake** (ISAAC seeds, `CreateUID`/UKEY, the login block) | `client.java`, `ISAACRandomGen`, `CreateUID` | A mismatch = "invalid login" with no useful error. |
| **Cache format and indices** (474 cache; archive/file numbering; `Skins.dat`, `Frames.dat`, loose `*.dat` frames) | `StreamLoader`, `Decompressor`, `Class36`, `OnDemandFetcher` | The client reads what the server/cache serves. Renumbering a single index silently loads the wrong model. |
| **On-demand fetching** (`OnDemandFetcher` requests vs what the server answers) | `OnDemandFetcher` | Desyncs model/anim loading mid-game. |
| **Object collision footprints** | `ObjectCollisionSizes` (generated) vs `Data/objectSize.cfg` | Client and server must agree or players clip through/path into scenery inconsistently. |

**Consequences for how this plan is written:**
- ⚠️ **Java version is *not* on this list, deliberately.** It cannot be — separate JVMs. Do not let the version upgrade be treated as risky to the server.
- ⚠️ **No phase may "tidy up" a packet read.** Refactors of `client.java` must be *purely mechanical and behaviour-preserving* (rename/move), with the packet switch treated as frozen code that moves but never changes.
- ⚠️ **Cache-reading changes are the other frozen surface.** Phase 3 *adds bounds checks around* reads; it does not change what is read or in what order.
- The client must be regression-tested against the **same server build** before and after each phase, using the Phase 0 golden-master baseline.

### ⚠️ The contract was missing a whole layer: this is NOT a pure 474 client

**Added 2026-10-04 after the user revealed that the client already carries 667-revision content and is meant to carry more.** The table above treats "the cache format" as one frozen thing. It is not. The client is a **474 base with 667 (and 647) data injected on top**, through at least four different ad-hoc mechanisms, each relying on an **undocumented ID-avoidance convention** so the injections cannot clobber 474 content:

| Injection | Mechanism | Avoiding-collision convention |
|---|---|---|
| **667 NPCs** — `Nex` (ids **13447+**), `Max` (650), `Callisto`, `Blood reaver` | **Hardcoded `switch` in `EntityDef`** (471 lines, 10 cases) with literal model/anim ids | Uses **high NPC ids** so 474 npc ids are untouched; custom models remapped into the **90xxx** range (`252 → 90252`) |
| **667 curse seq/spotanim + models** | `CurseData667` reads a `CursePack/` folder under the cache (`seq.dat`, `spotanim.dat`, `anims/*.gz`, `models/*.gz`) | Frame file ids remapped into **Class36 slots 28000+** so curse frames cannot overwrite whip/godsword/run frames that share the original 667 file ids |
| **667/647 frame archives** | `Class36.loadFrames()` also **auto-loads any loose numeric `{id}.dat` in the cache root with id ≥ 100** (`load_647`), e.g. Nex's `3502.dat` | Implicit: filename must be purely numeric and ≥100 |
| **667 definition readers** | `SpotAnim.readValues667` coexists with the 474 `readValues`; `CurseData667` hand-replicates 667 opcode skipping (`skipSequence`/`skipSpotAnim`) | Selecting only wanted ids and **skipping the rest by re-implementing the 667 format** |

**Why this materially changes the plan:**
- ⚠️ **The "cache format is frozen" row was incomplete.** There are four data sources with three different collision-avoidance schemes. A refactor that changes `Class36`'s slot allocation, model-loading order, or the `EntityDef` id range can **silently clobber 474 content** — a bug that looks like "some player's godsword animation is wrong now", with no crash and no error.
- ⚠️ **The remapping conventions are load-bearing but nowhere documented in code as a contract.** `ANIM_FILE_BASE = 28000` and the `90xxx` model range are magic numbers with a comment each, not an invariant anything enforces. Phase 8's "remove superseded code" is the obvious place someone deletes one by accident.
- ⚠️ **Phase 6 (animation preservation) is bigger and riskier than written.** It says "474 frames/skins", but the frame space is *shared* between 474 packed data, loose 647 archives, and the 28000+ curse remap. Phase 6.1 must document **all four sources and the slot budget** before touching anything.
- ⚠️ **Phase 3 (cache readers) must preserve the injection paths**, including `CurseData667`'s hand-written 667 opcode skippers, which are frozen *by the same logic as the packet switch* — they encode an external format.
- ✅ **Good news for the user's stated direction:** adding *more* 667 data via these conventions does **not** touch the wire protocol, so it does not threaten server compatibility. The risk is internal (474 content integrity), which is a much easier thing to test with the Phase 0.4 harness.

### The 474/634 chooser is dead UI — worth knowing before it misleads anyone

`Loader` shows "474 characters" / "**634** characters" and passes `"474"` or `"667"` to `client.main`. ⚠️ **Every part of that is inert:**
- The label says **634** but the code passes **667** — they disagree.
- `client.main(args)` forwards to `new Jframe(args)`, and **`Jframe`'s constructor never reads `args`**. The argument is dropped on the floor.

**So both radio buttons launch a byte-identical client, and the revision label is decorative.** Not a bug to fix now (and *not* something a refactor should silently "correct", since changing it changes behaviour), but it must not be mistaken for a real 474/667 switch when reasoning about the data model — the 667 content is always present.

## Reference review (what I actually found, not what the brief assumed)

### `ubjelly/317-OpenGL` — ⚠️ not usable
The entire repository is **three files**: `src/org/derithium/Client.java` (1,647 B), `Game.java` (215 B), `ImageProducer.java` (864 B), plus compiled copies in `bin/`. There is no renderer, no shader, no GL code — it is an abandoned skeleton of a structure (`org.derithium`) with nothing in it. **Nothing can be learned from it beyond "someone once intended a `Game`/`ImageProducer` split".** Do not spend further time on it.

### `Elvarg-Community/Elvarg-Client-Public` — the real reference, but **not portable**
This is a **full RuneLite client** (Gradle Kotlin build; **1,156** Java files: **1,085** under `net/runelite/`, **209** under `com/runescape/`; "Current Data: 206"; README lists Textures, Model Class, HD, GPU, RuneLite as its features). Relevant architecture:

- `net/runelite/client/plugins/gpu/GpuPlugin.java` — RuneLite's official GPU plugin; `Shader.java`, `GpuFloatBuffer.java`, `GpuIntBuffer.java`.
- `net/runelite/client/plugins/hd/…` — the HD plugin: `model/ModelPusher.java`, `model/ModelHasher.java`, `model/ModelCache.java`, `opengl/shader/Shader.java`, `utils/ModelHash.java`.
- `com/runescape/entity/model/` — clean `Model`, `ModelHeader`, `ModelLoader`, `Renderable`.
- `com/runescape/cache/anim/Frame.java`, `FrameBase.java` — the animation transform structures.

⚠️ **It cannot be dropped in or cherry-picked wholesale.** It is a RuneLite client: named packages, a mixin/API layer, a plugin host, and a different cache/data revision. Our client is a **flat default-package 317/474 client of 131 files and 59,694 lines**. The value is as a **technique blueprint** — specifically its *seam*: it renders the 3D scene through GL and then composites the software-drawn UI over the top, rather than trying to GPU everything.

### Verdict on the user's three suggested prompts

- **Prompt 1 (GpuRenderer + LWJGL window over the native frame hook)** — ✅ **the abstract-`GpuRenderer` idea is right, ❌ the "bind an LWJGL display over the client's native frame hook" part is the known dead end.** `Display.setParent` was already attempted in this exact codebase and abandoned for deadlocking Windows (see `LwjglPresent`). Phase 6–7 keep the abstraction but use **LWJGL 3's `AWTGLCanvas`** (a single AWT component, the supported path) or a headless offscreen context — **never `Display.setParent`**.
- **Prompt 2 (Model → vertex arrays / VBO)** — ✅ correct and safe **as an additive phase**. Phase 5 exposes geometry; it must not alter the software rasteriser's output.
- **Prompt 3 (474 animation transforms must survive GPU rendering)** — ✅ correct and important, and the resolution is the one RuneLite actually uses: **apply the per-frame transform on the CPU (as `Model.method443`/`Animable` already do) and upload the transformed vertices**, rather than moving skinning into a shader. Our frames are `Class36` (`Skins.dat`, `Frames.dat`, loose frame files, plus `load_647`) and skins are `Class18`. See Phase 6.

### The LWJGL lesson, recorded once so it is not relearned
- `deps/lwjgl.jar` is **LWJGL 2.x** (`org/lwjgl/WindowsSysImplementation`, `LWJGLUtil`, `BufferChecks`) and is **imported by zero files**.
- ⚠️ **`Display.setParent` + AWT = deadlock on Windows.** Both toolkits pump the same HWND. This is why `LwjglPresent` is a stub.
- **The recommendation for Phase 7 is LWJGL 3** (`org.lwjgl.opengl.awt.AWTGLCanvas`), which supports Java 8+ and has a supported AWT embedding, and is a different design from LWJGL 2 rather than an upgrade of it.

---

## Architecture as it stands (the baseline this plan must not break)

- **Entry:** `Loader` (Swing chooser: *474 characters* vs *634 characters*) → `client.main(new String[]{"474"})`.
- **Shell:** `RSApplet extends Applet implements Runnable` + `RSFrame`; full window/listener/input plumbing.
- **Game loop:** `RSApplet.run()` — the classic 317 loop (`delayTime = 20` → 50 logic ticks/s via `processGameLoop`, `minDelay = 1`), **plus an `fpsUnlocked` uncapped branch** (`while (now - lastLogicTime >= 20 && ticks < 8)`) that draws every frame. Rendering is `processDrawing()`.
- **Renderer:** pure **software** — `RSImageProducer` wraps `int[]` pixels; `DrawingArea` does primitives; `Texture.java` (2,016 lines) does software texture mapping; **`Model.java` (2,823 lines) is the software rasteriser** (`method443` applies transform, `method478` scale, `method476` recolour, `method479` lighting).
- **Present:** `Graphics.drawImage` of the framebuffer, optionally through Java2D's GL pipeline (`GlPresent`).
- **Scene:** `WorldController` (2,548), `ObjectManager`, `Ground`.
- **Big files:** `client.java` **20,129** lines; `Model` 2,823; `WorldController` 2,548; `Texture` 2,016; `RSInterface` 1,722; `Sprite` 1,111; `OnDemandFetcher` 629; `Class11` 571.
- **Total:** 131 files, **59,694** lines, flat default package, no tests, no build system.

The backup includes `src`, `bin`, `deps`, `Sprites`, `tools` and the root `*.bat`/`.classpath`/`.project`. ⚠️ Note the disk has only ~7.2 GB free, so `Proxy Client/` at 6.1 MB is cheap to back up but the repo as a whole is not — future backups should stay scoped to `Proxy Client/`. ⚠️ **Re-verified after the fact:** all **423/423** backed-up files hash-match their live counterparts (0 missing, 0 different), and the live `Proxy Client/` differs from the backup by exactly **8,871 bytes** — the two files this session added — so nothing pre-existing was altered. ⚠️ **The backup was initially created as untracked (`??`), which meant `git clean -fd` — the very command this plan warns about — would have deleted it; a `git clean -fdn` dry run made inside the client directory confirmed it. Fixed in commit `ad8babe3`** by dropping a `_client-backup-*/` rule into a new workspace-root `.gitignore`, since `git clean` spares ignored files unless `-x` is passed. ⚠️ **That same dry run also listed the new `RunTests.bat` and `test/` as removable** — the 0.6 untracked-files hazard recurring, this time caught before the loss rather than after; both are committed in `ad8babe3`.

Verified before committing that the claim held (`client.java` unmodified + references `LwjglPresent`/`OverlayRefresh`), that all seven exist on disk, and that **none were gitignored** (so no `-f` was hiding the problem). After the commit: all seven are in `HEAD`, **no untracked `.java` remains under `Proxy Client/src`**, and `git show --stat` confirms the commit added files only — **it modified no source and deleted nothing**, so the pre-existing uncommitted client work (46 modified files) is untouched. ⚠️ **Deliberately not included:** the two `*.bak-pre-orig` files (non-`.java`, harmless to `javac`, and Phase 8's business) and the 46 modified files, which are a separate decision. ⚠️ **Also verified in passing: the server's `HEAD` was never broken** — every file referencing its untracked `ConnectionPool`/`MusicState` is itself modified, so only the client had this fault.
- [x] **0.2 ✅ DONE (2026-10-04).** Wrote two hash manifests into the backup directory: `BIN-BASELINE.sha256` (**213** compiled classes) and `SRC-BASELINE.sha256` (**131** source files), each `SHA256<two spaces>relative-path`, sorted, so a later phase can be diffed against it with one command. ⚠️ Stored **inside the backup**, not in the repo, to avoid adding a generated artifact that would drift — the regeneration command is `Get-ChildItem bin -Filter *.class -Recurse | Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256`. ⚠️ `bin/` is git-tracked, so git partly covers this already; the manifest exists because `Compile.bat`'s `robocopy` **does not delete removed classes and is not atomic**, so a stale or half-copied `.class` is a real failure mode that git alone would not flag during a build.
- ✅ **0.3 DONE (2026-10-04) — the user captured a real session, and it validates the tap.** Ran `Record.bat`, logged in, played, walked the requested route including the Nex/667 content and the Rock Crab area. **Result: 9,470 protocol units — 365 sent (W) / 9,105 received (R), largest 171 bytes** — stored as the golden master at `Proxy Client/golden-master/session-20261004.log` (135 KB, committed: it is the oracle, so it must be versioned).
  - ⚠️ **The capture decodes exactly against the source, which is why I trust it.** The first 12 units are the login handshake as `client.java:10409-10461` actually performs it: a 2-byte packet (`0e00`), then **9** single-byte reads (the code does 8 discarded `read()`s then 1 more for the response code), then an 8-byte seed flush (`015c9e3e02589cbe`), then the **171-byte** login block whose first bytes are opcode `0x10`, length `0xa9`, `0xff`, and `0x013d` = **317**. Every one of those matches the code path, so the tap is proven to sit in the right place rather than assumed to.
  - ⚠️ **No credentials in the file:** the username and password are written into the login buffer and RSA-encrypted in place by `stream.doKeys()` before being sent, so the committed capture contains ciphertext. (The 5-bit name-hash byte is in the clear; harmless.)
  - ⚠️ **A bug was found by the capture's very filename.** The file was `packet-tap-.log` — empty timestamp — because `set OUT=packet-tap-%STAMP%.log` sat inside a parenthesised block, so `%STAMP%` was expanded when the block was *parsed*, before `set STAMP` ran. Fixed by branching with `goto` instead of a block (which also avoids `setlocal enabledelayedexpansion`, whose `!` handling would be a hazard in a path). Verified: no-argument now resolves to `packet-tap-20261004-153650.log`.
  - ⚠️ **The screen recording half is still the user's, and it is not optional** — see the limitation below. The packet half is now built and self-tested, so you don't need to work out how to record packets:
  - **`Proxy Client/PacketTap.java`** — a passive tap on `RSSocket`, the single choke point all game traffic passes through. ⚠️ **It taps `read()`, `flushInputStream()` and `queueBytes()` rather than the raw socket, and that choice is the whole point:** TCP chunk boundaries are *not* reproducible (the same session produces different `write()` sizes on different runs), so a log of raw chunks cannot be diffed. The client, however, consumes the stream one protocol unit at a time, so tapping those three methods records the protocol as the client understands it — which *is* reproducible.
  - **`Proxy Client/Record.bat`** — launches the client exactly as `Run.bat` does, with the tap switched on. ⚠️ `Run.bat` is untouched.
  - ⚠️ **Off unless asked for.** The tap enables only via `-Dsoultrail.packettap=<file>`. With it unset, `enabled()` is false and every call returns immediately. **Verified, not assumed:** running against `bin/` with no property gives `enabled() = false` and creates no file, so normal play is unchanged.
  - ⚠️ **The tap is self-tested** (6 new harness assertions, now **20/20**): log format, two-digit hex padding, and a check that **every data line matches `<dir> <len> <hex>` with no timestamps** — because a log containing a counter or a clock cannot be diffed, which would make the whole oracle worthless. ⚠️ That test exists because this failure is silent and expensive: a tap that quietly writes nothing costs you the only chance to record that session against that server build.
  - ⚠️ Flushed on every record, deliberately: the capture most likely to matter is the one taken during a crash, and a buffer that dies with the process would lose exactly the packets under investigation.
  - ⚠️⚠️ **CORRECTION, forced by the real capture — my original claim here was WRONG.** I wrote that the log was diffable. **A byte-for-byte comparison of two sessions is impossible on this protocol**, and it has nothing to do with the tap: after login every packet **opcode is ISAAC-encrypted** with keys the client seeds from **`Math.random()`** (`client.java:10426-10427`) plus the server's 8 random bytes (`aLong1215`), and the login block itself embeds those seeds. Two runs share neither opcodes nor handshake bytes. The tap design is still right and the no-timestamps rule is still necessary, but what is reproducible is the **structure — direction + length — not the bytes**, so 0.5 compares a structural signature. ⚠️ **This is worth leaving visible:** the mistake was stating a verification property without testing it against a real capture, and it would have produced an oracle that fails on every run for reasons that look like a protocol bug.
  - ⚠️ **What the packet oracle can and cannot catch:** it catches **desync** (a read in the wrong order consumes a different number of bytes, which changes the length/order sequence), reordering, and dropped or duplicated packets. It does **not** compare payload contents — a packet whose payload changed while its length stayed the same is invisible. ⚠️ **That is exactly why the screen recording is not optional:** only it can show that the 474 assets still render correctly. The two halves cover different risks and neither replaces the other.
  - ⚠️ **The 667 content is confirmed working right now (user, 2026-10-04)**, so that is the baseline the recording captures and every later phase must preserve. ⚠️ **Recording it is the more valuable half**, because no unit test covers the 474-integrity risk the injection layers introduce — `PacketTap` cannot see a wrong animation.
  - ⚠️ **Not covered by this tap:** `OnDemandFetcher`'s separate socket (cache/model fetching, also in the contract table). Worth adding if a later phase touches fetching; the four named actions don't exercise it.
  - ⚠️ **The gate is lifted:** `Record.bat` has now been run against the unmodified server and produced a valid capture, so the phases that move packet code have an oracle to prove they did not desync.
- [x] **0.4 ✅ DONE (2026-10-04, commit `ad8babe3`) — headless testability is proven, and it is the plan's biggest risk retired.** Added `Proxy Client/test/ClientHarness.java` (default package, dependency-free, exits non-zero on failure) plus `Proxy Client/RunTests.bat`, and they run **14/14 green against the compiled client in `bin/` with no display, no server and no game loop**. The harness covers `Stream` — the byte reader/writer every packet and every cache read passes through, and therefore the one class where a silent bug corrupts everything downstream — asserting round-trips **and byte order** (big-endian on the wire, verified against the raw buffer, because a round-trip alone would pass even if both halves were little-endian) plus sign-extension, 3-byte reads, the `g2`/`g4` aliases and bit access. ⚠️ **The harness was mutation-tested to prove its exit code actually gates:** corrupting a single expectation to `0x1235` gave `passed: 13 failed: 1`, `RESULT: FAIL` and **exit code 1** — without that check, "the tests pass" would be an unverified claim, which is the exact failure mode this item exists to prevent. ⚠️ **Deliberately not JUnit:** the client has no dependency resolution at all, so a dependency-free `main` gates a phase immediately, and Phase 1 folds it into the Gradle build as `gradlew harness` without dragging in a test framework. ⚠️ **`RunTests.bat` deliberately does not call `Compile.bat`** — that script's `:killclient` runs `taskkill /F` on the game window and would close the player's client as a side effect of running tests. ⚠️ **One finding came straight out of this, now recorded under "Known broken":** `Stream.readUnsignedWord()` **silently returns a hardcoded 1795** on an out-of-range read instead of throwing — so the harness asserts today's behaviour as a tripwire for Phase 2 rather than the behaviour it *should* have, because a test asserting the fix would fail today and get deleted. ⚠️ **One correction, caught in Phase 1: my original note here said client sources "must stay ASCII" — that was wrong and is worth leaving visible.** Thirteen of the 131 sources *already contain* non-ASCII, and they compile today **only because the build happens to use Cp1252**. The real rule is that the build's encoding is load-bearing. Worse, it turned out to be a live bug, not just a constraint: four of those files carry a UTF-8 em dash inside a **string literal**, and reading UTF-8 bytes as Cp1252 compiled them into three-character mojibake that players see. Phase 1 fixes it. ⚠️ **The lesson is that "the harness failed to compile because of a warning symbol" was a symptom I read too narrowly** — it pointed at an encoding hazard that affected shipped text, and the correct response was to investigate the existing sources, not just to strip the character from my own file.
- [x] **0.5 ✅ DONE (2026-10-04) — the compatibility check is real, re-runnable, and proven to fail when it should.** Added `Proxy Client/tools/compare-capture.ps1` and the `gradlew compareCapture` task (not in `check` — it needs a capture from a human session, so it cannot run unattended). The golden master is `Proxy Client/golden-master/session-20261004.log`.
  - **How it works:** each line reduces to `<dir> <len>`, so the same parser reads a raw log or a pre-reduced signature and no conversion step is needed. It reports the unit count, the total divergence count, the first divergence with context, and every divergence position — because one late divergence is usually innocent (a wandering NPC) while many starting early is a protocol change, and a single number cannot tell those apart.
  - ⚠️ **Mutation-tested, not assumed** — the same standard as 0.4, and it matters more here because a comparator that always says "identical" is worse than none:
    - self-comparison of the capture → `9470 units, 0 divergences, IDENTICAL`, exit **0**;
    - **3 units deleted** mid-capture → `9467 units, 3093 divergences`, first at unit 4737, exit **1**;
    - **a single unit resized** `R 6`→`R 7` with the count unchanged → `1 divergence`, exit **1**.
    - The third case is the important one: it proves the check is sensitive to a **length** change alone, which is precisely the signature of a desync, rather than only to gross truncation.
  - ⚠️ **A fresh capture must be taken after each phase that touches packet code**, and it only means anything for the **same route and roughly the same world state** — server tick timing and NPC positions legitimately shift some units, so a clean run is evidence of no desync, and a dirty run is a signal to look, not automatic proof of a bug.
  - ⚠️⚠️ **REPAIRED AGAIN (2026-10-04 late night) — the "deterministic 600-unit prefix" gate was FALSE, and it was replaced by a bounded ALIGNMENT.** The gate required unit-for-unit agreement across the login/load region, and that region is not deterministic: it carries a **length-prefixed loading-progress String** whose byte length depends on its own value (`"98%\n"` = 6 bytes, `"100%\n"` = 7, so BOTH the `R 2` length prefix and the `R 6`/`R 7` payload change) and whose frequency varies per session (3, 7 and 31 measured), plus **ordinary inserted client actions** (an extra `W 1` at unit 520). ⚠️ **The proof it was the gate and not the client: captures from the SAME BINARY, compared against each other, scored DRIFT** — `221412` vs `221811` and `221551` vs `221811` both collapsed to a **17-unit** prefix while scoring **98.1% and 96.8%**, *higher* than the pair that passed (`221412` vs `221551`, 1,100-unit prefix, 97.5%). A client cannot drift from itself, and the old message ("even the LOGIN HANDSHAKE differs") was exactly that false alarm. ⚠️ **The replacement asks a different question: can the golden's first 600 units be matched, in order, into the current's first 664?** (window 600, slack 64, tolerance 10 — tuned on measurements, not guessed). ⚠️ **Re-verified end to end, and `gradlew compareCapture` now succeeds where it previously failed: all SEVEN healthy comparisons are COMPATIBLE — golden vs the three post-rename captures at 594/594/593, golden vs the pre-rename reference at 600, and the three same-binary pairs at 600/599/599; identical inputs are still refused (exit 2); and every cascade is still DRIFT — breaks at unit 18/300/500/560 score 351/470/568/589 against the 590 required.** ⚠️ **The tolerance is 10 and not tighter because there are two healthy baselines, not one: same-binary pairs score 599-600, but golden-vs-fresh tops out at 593-594, the ~6-unit cost being benign reordering of the same variable-length frames (golden carries `R 2 ffff` + `R 6 00566401c763` early where `221412` carries them later; both sessions hold ~163 of that frame class).** ⚠️ **The positional prefix is still printed but is now labelled diagnostic-only, since healthy pairs score ~17 there.** ⚠️ **Honest cost of the change, recorded rather than glossed: a 3-unit deletion confined to the prefix now PASSES (it is indistinguishable from benign variance at this granularity), and a cascade beginning in the LAST ~35 units of the window also passes.** Both weaken old mutation cases on purpose — a real misread length **cascades**, and every cascade up to unit 560 still fails, so the gate catches the realistic fault while no longer failing correct code. ⚠️ **The detection boundary was measured, not assumed: cascades at unit 440/480/500/520/540/560/580 score 536/559/568/573/580/589/597, and the healthy floor is 593 — so a break past roughly unit 565 is inside the noise.** ⚠️ **Deliberately NOT done: canonicalising the progress frame to a single token.** It was measured to lift the failing pairs only from 17 to 518 (still under the gate, because the unit-520 insertion is a separate cause), so the alignment is the load-bearing fix; adding a token-collapse rule would mask genuine length-field changes for no measured gain.
  - ⚠️ **Also corrected in the tool's own header:** it used to state that the prefix "matched to the unit across two sessions 6h apart" and call that region deterministic. That measurement held for one pair by luck (both happened to record a two-digit percentage) and the claim is now replaced with the measured per-session variance.

### Phase 1 — Build system and Java version
- ✅ **1.1 DONE (2026-10-04, commit `7c8f1987`) — verified live by the user against the server.** `Proxy Client/build.gradle` + `settings.gradle` + the server's Gradle **9.8.0** wrapper (already cached in `~/.gradle`, so no download; confirmed running on Java 25). Tasks: `compileJava`, `installBin` (copies into `bin/` — the directory `Run.bat` launches from — overwriting but **never purging**, faithful to the old `robocopy`), `compileHarness`/`harness`, `reportStaleClasses`, `generateObjectCollisionSizes`, `verifyObjectCollisionSizes`, `run`. **`Run.bat` is untouched** and stays the normal way to play. ⚠️ **`Compile.bat` is kept as a working fallback** with a deprecation header rather than deleted — removing the known-good build before a live session confirms the replacement is the wrong order. ⚠️ **This was correctly left unticked until it could be settled properly:** `installBin` had already replaced `bin/` with Gradle-built Java 8 classes, so *what the player launches had changed*, and the rule above requires a live client against the real server. **The user ran it and reported "all works fine" on 2026-10-04**, so this is now ticked on evidence rather than on a compile. ⚠️ **Expect every one of the 206 rebuilt classes to differ in size and hash — that is not drift.** Two intentional causes: the class-file version moved 51→52, and Gradle's `JavaCompile` turns on **full debug info** by default (`-g`, including local variable tables) whereas `Compile.bat` passed no `-g` and got only source+lines. That is why some classes grew ~40% (e.g. `rsDrawingArea.class` 7,457 → 10,703 bytes). Debug attributes cannot affect behaviour — confirmed by all 213 classes loading — and the trade buys variable names in a debugger, which is worth having for the phases ahead. The **7 byte-identical classes are the stale orphans** below, which the build deliberately does not touch.
- ✅ **1.2 DONE (2026-10-04) with one deliberate deviation from this plan — see below.** ⚠️ `-source 1.7` is gone and replaced by **`options.release = 8`**, so the build no longer depends on a hardcoded JDK 8 path. **The deviation: no `toolchain` block.** This plan called for pinning a toolchain, but a toolchain is the wrong tool here: the client's correct target is Java 8, and `javac 25` already emits that via `--release 8`. ⚠️ **To be accurate about availability rather than convenient:** a toolchain was *possible* — Gradle already has a JDK 21 cached under `~/.gradle/jdks`, provisioned by the server's own build — but it would add a second moving part that changes *which compiler runs* without changing the output, and it risks a future edit silently compiling at a different level. With only `options.release`, the pin lives in one place. The target is now a single override — `-PclientJavaRelease=17` — and `foojay-resolver` is deliberately absent from `settings.gradle` so nothing is ever fetched. ⚠️ **`--release 8` was checked for a specific hazard before use:** unlike `-source/-target`, `--release` narrows the *API* to the documented Java 8 surface, so it would break any use of internal JDK APIs such as `sun.*`. The client imports none — the only `sun.*` occurrences are system-property string literals in `GlPresent.java` — so the narrower surface is safe. Verified by compiling. ⚠️ **Result: 0 errors, and the class-file major version moved 51 (Java 7) to 52 (Java 8) on all 206 freshly built classes.**
  - **Recommended ladder:** first land **Java 8 target** (`--release 8`) as the minimal safe step and verify the golden master; then move to **Java 17 LTS** once Phase 0.4's tests exist and pass. Java 17 is the sweet spot for LWJGL 3; Java 25 is available here but is unnecessarily bleeding-edge for a game client.
  - ⚠️ **The runtime is already Java 25** (bare `java` in `Run.bat`), so a higher target is *less* risky than it sounds — but the *compile* target change is what can surface latent warnings, so it is verified against the golden master.
- ✅ **1.3 DONE (2026-10-04) — baseline recorded.** `-Xlint:all,-options` is on every `JavaCompile`, so warnings are surfaced rather than hidden. **Baseline: 0 errors, 100 warnings** — 77 `[serial]`, 9 `[rawtypes]`, 5 `[unchecked]`, 5 `[deprecation]`, 2 `[static]`, 1 `[empty]`, plus javac's summary "additionally use unchecked or unsafe operations" note. ⚠️ **The count was reproduced independently with a direct `javac` run using the identical flags (also exactly 100)** — worth stating because an earlier run reported 97 and the discrepancy was my own regex-based counting, not the build; later phases need a number they can trust. ⚠️ **None fixed, as this item requires** — the point is a baseline that later phases must not exceed. `-options` is excluded deliberately: javac 25 merely notes that Java 8 is obsolete, which is not a code warning.
- ✅ **1.4 DONE (2026-10-04) — and it is a real check, not just a documented step.** The generator is wired in as `gradlew generateObjectCollisionSizes`, and drift is now *detectable* rather than merely documented: the generated file embeds `SOURCE_CFG_SHA256`, the SHA-256 of the `objectSize.cfg` it came from, and `verifyObjectCollisionSizes` recomputes it and fails on a mismatch. It runs as part of `gradlew check`. ⚠️ **This needed a change to the generated file and the generator**, which was done and verified surgically: regenerating changed **exactly 8 lines** (the new javadoc plus the constant) and left the **28 KB `PACKED` collision table byte-identical**, so collision behaviour is untouched — confirmed by string-comparing the table before and after and by recompiling. ⚠️ **The check was mutation-tested to prove it has teeth:** corrupting the embedded hash to all-zeros made the task FAIL with both hashes printed and the fix command named, and restoring it made the task pass again. ⚠️ **Deliberately implemented with no PowerShell and no generator re-run inside the check** — the first attempt shelled out and re-generated a tracked source file as a side effect of verifying it, which is the wrong trade; hashing needs neither, and works offline. ⚠️ **Related:** the generator writes the file as **UTF-8**, which the old build read as Cp1252 — a latent generator/compiler mismatch that Phase 1.2's encoding change also removes.

### Phase 2 — Defensive data layer (crash hardening) ← the user's already-agreed fix
Additive guards only. ⚠️ **Change *what happens on invalid data*, never *what is read or in what order*** (frozen cache contract).
- ✅ **2.1 DONE (2026-10-04, live-verified).** The crash the user hit both guards are in and pinned by the harness, but per the client rule this is **not ticked until it has been played against the unmodified server.**
  - **`forID(int i)`** now returns a shared defaulted stand-in for any id outside the cache: `if (cache == null || streamIndices == null || i < 0 || i >= streamIndices.length) return invalid();`. This is the whole crash — `certID` stays `-1` when opcode 97 is absent, and `toNote()` then called `forID(-1)`, which indexed `streamIndices[-1]`. ⚠️ **Guarding `forID` rather than only `toNote` fixes the class of bug, not the instance:** `forID` is public and called from many places, so every caller is now protected.
  - **`toNote()`** also bails out when either cert id is missing or out of range, and when the resolved name is null/empty (`charAt(0)` below would throw). ⚠️ **Both lookups now happen before any assignment** so a rejected definition is not left half-rewritten into a broken note — and the **order of the two `forID` calls is unchanged**, which matters because `forID` rotates the shared ten-entry cache.
  - ⚠️ **The stand-in is shared on purpose.** `forID` already returns objects from a ten-entry cache that are reused and mutated, so callers cannot depend on a definition's identity anyway; sharing also avoids allocating per frame if a bad id is looked up in a render loop. `setDefaults()` leaves `certTemplateID` at `-1`, so the stand-in cannot recurse back into `toNote()`.
  - ⚠️ **The harness test was mutation-tested, and building it exposed a trap worth recording.** It loads a **synthetic but genuinely valid cache** (`obj.dat` + `obj.idx` in a hand-built gzip `StreamLoader`) so that `streamIndices` is really populated. **Testing the `cache == null` path would have been worthless:** that path short-circuits *before* the index check, so a regression dropping only the bounds test would still have passed. Removing `i < 0 || i >= streamIndices.length` from the guard made all four ItemDef assertions FAIL with `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` and exit code 1 — proving the test pins the reported crash rather than the null case. Harness then went 20 → **24/24** (and stands at **29/29** after 2.2).
  - ⚠️ **`ItemDef_2.Items(i)` calls `forID(i)` recursively** — it terminates only because `itemDef.id = i` is set *before* that call. Worth knowing before touching that ordering.
  - ⚠️ **Found and deliberately NOT fixed here (belongs in 2.2):** `streamIndices[i]` is `0` for any id inside the array's range that the cache never defined (`unpackConfig` allocates `totalItems + 50000` slots but only fills `totalItems - 21`), so such an id parses whatever sits at `obj.dat` offset 0 instead of being rejected. Unlike the `-1` case this is **not** the reported crash, and treating offset 0 as invalid is a behaviour change that needs verifying against the real cache first — so it is recorded, not slipped in.
- [x] **2.2 Auditing the other definition readers for the same shape** — `ObjectDef`, `EntityDef`, `Flo`, `IDK`, `VarBit`, `Varp`, `Animation`/`SpotAnim`: any place a cache-derived index or id is used to index an array or a `Stream` without a range check. Fix the *class* of bug, not just the one instance. ✅ **Every reader this phase names is now audited, all REAL instances are fixed and mutation-pinned, and the live session confirmed no regression** (the `ObjectManager` rendering change is inert on valid data, and the capture's 701-unit deterministic prefix — the region that reader runs during — is intact).
  - 🟡 **`EntityDef` and `ObjectDef` DONE (2026-10-04) — both were ALREADY guarded, so no production code changed. Harness 24 → 29/29.** This step is the reason the audit was worth doing anyway: neither class needed a fix, but neither had a single test either, and an unguarded guard is one refactor away from being deleted with nothing to catch it.
    - **`EntityDef.forID`** opens with `if (i < 0 || streamIndices == null || i >= streamIndices.length) readId = 0;`, then reads `streamIndices[readId]`. Pinned by `entityDefOutOfRangeIdIsSafe()`, which (like the 2.1 test) loads a **real synthetic cache** — a hand-built gzip `StreamLoader` with valid `npc.dat`/`npc.idx` — because testing the `streamIndices == null` path would short-circuit *before* the index check and pass with the bounds test deleted.
    - **`ObjectDef.forID`** clamps `if (i < 0) i = 0` (`:21-22`) before indexing `streamIndices474`, delegates oversize ids to `forID525`, and `forID525` clamps again (`:149`) against `streamIndices525`. Pinned by `objectDefOutOfRangeIdIsSafe()` with a **synthetic `loc.dat`/`loc.idx` + `525loc.dat`/`525loc.idx`** archive so both the 474 and the delegated 525 path are genuinely exercised.
    - ⚠️ **The finding worth keeping: my first test of the `ObjectDef` clamp was VACUOUS and the mutation test caught it.** It used `forID(-1)`, which **never reaches the clamp** — `ObjectDef`'s constructor defaults `type` to `-1`, so the `cache[j].type == i` scan at the top of `forID` matches a fresh cache entry and returns immediately. Deleting `if (i < 0) i = 0` left that test **passing**. I confirmed the mechanism by probing `new ObjectDef().type` (prints `-1`) rather than reasoning about it, then changed the test to `forID(-2)` (which misses the scan) and to assert **identity**: the clamped call writes `type = 0` (`:26`), so a following `forID(0)` must return the *same object*. That is what distinguishes "the clamp ran" from "nothing threw".
    - ⚠️ **Both classes were then mutation-tested properly.** Dropping `i < 0 || i >= streamIndices.length` from `EntityDef` fails its 2 assertions; dropping either `ObjectDef` clamp fails 3 assertions — every case with `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` and **exit 1**. Both source files were restored and verified byte-identical to `HEAD` afterwards (`git status` clean for them), so only `ClientHarness.java` carries a diff.
    - ⚠️ **Side finding, recorded not fixed:** for the same reason, **`ObjectDef.forID(-1)` silently returns an uninitialized cache entry** (fields left at Java defaults, `type = -1`) instead of crashing or being rejected. It is benign in that it cannot throw, but it is the same "returns a bogus definition" family as the deferred `streamIndices[i] == 0` item below, and treating it as invalid would be a behaviour change needing a real-cache check.
  - ✅ **`VarBit` and `Varp` — two REAL bugs of this shape, found and FIXED failing-test-first (2026-10-04). Harness 29 → 31/31.**
    - **`VarBit.unpackConfig`**: `Varp.cache[cache[j].anInt648].aBoolean713 = true;` — `anInt648` is the target **varp** id read out of `varbit.dat`, and `Varp.cache` is sized from `varp.dat`'s entry count. The two files can simply disagree and nothing checked, so a mismatched or corrupt cache threw `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` **during cache load, at startup, before any packet arrived.** ⚠️ **Worse than the ItemDef crash in that respect** — that one needed a live packet. The guard now also tolerates `Varp.cache == null` and a null target slot.
    - **`Varp.readValues`**: `anIntArray703[anInt702++] = i;` — the array is sized from `varp.dat`'s entry count, but the write is driven by a *counter over opcode-3 occurrences*, so one definition carrying opcode 3 twice ran off the end. Now bounds-checked; `anInt702` is simply not advanced once the table is full.
    - ⚠️ **Both were mutation-tested after fixing:** reverting either guard to its original form makes **its own** test fail again with `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` and exit 1 — so neither test is vacuous, which is the mistake the first `ObjectDef` test made. ⚠️ **And both crashes were observed BEFORE the fix**, so they are proven rather than hypothesised.
    - ⚠️ **The offset-table scan both made this tractable and corrected this plan's premise:** only `ItemDef`, `EntityDef` and `ObjectDef` use `streamIndices`/`currentOffset` offset tables, so those three are the *complete* set for that pattern. The `VarBit`/`Varp` bugs were found by searching for the same **shape** — a cache-derived value indexing an array — rather than the same syntax.
    - ⚠️ **Harness gotcha for anyone extending these tests:** `syntheticArchive` requires gzip to actually *shrink* the payload, because the loader sizes its buffer to the **uncompressed** length; a 5-byte definition therefore throws `IllegalStateException` *before* the code under test runs. Hence the `padded(...)` helper — the readers stop at their count/terminator, so the zero tail is never parsed and only serves to make the file big enough to compress.
  - ⚠️ **Found and deliberately NOT touched:** `CurseData667` indexes `Animation.anims[animId]`, where `animId` is taken from `spotanim.dat`'s `anInt406` — it validates the `id` it starts from but not the anim id derived from it. **Same shape, but it is 667-injection code, so it belongs to the Phase 6.5 inventory** rather than to a 474 hardening pass.
  - ✅ **`IDK` — the plainest instance of the shape, unguarded three times. FIXED failing-test-first (2026-10-04). Harness 31 → 33/33.**
    - `readValues` writes `anIntArray659[i - 40]` for `40 <= i < 50`, `anIntArray660[i - 50]` for `50 <= i < 60`, and `anIntArray661[i - 60]` for `60 <= i < 70` — but the arrays are `int[6]`, `int[6]` and `int[5]`. Each range addresses ten slots of a six/six/five-slot array, so **opcodes 46-49, 56-59 and 65-69 walked off the end, during cache load**. Valid 317 data only ever uses 40-45 / 50-55 / 60-64, so the guard is inert on a well-formed cache.
    - ⚠️ **The important detail is the fix, not the bug: the word must still be READ when the store is skipped.** Folding the read into the guard is the natural "simplification", and it would leave the stream two bytes ahead so every later definition in `idk.dat` would parse from the wrong offset — **silent** corruption, where the original failure was at least loud. So each block reads unconditionally and only the store is bounds-checked.
    - ⚠️ **Two tests, both mutation-proven:** the crash test (revert the guard → `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` and exit 1), and a deliberate **read-order pin** that puts an opcode-2 model list *after* the skipped store and asserts it still parses (move the read inside the guard → this test fails **while the crash test still passes**, which is the discrimination that makes it worth keeping). It reads the private `anIntArray658` by reflection because `IDK` exposes no getter — a Phase 3 field rename will break it, which is acceptable as long as it is renamed rather than deleted.
  - ✅ **`Flo`, `Animation` and `SpotAnim` audited (2026-10-04) — already safe, no change needed.** `Flo.readValues` uses only scalars (no indexing at all); `Animation.method258` guards `anIntArray355` (`i < 0 || i >= anIntArray355.length`) before indexing it *and* the parallel `anIntArray353`; `SpotAnim` guards both of its `Animation.anims` lookups and grows its opcode-40 arrays. ⚠️ **`Class36.method531` and `client.java`'s `safeAnimation`/`safeGfxAnimation` helpers are guarded as well** — an earlier hardening pass covered more than this plan credited, and the audit's value here is establishing that rather than assuming it.
  - ✅ **`ObjectManager` — the five `Flo.cache[id - 1]` sites are FIXED, failing-test-first and mutation-pinned (2026-10-04). Harness 33 → 34/34.** `method171` resolves each tile's floor straight from the landscape byte (`aByteArrayArrayArray142` / `...130 & 0xff`) with only an `id > 0` test and **no upper bound**, at `:113`, `:127`, `:189`, `:222` and `:243` (pre-fix numbering). ⚠️ **`:242` *looked* guarded but was the bug itself:** it compared the already-decremented `i19 - 1` against a length that is an exclusive bound, so it missed by one and let `Flo.cache[length]` through.
    - ⚠️ **The plan's own earlier claim was WRONG and is corrected here: this does not throw during region load — it fails SILENTLY.** `method171`'s entire body (36-450) sits inside `try { ... } catch(Exception e) { }` **with an empty handler**, so an out-of-range id surfaces nowhere: it abandons the rest of the region's blending and lighting and writes nothing — no crash, no message, no log. That is why the test could not assert "does not throw" (that would be vacuous, the mistake the first `ObjectDef` test made) and had to assert on a **later side effect of the same pass** instead.
    - ⚠️ **The fix is one helper, `boundedFloorId(int)`, called where each id is read** — it clamps to `Flo.cache.length`, which is what `:242` meant to do, so every downstream use of that id (including the two `:242` never covered) is bounded at once. ⚠️ **The ids here are 1-based** (0 means "no floor"), which is the whole off-by-one: the correct comparison is `id > length`, not `id - 1 > length`. ⚠️ It is only ever called for an id that already tested above zero, so the callers touch `Flo.cache` exactly as often as they did before and a missing `flo.dat` behaves as it always did. The broken clamp at `:242` is **removed** rather than left behind, because a wrong guard that no longer runs is a trap for the next reader.
    - ⚠️ **The scene scaffolding the earlier note called "a step in itself" is built, and the trick is worth keeping:** a **55-entry `Flo.cache`** so id **56** is exactly one past the end, plus three tiles in region level 1 — a bad overlay id at `[1][5][5]` (read by the smoothing pass at `:111`/`:124` **and** by the tile pass at `:189`), a bad underlay id at `[1][6][5]` (`:222`/`:243`), and a **valid witness** at `[1][12][5]` whose flat-tile shading bit (`0x924`, set at `:225`) is the assertion. ⚠️ **`[1][12][5]` is chosen deliberately:** `client.tileBlending` defaults to **true**, so `blendR = 5` and the smoothing pass re-reads row 5 at `l6 = 10`; a witness beyond that row is reached only if **all five** sites are bounded, which is why a single assertion catches a guard missing at any of them. The witness tile takes the untextured branch and the underlay tile resolves to `Flo.cache.length`, so `:244` skips the texture/render block for `i19 - 1 == 54` — the fixture needs no textures at all.
    - ⚠️ **Failing-first, then fully mutation-tested:** before the fix the test FAILED with `witness shading 0x0`; after it, `0x924`. Removing **each of the four call sites individually** fails it, neutering the helper's clamp fails it, and **clamping one too late (`id > length + 1` — precisely the original off-by-one) fails it too**, so the boundary is genuinely exercised rather than merely crossed. ⚠️ `ObjectManager`'s constructor allocates the landscape arrays itself, so the test fetches `aByteArrayArrayArray142`/`...130` by reflection and fills them in place, and reads `anIntArrayArrayArray135` back the same way.
  - ⚠️ **Found and deliberately NOT touched: `method171` swallows every exception with an empty `catch`, and the pattern repeats in `ObjectManager` around `:1015`.** Removing it would turn today's silent partial aborts into crashes — a behaviour change, and for a player mid-region-load arguably a worse one — so it is recorded rather than changed. ⚠️ **It is also the reason the five sites above went unnoticed for so long: the guard was missing and the failure was invisible, so nothing ever pointed at the line.** The right fix is probably to log once rather than rethrow, which is a policy decision for a later phase.
  - ⚠️ **The deferred `streamIndices[i] == 0` case is RE-SCOPED (2026-10-04): its stated precondition cannot be met.** The plan said to verify against the real cache before treating offset 0 as invalid. ⚠️ **Two things found while trying to do exactly that, and the first is the one that matters:** (a) **the cache is *packed*** — `C:\Users\llrbi\Biohazard.474` holds `main_file_cache.dat` (70 MB) plus `main_file_cache.idx0..4`, with **no loose `obj.dat`/`obj.idx`**, and `ItemDef` is fed through `client.streamLoaderForName` → `decompressors[0].decompress(i)` (`client.java:7011-7026`, `:11565`). So "a look at the real cache" is itself a cache-decoding task, not a `Read` away; (b) ⚠️ **more decisively, the cache cannot answer the actual question anyway.** What matters is not how big `idxCount` is but whether anything ever calls `forID` with an id the cache does not define — and the only thing that sends item ids is **the server, which is out of scope**. ⚠️ **What *is* established, and it is worth keeping:** unlike `certID = -1`, this is **not** a crash — an in-range-but-undefined id parses whatever sits at `obj.dat` offset 0 (deterministic per cache, not random). ⚠️ **And there is a coherence gap worth naming:** the 2.1 guard returns `invalid()` only for ids **above** `streamIndices.length`, which is `idxCount + 21 + 50000` — so the whole **~50,021-wide in-range window below that still parses offset 0.** The `+50000` is custom-item headroom, i.e. exactly the range this project *does* use for injected content, so "undefined ⇒ `invalid()`" would be a *rendering* change there rather than a pure hardening one. ⚠️ **Recommendation: decide it with the server in view** (does the server ever send an id the client's `obj.dat` does not define?), because the client side alone cannot justify the change and the plan's rule is not to slip in behaviour changes that touch what a player sees.
  - ⚠️ **Remaining before 2.2 can be ticked:** the deferred `streamIndices[i] == 0` case — ⚠️ **now re-scoped to a server-side question rather than a cache look** — and **a live session** — ⚠️ **because the `ObjectManager` change is a rendering change, not purely a hardening one.** On *valid* data it is provably inert (the clamp fires only when `id > Flo.cache.length`, which a well-formed map never does, and any id ≤ 55 is returned untouched), but on malformed data it changes behaviour from "silently abandon the rest of the region's blend" to "blend it, skipping the bad tile" — which is the point, and which only a live run can confirm looks right. ⚠️ **Every reader this phase names has now been audited** — `ItemDef`, `EntityDef`, `ObjectDef`, `Flo`, `IDK`, `VarBit`, `Varp`, `Animation`, `SpotAnim` — so what is left is **consumer-side** indexing plus one deferred behaviour question.
- [x] **2.3 Harden `StreamLoader` against truncated/corrupt archives** — a corrupt or partial cache file must degrade to "missing model", never crash. ⚠️ **Re-scoped by decision (2026-10-04): the original wording also said to "replace the silent hardcoded magic return values" in `Stream`, and that half is DROPPED rather than done** — it contradicts the frozen-contract rule, because those accessors are the sole readers of every packet field and cache definition, so changing what an out-of-range read returns changes how a desync presents. `Stream` is therefore untouched, byte for byte.
  - 🟡 **Reconnaissance DONE (2026-10-04) — the crash surfaces are located, so this is now an implementation task rather than a search.** Recorded so the next session starts with a failing test instead of re-deriving the map.
    - ⚠️ **`Stream`'s silent fallbacks are already pinned and deliberately NOT yet changed:** `readUnsignedWord()` wraps its body in `try { ... } catch(Exception e) { return readUnsignedWord2(); }` and `readUnsignedWord2()` is a **hardcoded `return 1795;`** (`Stream.java:52-54`, `:171-178`); `readUnsignedByte2()` likewise returns a hardcoded **`-263`**; `method421()` catches and returns **`-1`** (`:254-264`); and `writeBytes`' catch **rethrows as a bare `RuntimeException`, discarding the cause** (`:135-139`). ⚠️ **The blast radius is the reason to be careful rather than quick:** every definition reader and every packet parse goes through these, so an out-of-range read *already* yields a plausible wrong number instead of an error — which is why replacing the sentinel is a deliberate, separately-tested change and not a drive-by.
    - ⚠️ **`StreamLoader` has three real corrupt-archive crash paths**, and the important one is that they are **not all inside a `try`**:
      1. **`a(byte[])`'s gzip branch copies the compressed bytes into a buffer sized to the *uncompressed* size**: `byte[] abyte1 = new byte[i]; System.arraycopy(abyte0, 6, abyte1, 0, abyte0.length - 6);` (`StreamLoader.java:43-46`) — so **if the compressed payload is longer than the declared uncompressed size it throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException`**, and that line sits **outside** the `try` that only wraps the later `GZIPInputStream` (`:45-51`). ⚠️ **This is the exact constraint the harness fixtures already work around** (the `padded(...)` helper and the `IllegalStateException` guard in `syntheticArchive`) — so the failure mode is proven, not theorised.
      2. **The gzip `catch` prints the stack trace to stderr and leaves the destination all-zero** (`:49-53`), so a truncated entry degrades to an all-zero loader: `dataSize = 0` and every lookup `null`. ⚠️ **That lands on the "missing" outcome 2.3 wants, but incidentally rather than by intent** — and the all-zero buffer is only benign because the index table happens to read as empty. (Correcting my own earlier note here: I first recorded this catch as *silent*; it does print, and I left it printing on purpose, because it is the only place a corrupt cache entry is ever reported at all.)
      3. **`getDataForName` decompresses/copies a declared range without checking it fits the buffer** (`:91-99`): `Class13.method225(abyte0, anIntArray729[k], aByteArray726, anIntArray730[k], anIntArray731[k])` reads `[offset, offset + compressedSize)` from `aByteArray726` with no bound — reachable when the *index* disagrees with the data length (a corrupt or hostile archive, and note the LZ branch `Class13.method225(...)` at `:58` is likewise outside any `try`).
    - ✅ **IMPLEMENTED (2026-10-04) — three guards in `StreamLoader.a` plus one bound in `getDataForName`, and `Stream` untouched.** (1) the gzip copy is clamped to the declared size (`int copy = Math.min(i, abyte0.length - 6)`), so the oversize branch can no longer overflow its own buffer; (2) a file shorter than its own 6-byte header returns an empty loader instead of reading past the end; (3) the index table is refused unless `stream.currentOffset + (long) dataSize * 10` fits `aByteArray726` — needed because the entry loop reads 10 bytes per entry through `readDWord`/`read3Bytes`, **neither of which is guarded**, so a truncated table threw. In `getDataForName` the declared extent is now bounded before use, and ⚠️ **the span that has to fit differs per branch, which is the one subtlety worth keeping:** when the entry is already decompressed the copy length is the *uncompressed* size, but the LZ branch hands the *compressed* size to `Class13.method225`, so an LZ entry could run off the end **mid-decompression, inside a byte-at-a-time reader that only threw after consuming everything past the end** — hence `span = aBoolean732 ? length : anIntArray730[k]`.
    - ⚠️ **A fourth guard I wrote and then deliberately REMOVED, which is worth recording so it is not re-added:** an `aByteArray726.length < 2` check for a buffer too short to hold a count. It is fully subsumed by guard (3) — `readUnsignedWord` swallows its own out-of-range read and fabricates **1795**, and `2 + 1795 * 10` fails the same fit test — so keeping it would have meant shipping a guard with no test that can fail on it, which is the thing this phase exists to stop.
    - ✅ **Verified the way 2.2 was, failing-first and mutation-pinned.** Before the fix the new checks failed **6 times with `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException`** — the exact predicted mode, not "did not throw" — and after it the harness is **34 → 44/44**. All four guards were then **mutation-tested individually**: neutralising the clamp, the `getDataForName` bound, the short-header guard or the index-fit guard each makes its OWN checks fail with `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` and nothing else (42/2, 40/4, 42/2, 42/2), so no test is vacuous and there is no cross-talk. Fixtures are new helpers — `wrapGzip`, `rawArchive`, `payloadOneEntry`, `payloadWithDeclaredDataSize` — because every existing fixture is built *valid* on purpose and these deliberately are not.
    - ⚠️⚠️ **The golden-master gate came back VACUOUS, and that is a finding in its own right.** `gradlew compareCapture` reported **0 divergences / IDENTICAL** — but the "current" capture it picked (`packet-tap-.log`) was **byte-identical to the committed golden master** (same SHA-256, `249ECAF6…`). Two sessions cannot legitimately match, because the ISAAC keys and login seeds are per-session random; the file is simply the session the golden master was **promoted from** (golden created 15:35:56 by a copy that preserved its 15:33:27 mtime, committed 15:40:43), so the tool compared the golden against itself and printed a green that proves nothing. ⚠️ **`tools/compare-capture.ps1` now REFUSES to compare byte-identical files** (exit 2, with the reasoning in the message), which was verified both ways: an identical pair is refused, while a capture differing by one unit still compares and still pinpoints it (1 divergence at unit 9470). The `packet-tap-.log` name is itself the pre-fix `%STAMP%` artefact, so a fresh `Record.bat` capture will be stamped.
    - ⚠️⚠️ **Follow-up (2026-10-04 evening): a fresh capture now EXISTS and was analysed — and it also exposed that the comparator's positional verdict is unusable across sessions.** The new capture is a genuinely different, shorter route (**7,298 units, 235 sent / 7,063 received**, vs the golden's 9,470 / 365 / 9,105), so the positional diff reported **5,944 divergences** even though (a) the **first 701 units matched to the unit** — the login handshake *and* the initial region load, which is precisely the region Phase 2 touches — (b) the received stream around the first divergence was **undisturbed** (units 703-708 identical), (c) the long "consecutive" run was **one extra client packet (`W 7`) shifting the alignment by one**, (d) **no packet length was anomalous** (max 171 = the login block in both), and (e) both sessions ended in the same idle heartbeat. ⚠️ **`compare-capture.ps1` therefore now decides on `common prefix` + `LCS / shorter` instead of raw positional divergence**, calibrated on the real files (**89.1%** for same-protocol/different-route; **60.1%** shuffled / **62.5%** reversed as the destroyed-structure floor, so the threshold is **75%**, with a **600-unit** prefix gate under the measured 701). ⚠️ **Verified both ways:** the real pair → COMPATIBLE (exit 0), identical pair → refused (exit 2), 3 units dropped from the **prefix** → DRIFT (exit 1), one **prefix** unit resized → DRIFT (exit 1), 3 units dropped from the **tail** → COMPATIBLE (exit 0, the documented limit). **Two of those have LCS 100% and are caught only by the prefix gate** — the reason both signals are kept. ⚠️ **The surrounding tooling was hardened at the same time:** `Record.bat` now runs `tools/verify-capture.ps1` on its own output (exists → holds units → not the golden master) and refuses to compare a dead run, and the `compareCapture` Gradle task no longer auto-selects the bare `packet-tap-.log`.
    - ✅ **2.3 is TICKED (2026-10-04 evening):** the change is inert on valid data — it only rejects archives the format never defined — and the live session confirms no regression: the client logged in, loaded its regions and played normally, exited **code 0**, and the fresh capture (`packet-tap-20261004-213845.log`) compares **COMPATIBLE** with the **701-unit** deterministic login/load prefix intact. ⚠️ **That leaves the corrupt-data behaviour as the only untested corner, and the harness is what covers it** — a corrupt archive cannot be produced on demand in a live session, which is exactly why the four bounds were mutation-tested rather than played.
    - ⚠️ **Where truncation is actually absorbed today, for completeness:** `client.streamLoaderForName` wraps both the load and the constructor in `catch (Exception _ex) { }` (`client.java:7010-7031`), so in the *real* client these exceptions are swallowed a level up — meaning the observable symptom is a **missing/broken model, not a crash**, and the task is to make that degradation deliberate and bounded instead of incidental.

It stayed silent here because the codebase carries no `@Override` anywhere, and an `@Override` on a severed override is a compile error.** ⚠️ **The corrective diff (`f4b2907a`) is ten declarations gaining `public` and nothing else; `gradlew clean check installBin` is green; the live retest is the outstanding step.** ⚠️ **Also recorded: the packet oracle CANNOT catch this — it compares protocol units, and a client that never paints still frames packets identically — so a screen check is not optional for this phase.**
