#### Phase 6.5.2 DONE AND LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-10-05) - the slot budget has one owner

**What changed:** `model.FrameSlots` is now the single owner of the frame-slot budget, and the three writers consult it instead of re-deriving their bounds locally.

| what | before | now |
|---|---|---|
| loose floor | `id >= 100` inside `loadLooseFrameFiles` | `FrameSlots.isLooseSlot(id)` |
| curse window | `ANIM_FILE_BASE = 28000` + `local < 0 || local >= 7` | `FrameSlots.isCurseSlot(id)` / `CURSE_BASE` |
| remap + preload | `ANIM_FILE_BASE + i` in two places | `FrameSlots.CURSE_BASE + i` |
| key limit | **not expressed anywhere** | `FrameSlots.MAX_FILE_ID = 32767` |

⚠ **This is a MOVE, not a change, and that was verified two ways: by hand, that each rewritten condition accepts/rejects exactly the same ids as the literal it replaced** (the old `local < 0 || local >= 7` and `!isCurseSlot(id)` reject precisely the same set), **and by test.** ⚠ **Nothing about precedence moved** - which writer wins a contested slot is still decided by `method531` (EQUAL-EMPTY) and by `loadFrames` running packed-then-loose; `FrameSlots` owns only the NUMBERS, and says so.

⚠ **`MAX_FILE_ID` is the one thing here that was previously INEXPRESSIBLE.** `Animation.anintArray353` packs the file into the HIGH 16 bits, so `file << 16` must stay a positive `int`; an id above 32767 silently yields a NEGATIVE key and the frame simply never resolves. ⚠ **The CursePack window (28000..28006) is inside the limit, but nothing stated the limit before - and the loose loader still accepts any `{id}.dat` >= 100 with no upper bound.** That is recorded as a hazard rather than silently changed, because rejecting high ids would change what loads.

⚠ **6.5.2's stated minimum bar is met: "a test that asserts the 474 slots still decode identically after an injection".** A CursePack injection at 28000 is performed, and a 474-range slot (500) must decode to `27,38,49` both before AND after - with a NON-VACUOUS control proving the two slots genuinely hold different data, so the comparison cannot pass by comparing nothing.

**Static gates:** harness **174 → 187/187** (13 new), `clean check installBin` green, warnings exactly **100**. ⚠ **Mutation-proved four ways, all caught:** moving `CURSE_BASE` to 3000 (inside the packed range - the exact collision it prevents); raising `LOOSE_MIN_ID` to 1000; raising `MAX_FILE_ID` to 65535; dropping `CURSE_COUNT` to 5.

⚠⚠ **AND ONE HONEST GAP, RECORDED RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER: the fifth mutation - wiring `loadLooseFrameFiles` to a predicate that never fires - is NOT caught by any unit test.** It cannot be, because that method reads the real cache directory and no test supplies one. ⚠ **The live gate is what covers it:** the real cache root holds exactly 1777, 2160 and 3502, all of which load through that call, so a broken predicate would visibly break those animations. ⚠ **THAT WAS THE STATE AT IMPLEMENTATION TIME - THE PROBE BELOW NOW CLOSES IT, so this paragraph is history, not an open gap.**

⚠ **A CAPTURE WAS SUBMITTED FOR THIS GATE AND REJECTED - recorded here so it is never mistaken for the 6.5.2 evidence.** `packet-tap-20261005-223450.log` (4089 units, 4038 received / 51 sent, COMPATIBLE, prefix 599/600, LCS 98.4%) is a clean, valid capture **of the client as it stood BEFORE 6.5.2**, so it exercises the OLD code path and cannot close this phase. ⚠ **Proved by BYTECODE, not by the clock alone:** the committed `Proxy Client/bin/def/CurseData667.class` references `ANIM_FILE_BASE` and contains no `FrameSlots` symbol at all, and `bin/` was never cleaned (436 classes, most dated 09-20..10-01), so the class that tap ran against is the pre-6.5.2 one. The timestamps agree: the log was written at **22:35:33**, `src/model/FrameSlots.java` was created at **22:57:41**, the 6.5.2 sources last changed at **22:59:25**, and `bin/` + `build/` were rebuilt at **22:59:34-36**. ⚠ **What that capture IS good for:** it re-proves the PRE-EDIT baseline was COMPATIBLE at 599/600 and 98.4%, which is exactly the control the post-edit run should be judged against - it is just not the gate itself.

⚠ **Static gate re-run after the 6.5.2 edit and green: `passed: 187  failed: 0`, `clean check installBin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL, warnings exactly 100.**

⚠⚠ **LIVE GATE PASSED (2026-10-05 23:06) ON A BUILD THAT PROVABLY CONTAINED THE CHANGE.** Capture `packet-tap-20261005-230541.log` was written at **23:06:19**, against `bin/` built at **22:59:36** - and decisively, the bytecode it loaded references `FrameSlots` and `isCurseSlot`/`isLooseSlot` with `ANIM_FILE_BASE` GONE, so the tap exercised the REWIRED predicates rather than the old literals. **All four of 6.5.2's stated checks pass:** **(a) protocol** - COMPATIBLE, prefix **595/600** (need >= 590), LCS **97.8%** (need >= 75%), exit 0, 4153 units (4095 received / 58 sent); no wire code was touched, and this is consistent with the previous baseline's 599/600 and 98.4%. **(b) loose frames still load** - proven by the probe below; this was the only check with no unit-test coverage. **(c) CursePack still injects at 28000+** - the startup log prints `CursePack models loaded: 9/9` and `Injected curse seq/gfx from CursePack (frames remapped)`, so the remap still resolves to the high slots. **(d) animations still play** - no exceptions and a normal `Client exited (code 0)` across the 667 content (walked, attacked, picked up items, past Nex / curse / Bloat and the Rock Crab area).

⚠⚠ **THE RECORDED GAP IS NOW CLOSED, NOT EYEBALLED.** `tools/loose-frame-probe/run.ps1` compiles the probe in `tools/loose-frame-probe/` - a directory gradle does NOT compile (`sourceSets.main.java.srcDirs = ['src']`), so it cannot reach the shipped client - and calls the REAL `Frames.loadFrames()` against the REAL `%USERPROFILE%\Biohazard.474` root, which holds exactly 1777.dat, 2160.dat and 3502.dat. Result: `animationlist` **1777 -> 546**, **2160 -> 1695**, **3502 -> 1236**. ⚠ **Two controls make that non-vacuous:** `frameData[500]` is present, proving the packed path genuinely decoded, and `animationlist[500]` is **0**, proving packed-only ids do NOT enter that array - so the three non-zero results can only come from the loose loader. ⚠ **And it is mutation-proved: `run.ps1 -Mutate` rebuilds `FrameSlots` with `isLooseSlot` returning false into a shadow directory, after which the probe reports `LOOSE_FAIL` (exit 1) with `animationlist` never allocated.** That is exactly the fifth mutation the harness misses, so the gap is no longer merely "left to the live gate" - it has a repeatable check that fails the moment the wiring breaks.

⚠ **Why the probe is shaped this way:** `loadPacked` fills `frameData` (a `byte[][]`) only, while `animationlist[file]` is populated ONLY by the loose loader during `loadFrames` (the lazy decode in the accessor runs on access, not at load). So a non-zero `animationlist[1777]` is specific evidence that `FrameSlots.isLooseSlot` fired, rather than a general sign that *something* loaded.

#### Phase 6.5.3 DONE AND LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-10-05) - reuse the 474 readers where the formats agree

**The correction comes first, because it reframes the phase.** This plan cited `SpotAnim.readValues667` as "the existing precedent that the format genuinely differs". It is not. It is:

```java
void readValues667(Stream stream) { readValues(stream); }
```

⚠ **A pure pass-through, and it existed for a VISIBILITY reason, not a format one:** `SpotAnim.readValues` was `private`, and `CurseData667` is in the same package and needed to call it. So the 667 "variant" was a package shim whose name asserted a difference that does not exist - the kind of thing that makes the next reader believe the formats differ and hand-roll a second parser. **The alias is now deleted and `readValues` is public**, so the reuse is literal and self-describing.

**The measurement that grounds "the formats agree"** (walk both real CursePack files with each path's opcode table, requiring the walk to land on exact EOF):

| file | entries | bytes | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `seq.dat` | 15371 | 2516296 | lands EXACTLY on EOF, **0** bad opcodes; opcodes used: 0,1,2,3,5,6,7,8,9,10,11 |
| `spotanim.dat` | 2982 | 29046 | lands EXACTLY on EOF, **0** bad opcodes; opcodes used: 0,1,2,4,5,6,7,8,40 |

⚠⚠ **The ONE genuine divergence, recorded rather than tidied: seq opcode 12.** The skip table consumes one byte for it; `Animation.readValues` has **no case for 12** and consumes nothing (it prints `Unrecognized seq.dat config code: 12`). That is a real difference in where the cursor lands - **but opcode 12 occurs ZERO times in the real pack**, so it is latent. Removing that case would change nothing today and would silently break the pack the day it uses 12, which is exactly why it is asserted instead of removed.

⚠ **Why the skip tables exist at all, stated explicitly rather than left as an accident:** the readers **allocate** per entry (opcode 1 builds three `int[]` of length n), so decoding the ~15k UNWANTED entries only to discard them would churn garbage. The hand-written walks are a deliberate optimisation - and their cost is a second copy of the opcode knowledge, which is the drift risk 6.5.3 has to bound.

**The drift guard (the durable part).** 5 new tests pin the skip tables against the readers **opcode by opcode**, and two design choices are what give them teeth:

⚠ **One continuous stream, not one stream per entry.** A misalignment at entry i then shifts every later boundary, so a one-byte disagreement cannot hide behind a per-entry coincidence.
⚠ **Poison (non-opcode) payload bytes.** This is the part I got WRONG first: with small payload values, a mutated skipper that consumed one byte where the reader consumes two landed on another VALID opcode, coincidentally re-synced, and the mutation **PASSED** - a drift guard that could not detect drift. With poison payloads the same mutation hits an unrecognised opcode and stops the walk early, and the test fails with `opcode 7 skip=35 read=36`. **The comment in the fixture records this, because the lesson matters more than a tidy-looking test.**

⚠ **Mutation-proved: seq opcode 7 changed from a word to a byte -> FAIL (2 failures, `skip=35 read=36`), build FAILED. Reverted and re-gated green.**

**Static gates:** harness 187 -> **192/192** (5 new), `clean check installBin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL, warnings exactly **100**.

⚠⚠ **LIVE GATE PASSED (2026-10-05 23:22) ON A BUILD THAT PROVABLY CONTAINED THE CHANGE - and the bytecode proof here doubles as a warning about `bin/`.** Capture `packet-tap-20261005-232207.log` was written at **23:22:32** against `bin/def/` built at **23:20:13**, and `javap` on the classes it actually loaded confirms the rewiring: `def.SpotAnim` declares **`public void readValues(net.Stream)`** and contains **no `readValues667` at all**, while `def.CurseData667` issues **`invokevirtual def/SpotAnim.readValues:(Lnet/Stream;)V`** - the 667 spotanim path is the 474 reader by name, exactly as intended. **(a)** protocol COMPATIBLE, prefix **595/600** (need >= 590), LCS **3911/3965 = 98.6%** (need >= 75%), 3965 units (3929 received / 36 sent), exit 0. **(b)** the walk still lands on the correct boundaries: the startup log shows `CursePack: C:\Users\llrbi\Biohazard.474\CursePack`, `CursePack models loaded: 9/9` and `Injected curse seq/gfx from CursePack (frames remapped)` - which for a file walk is the real evidence, since a desync corrupts the injected definitions rather than necessarily throwing. **(c)** 667 content visited as the capture script asks.

⚠⚠ **THE `bin/` GOTCHA, PRE-EXISTING RATHER THAN CAUSED HERE.** `bin/` still holds **212 default-package `.class` files from BEFORE the Phase 3.1 package move**, including **`bin/SpotAnim.class` and `bin/CurseData667.class` (both 2026-10-04 23:14) which STILL contain `readValues667`**. A naive "does the build still contain readValues667?" scan over `bin/` therefore returns a **FALSE POSITIVE** and looks like the change never landed. ⚠ **The capture is NOT affected: nothing in the current build references those copies.** `javap` over every packaged class finds **zero** bare references to them, and the startup log prints the CursePack sequence **exactly once** (from `def.CurseData667`), so Java resolves `def.*` by name and the stale copies are unreachable. ⚠ They are **unmodified vs HEAD**, so no phase introduced them, and `reportStaleClasses` already lists them under **"(Phase 8 cleanup)"** - known and scheduled, not a silent trap. ⚠ **Recorded anyway, because the next person verifying a `def/`-level change by grepping `bin/` will hit exactly this.**

⚠ **The measurement is now REPEATABLE rather than a one-off: `tools/curse-opcode-probe/run.ps1`.** It runs BOTH shipping paths over the REAL pack (calling the pack's private skipper and the 474 reader by reflection, so it cannot drift from what it checks) and requires the cursor to land identically after every entry, and both walks to reach exact EOF. **On the real pack: seq 15371/15371 entries walked by both, both ending exactly at byte 2516296; spotanim 2982/2982, both ending exactly at 29046.** ⚠ **Agreement is checked PER ENTRY, so the walk is proven identical at every boundary, not merely at the end.**

⚠⚠ **That agreement is what PROVES the opcode-12 divergence is latent, and it is a stronger result than the histogram.** A single opcode 12 makes the reader consume nothing where the skipper consumes one byte, so the cursor ends up one byte out and every later boundary disagrees - and there is no path that re-syncs them, because a skipper only ever stops early or consumes a fixed amount. So "no entry disagrees" is only possible if 12 never occurs. ⚠ **The probe reports entry-start opcodes only (the first opcode of each entry) and labels them as such** - a FULL histogram is not observable from it, because `Stream` is `final` and the skippers do not report what they read - so the probe adds no third opcode table. Its entry-start counts cross-check the independent measurement: 15172 and 2844 entries begin with opcode 1, matching that histogram exactly.

⚠⚠ **The probe is mutation-proved too, and the failure CORRECTS something I had written here first.** Making the skipper read opcode 7 as a byte instead of a word fails it (exit 1) - but not by corrupting quietly: the desynchronised skipper ran PAST EOF and threw `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` out of `CurseData667.inject`, which has no `try`/`catch` of its own and is called during client startup (`client.java:8259`). **So a desync has two outcomes and BOTH are bad:** it throws if it overruns the file, and it silently mangles every later definition if the drift happens to stay inside it. That is exactly why the live gate cannot be replaced by "it did not crash". The probe reports either outcome as a verdict (offending entry, walk counts, first diverging offset) instead of escaping as a stack trace, because a check whose whole job is to be readable when something is wrong should not fail by crashing.

⚠ **"What is NOT covered" is now narrower but not empty, and is recorded rather than implied:** the hermetic guard can only pin the two tables against EACH OTHER, not against the real pack - the probe is what closes that - and the full opcode histogram remains a non-hermetic one-off. ⚠ Also still true and worth knowing: `CurseData667`'s `catch` blocks around gzip/model reads are empty, so those failures stay silent.

#### Phase 6.5.4 ✅ DONE (2026-10-05) - the 667 pipeline verified as a whole, by consolidation

**Why consolidating is legitimate HERE rather than a shortcut.** 6.5.4 asks for a live verification of the pipeline "same as every other phase", and names the asymmetry that matters: 667 content cannot break SERVER compatibility because it never touches the wire, but it CAN break 474 content. **Both halves of that asymmetry were already measured on the unmodified server by the two gates this phase closed**, so 6.5.4's content is a **consolidation of evidence that exists**, not a new experiment. It is recorded here as one place with the artifact named for each claim, because "it was verified somewhere" is not a verification.

**The claim -> artifact map:**

| claim | artifact | result |
|---|---|---|
| 667 cannot break the wire | golden-master `compareCapture`, on the two gates that touched 667 code | 6.5.2 capture COMPATIBLE, prefix **595/600**, LCS **97.8%**; 6.5.3 capture COMPATIBLE, prefix **595/600**, LCS **98.6%**; both exit 0 |
| 667 does not break 474 content | harness (192 tests), incl. the 474 slot-decodes-identically-after-injection test and the real-model export/raster pins | **192/192**, 100 warnings |
| 474 loose slots still load | `tools/loose-frame-probe` | 1777/2160/3502 -> **546/1695/1236** frames; controls prove the packed path is live AND packed-only ids stay out; **LOOSE_OK** |
| 667 definitions read correctly | `tools/curse-opcode-probe` | seq **15371/15371**, spotanim **2982/2982**, walked by BOTH paths with identical per-entry offsets and both reaching exact EOF; **OPCODE_PROBE_OK** |
| CursePack injects at 28000+ | client startup log | `CursePack models loaded: 9/9`, `Injected curse seq/gfx from CursePack (frames remapped)` |
| 667 content renders | the operator's screen recording | ⚠ **NOT machine-checked** - see below |

**All three machine gates were re-run as ONE snapshot at commit `e61772e8`**, so the numbers above belong to a single tree rather than to several builds: gate **192/192** + **100** warnings; loose-frame probe **exit 0**; curse-opcode probe **exit 0**.

⚠⚠ **What consolidation does NOT cover, stated rather than implied:**

1. **Visual rendering is not machine-verified.** No artifact here proves that a curse gfx DRAWS correctly - that rests on the operator's screen recording. The code-path evidence (definitions read, frames loaded, injection logged) is **necessary but not sufficient**, and this plan should not be read as claiming otherwise.
2. **The scene-level raster hash (4.3) is still unreachable headlessly** - `method315`/`method316` read `client.tileMarkers` - so the raster pins cover the triangle paths, not the scene path.
3. **Both probes are non-hermetic**: they read the real cache root and the real CursePack, so they are gate tools that must be RUN, not tests that run themselves.

✅ **6.5.4 closed on that basis, and with it Phase 6.5's verification obligations:** the slot budget has one owner (6.5.2), the readers are reused where the formats agree with the one divergence recorded (6.5.3), and the pipeline as a whole is accounted for above (6.5.4).

#### Registry recon (2026-10-05) - and the defect it found

⚠⚠ **Three hand-written animations render with NO FRAMES, and have been silently broken.** `Animation.java` writes 25 animations by hand across `applyDeathlyExtraAnims`, `applyBorkAnims` and `applyBloodReaverAnims`. Each frame literal hides its source file, because a frame is encoded `file << 16 | index` - so the file a hand-written animation depends on is a **magic number buried in its data**, which is precisely why the dependency was never visible:

| file | references | packed `Frames.dat`? | loose `{file}.dat`? | resolves? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1777 | 133 (Blood reaver) | yes (id <= 3229) | yes | ✅ via loose - **loose SHADOWS packed** |
| 2160 | 154 (Bork) | yes | yes | ✅ via loose - **loose SHADOWS packed** |
| 3502 | 427 (15 anims) | **no** | yes | ✅ via loose |
| **3403** | 28 (anim 4000) | **no** | **no** | ❌ **NOWHERE** |
| **3353** | 55 (anims 4001, 4002) | **no** | **no** | ❌ **NOWHERE** |

**Three independent measurements, not one.** (1) Deriving `frame >> 16` from every frame literal gives the files above. (2) Parsing `Frames.dat` directly - 3230 entries, ids **0..3229**, exact byte coverage - proves the packed archive cannot supply 3353 or 3403. (3) Calling the REAL `Frames.method531` on a sample frame from each file returns **NULL for 3403 and 3353**, while 1777/2160/3502 resolve.

```
file    animlist[file]  packed[0]  packed[1]  method531(sample)
1777    546            true       false      ok
2160    1695           true       false      ok
3502    1236           false      false      ok
3403    0              false      false      NULL (unresolved)   <- anim 4000
3353    0              false      false      NULL (unresolved)   <- anims 4001, 4002
```

⚠ **This is reachable, not academic: anims 4001 and 4002 are assigned to SPOTANIMS 1247 and 1248** (`SpotAnim.unpackConfig`), with model 60776 - so those graphics animate with no frames. Anim 4000 has no other reference in `src/`, so it may be unused; that is stated as uncertain rather than claimed as dead.

✅ **It is a WIRING gap, not a missing-data gap - proved, not assumed.** `667 Data\667 Animations\3403.gz` (43070 bytes) and `3353.gz` (8934 bytes) exist outside the client, and decoding them through the same `Frames.load` the CursePack path uses yields **2370** and **438** frames. The hand-written animations need indices 255..699 (anim 4000) and <= 141 (anims 4001/4002), so **every required frame is in range**. A CursePack control (2998.gz -> 723 frames) proves the environment really was decoding, so this cannot pass by doing nothing.

⚠ **Two side findings, recorded rather than left trailing:** the loose 1777/2160 files **shadow** their packed 474 counterparts, which is this plan's open item 5 now MEASURED instead of suspected; and there is a stray `src/Animation.java.bak-pre-orig` (a pre-package-move copy - not compiled, since gradle only takes `*.java`, but it belongs with the Phase 8 cleanup).

⚠ **The registry is now the natural home for this.** Declaring (id, revision, source file) per injected animation would have caught the gap at declaration time, and the same declaration is what lets the loader fix it. **The decision - declare + REPORT (no behaviour change) versus declare + LOAD (fixes anims 4000/4001/4002, a deliberate behaviour change) - is the user's, and is the first question of the build.**

#### Phase 6.5.5 ✅ DONE (2026-10-05) - the content registry, and the three animations it fixed

✅ **Ticked on the operator's explicit waiver of the visual axis** - recorded as a waiver, not as a verification. ⚠ **The unverified item is the RENDERING of the DEATHLY-EXTRA gfx (anims 4000/4001/4002, via spotanims 1247/1248 and model 60776), which is a DIFFERENT id set from the CursePack curse gfx (2213, 2226, 2228, 2229, 2230, 2259, 2263, 2264, 2266) - the curse-gfx side was already ticked in 6.5.3/6.5.4.**

**What it does.** `def.ContentRegistry` is the one place that declares each externally-sourced frame file - (slot, revision, asset, the animations that need it) - and the one place that loads them, from `{cache}/667Anims/anims/{id}.gz`, mirroring `CurseData667`'s CursePack convention. `client.java` runs `ContentRegistry.init()` + `loadAll()` immediately after `CurseData667.inject()`, so the declared sources are in place before anything can play them. The pack is deployed the same way CursePack is: copied into the cache root.

**What it fixes.** Anims **4000** (file 3403) and **4001/4002** (file 3353) rendered with **NO FRAMES**, because nothing supplied those files. Anims 4001/4002 are assigned to **spotanims 1247/1248**, so this was reachable graphics rather than dead data.

⚠ **Why the slot choice is safe, and pinned rather than asserted.** 3403/3353 sit ABOVE the packed 474 range (0..3229) and OUTSIDE the CursePack window (28000..28007). That is checked by test - `Registry: no declared slot lands inside the packed 474 range` and `... inside the CursePack window` - because `Frames.load` does **not** look before it writes, so a colliding slot would silently overwrite real frames rather than fail.

**Probe result (real cache, deliberately non-hermetic):**

```
sources loaded            : 2/2
declared anim frames      : 83
resolved BEFORE the load  : 0 / 83   (control - must be 0)
resolved AFTER the load   : 83 / 83
declaration drift report  : clean
CONTENT_REGISTRY_OK
```

⚠⚠ **The control is the part that matters.** 0/83 BEFORE the load is what proves the defect was real AND that the probe is sensitive. A probe reporting success whether or not the file existed is exactly how this defect survived, so `tools/content-registry-probe/run.ps1` resolves every declared frame TWICE - once with the load suppressed, once with it performed - and fails if the control is not zero. It also reads the FACT (the animations' own frames, rebuilt the way `Animation.unpackConfig` builds them) rather than the CLAIM (the declaration), because a probe that re-read the declaration would only prove it agrees with itself.

**Static gates:** harness 192 -> **207/207** (15 new), `clean check installBin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL, warnings exactly **100** in the main compile and **ZERO** in the harness.

⚠ **A stale gate claim corrected while doing this:** the harness had **TWO `dangling-doc-comments` warnings** - one of them dating from 6.5.3 - that **incremental compilation had been hiding**, so earlier "warnings exactly 100" readings described a stale compile. `clean` surfaced them; both blocks are now `//` section comments and the harness compiles with zero warnings.

⚠⚠ **This is a BEHAVIOUR change, which is why it is not ticked.** Anims 4000/4001/4002 will start animating where they previously drew nothing. The wire cannot be affected - loading frame files does not touch packet framing - so the expectation is an unchanged capture verdict, but the VISUAL result is the actual claim, and only the live client can show it.

**LIVE GATE (2026-10-05 23:39, capture `packet-tap-20261005-233954.log`) - ✅ PASSED AT EVERY MEASURABLE LEVEL.**

| check | evidence |
|---|---|
| ran the 6.5.5 bytecode | `client.class` built **23:37:06**, log written **23:40:28** |
| boot path calls the registry | `javap game.client`: `invokestatic def/ContentRegistry.init:()V` then `loadAll:()I` |
| registry found its pack | console: `667Anims: C:\Users\llrbi\Biohazard.474\667Anims` |
| both sources loaded | NO `667Anims source missing` and NO `source unreadable` lines |
| wire unchanged | **COMPATIBLE**, prefix **595/600**, LCS **3984/4058 = 98.2%**, exit 0 |
| 6.5.3 path unaffected | `CursePack models loaded: 9/9` + injection line, unchanged |

⚠ **Where the boot evidence lives, because it is easy to look in the wrong place:** these lines go to the client CONSOLE. The packet tap logs packets only, so searching the `.log` for `667Anims` finds nothing - correctly. The console is the artifact for "did the registry run", and the `.log` is the artifact for "is the wire unchanged".

⚠ **And the wire prediction held as stated.** 6.5.4 recorded that 667 content cannot break SERVER compatibility because it never touches the wire; loading frame files is the same category, and the verdicts bear it out - **prefix 595/600 on all three captures** (6.5.2, 6.5.3, 6.5.5) at LCS 97.8%, 98.6% and 98.2%. Three independent runs landing on the identical prefix score is a stronger statement than any one of them.

⚠⚠ **THE VISUAL CLAIM IS STILL UNVERIFIED, AND THE CHECKBOX STAYS UNTICKED FOR IT.** What is proved is that the declared frames **resolve** (probe: 0/83 before the load, 83/83 after) and that the client **loads** them (console). What is NOT proved is that anims 4000/4001/4002 **render** - that is what the operator's screen recording is for, and this plan does not report a screen recording it cannot see.

⚠ **Session hygiene:** the deployed pack survived the run, the cache root still holds NO numeric `.dat` for 3353/3403 (so the loose loader cannot shadow the registry), and the character save-state (`bob.txt`, dirtied in three places by every capture) was restored.

**REPEAT GATE (2026-10-05 23:42, capture `packet-tap-20261005-234258.log`) - ✅ the gate REPRODUCES.** Same build (`client.class` 23:37:06, and **no source change since the 6.5.5 commit**), same console lines, **COMPATIBLE, prefix 595/600, LCS 98.9%, exit 0**. ⚠ **Prefix 595/600 has now appeared on FOUR consecutive captures** (6.5.2, 6.5.3, and two 6.5.5 runs) at LCS 97.8 / 98.6 / 98.2 / 98.9. A stable score across independent sessions is a stronger statement than any single run.

⚠⚠ **WHAT I TRIED SO AS TO STOP DEPENDING ON THE OPERATOR'S EYES - AND WHY IT DOES NOT SETTLE IT.** Since the visual claim was the last thing outstanding, I investigated proving the animations FUNCTION mechanically. Spotanims 1247/1248 both point at **model 60776**, and that model exists (`667 Data\667 GFX Models\60776.gz`), so the plan was: size the model array with `Model.method459(0, null)`, load the model through the SAME injection path `Model.method460` that CurseData667 uses, apply two different frames of anim 4001 via `Model.method470`, and assert the vertices move.

⚠ **It cannot cleanly adjudicate, and I stopped rather than report a misleading number.** `method470` only deforms a model that carries SKIN data (`anIntArrayArray1657`, built by `method469`); a model with no vertex groups is legitimately STATIC however many frames load. So "vertices did not move" would be ambiguous between *correctly static because the model has no skins* and *the wiring is broken* - and a check that cannot tell those apart is not a gate. Separating them requires reasoning about the 667 CONTENT, which is not what 6.5.5 changes.

⚠ **Which reframes 6.5.5's own claim, and I overreached when I called the visual its gate.** 6.5.5 is a WIRING fix: its correctness is "the registry declares the right slots, loads the right sources, and collides with nothing" - all of which IS verified (declaration tests, probe control 0/83 -> 83/83, live console, wire unchanged). Whether the resulting graphics LOOK right depends on the 667 data itself, which the registry neither supplies nor alters. **So the checkbox is held pending one word from the operator - not pending something I can compute.**

⚠ **The gate rode along in the 2026-10-06 00:16 session (recorded in full under Phase 7.2a) and was re-demonstrated on the 2026-10-06 01:04 session: `CursePack models loaded: 9/9` and the `Injected curse seq/gfx from CursePack (frames remapped)` line, both unchanged across four independent captures. This heading and its checkbox read "AWAITING THE LIVE GATE" long after the gate had passed; corrected here rather than left to contradict the record.**

**What moved.** The CursePack's declarations left `CurseData667`, and SpotAnim's two override branches left `SpotAnim`. `def.ContentRegistry` now declares:

| declaration | was | now |
|---|---|---|
| curse sequences to inject | `CurseData667.CURSE_SEQ_IDS` (private) | `ContentRegistry.CURSE_SEQ_IDS` |
| curse graphics to inject | `CurseData667.CURSE_GFX_IDS` (private) | `ContentRegistry.CURSE_GFX_IDS` |

**The loaders keep the MECHANISM** - how to walk an unwanted entry, how to remap a frame, when to re-read a file - because that is genuinely the loader's business. What the registry owns is the DECLARATION: what content exists, where it comes from, and where it lands. That is exactly the split 6.5.1 chose, with `FrameSlots` still owning the slot BUDGET (6.5.2).

⚠⚠ **Pinned as a MOVE, not a change.** A "copy the array somewhere else" edit is the easiest place to silently alter content, so the tests assert the VALUES: `CURSE_SEQ_IDS` is exactly the historical 11 ids, `CURSE_GFX_IDS` the historical 9, `CURSE_MODEL_IDS` the historical 9, `CURSE_ANIM_FILES` the historical 7, and the overrides still carry gfx 1247 -> model 60776/anim 4001 and gfx 1248 -> model 60776/anim 4002.

### Phase 7 — OpenGL backend (proceed only after 0–6, all verified) ⚠️ LAST
- [x] **7.1 ⚠ Do NOT use `Display.setParent`** — it deadlocks Windows here already. Use **LWJGL 3** with `org.lwjgl.opengl.awt.AWTGLCanvas` (single AWT component), or an offscreen context, and record the choice with the reason. ✅ **DECIDED AND MEASURED 2026-10-05: OFFSCREEN. LWJGL 3.3.6 renders into an FBO with a depth attachment on a HIDDEN GLFW window's context, and the result is read back into the software framebuffer; `AWTGLCanvas` is REJECTED.** ⚠ **The hybrid requirement decides it: software UI composited ON TOP of the scene is natural when the scene lands in the same `int[]` the software path writes, and is the unreliable heavyweight-overlap case with an AWT `Canvas`. Offscreen also means NO AWT/GL interop exists, so `Display.setParent`'s deadlock class cannot arise.** ⚠ **Proven on this machine by `tools/gl-smoke` (reproducible): core-profile 3.3 context on a hidden window, shader linked, FBO COMPLETE, readback correct, control holds, depth test correct, `glGetError` clean. Jars vendored to `deps/lwjgl3/` (6, 2.24 MB); not on the main classpath yet.**
- [ ] **7.2 Implement the GL backend behind Phase 4's seam**, hybrid by design: 3D scene via GL, **UI/chat/tabs stay software and composite on top** (the RuneLite seam). This is what keeps the work bounded. ⚠ **7.2a IS IN, LIVE-GATED, AND ITS CONTEXT IS PROVEN IN-PROCESS (2026-10-06): `ui/GlScene` + `ui/GlSceneRenderer` exist, `RendererConfig` installs the `gl` arm, the LWJGL 2/3 collision is resolved at COMPILE and RUNTIME (`tools/gl-classpath-probe`, mutation-proved), the software path boots unchanged (COMPATIBLE, prefix 595/600), AND selecting `renderer=gl` brings the offscreen context up on the GAME THREAD (`offscreen 765x503, GL_VERSION=3.3.0 NVIDIA 596.36`) with no stray window and no exceptions.** ⚠ **7.2b-1 IS IN AND GPU-VERIFIED (2026-10-06): `ui/GlBatcher` is the GPU triangle sink - pixel-space triangles into 5.2's buffers, one VBO, drawn into `GlScene`'s FBO with depth, read back as `0x00RRGGBB` - proved by `tools/gl-batch-probe` (mutation-proved three ways).** ⚠ **7.2b-2a IS IN AND ORACLE-VERIFIED (2026-10-06): `ui/GlModelProjection` reproduces `Model.method443`'s transform, and the harness requires EXACT agreement with the real `method443`'s own projected arrays - harness 252/252.** ⚠ **7.2b-2b - WIRING THE SCENE - HAS NOT STARTED: the arm still declines every operation, so the LIFECYCLE, the SINK and the PROJECTION are proven and NOT a pixel of the game.**

#### Phase 7.1 DECIDED (2026-10-05) - OFFSCREEN, and why NOT `AWTGLCanvas`

**The decision, in one line:** render the 3D scene with **LWJGL 3.3.6 into an OFFSCREEN framebuffer** - a hidden GLFW window's context plus an FBO with a colour and a depth attachment - **read it back with `glReadPixels` into the software framebuffer**, and let the software renderer draw the UI on top.

**The four options, and why three lose:**

| option | verdict | reason |
|---|---|---|
| `Display.setParent` (LWJGL 2) | rejected - already recorded | deadlocks Windows here: AWT and LWJGL pump the SAME HWND when the frame gains or loses focus |
| **offscreen FBO + readback (LWJGL 3)** | **CHOSEN** | no AWT/GL interop at all; the scene lands in the SAME `int[]` the software path writes, so UI compositing and every Phase 4 seam are untouched |
| `AWTGLCanvas` (LWJGL 3) | rejected | it is a HEAVYWEIGHT AWT `Canvas`. 7.2's design needs software UI/chat/tabs drawn ON TOP of the scene, and overlapping software-drawn content over a heavyweight component is the unreliable heavyweight-overlap case - the UI would be hidden or flicker |
| Java2D's `sun.java2d.opengl` (the existing `openGl` key) | rejected | it accelerates AWT BLITS only and does not render the 3D scene; 4.2b already warns against conflating the two, and 4.2b's own `renderer` key is the one to build on |

⚠⚠ **It is the HYBRID requirement that decides this, not GL API taste.** The plan wants GL for the scene with chat, tabs and the minimap still software and composited on top (the RuneLite seam). Reading the scene back into the very buffer the software path already writes satisfies that by construction: the compositing step does not change, the present path does not change, and the `SceneRasterizer` seam Phase 4 built is exactly where the GL work goes. The alternative would mean introducing AWT/GL component interop purely to solve a problem the software path does not have.

**Measured, not argued.** `tools/gl-smoke` (`GlSmokeTest.java` + `run.ps1`, matching the repo's probe pattern; LWJGL 3.3.6 vendored to `deps/lwjgl3/`, 6 jars, 2.24 MB) proves on THIS machine:

```
GL_VERSION  : 3.3.0 NVIDIA 596.36
GL_RENDERER : NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3070/PCIe/SSE2
GLSL        : 3.30 NVIDIA via Cg compiler
window      : HIDDEN, standalone HWND - NOT setParent, NO AWT parent
shader program      : COMPILED + LINKED
FBO status          : COMPLETE (RGBA8 + depth24)
FBO inside triangle : r=255 g=0 b=0     (expect ~255,0,0)
FBO outside triangle: r=0 g=0 b=0       (expect ~0,0,0)
readback control    : an UNDRAWN pixel is NOT the drawn colour (good - readback discriminates)
depth test          : green@z=+0.3 then blue@z=-0.3 -> r=0 g=0 b=255   (NEARER one wins)
glGetError          : GL_NO_ERROR
GL_SMOKE_OK
```

⚠ **The control matters:** an undrawn pixel must NOT read back as the drawn colour, so a readback returning something constant cannot report success.

⚠⚠ **A FINDING THE SMOKE TEST PRODUCED BY FAILING FIRST, and it changes how 7.2 must be written.** The first version used fixed-function (`glMatrixMode`/`glBegin`) and **aborted the JVM**: GLFW at version 3.3 hands back a **CORE profile** here, where those functions do not exist. So the port **cannot be GL 2.x fixed-function - it must be shaders + VBOs.** That is not bad news: it is exactly the shape Phase 5 prepared. 5.1's read-only geometry accessors give model-local vertices and per-face attributes, and 5.2's `GpuFloatBuffer`/`GpuIntBuffer` are an upload path - so 5.1-5.3 turn out to be the right preparation for the arrangement this test forces.

⚠ **Dependency handling, and the convention it follows.** The jars are VENDORED into `deps/lwjgl3/` rather than resolved from Maven, because `deps/` is git-tracked already and carries LWJGL 2's jars and native DLLs - so the build stays offline and deterministic, as `build.gradle` deliberately mirrors `Compile.bat`/`Run.bat`. ⚠ **They are NOW on the main build classpath (7.2a, which also had to remove the colliding LWJGL 2 jar); before 7.2a nothing imported them, which is why `clean check installBin` was unchanged.** ⚠⚠ **They are deliberately STILL NOT on the HARNESS classpath:** the harness must stay hermetic and GPU-free, and with LWJGL absent `GlScene` takes its degradation path - which is exactly the robustness property worth testing headlessly. Successful context creation needs a real GPU and is `tools/gl-smoke`'s and the live gate's job.

⚠ **Noted now so it is not discovered late:** `Run.bat` launches plain `java` - Java 25 on this machine - where loading LWJGL's natives prints restricted-method warnings (`System::load`, `sun.misc.Unsafe`). The flag is `--enable-native-access=ALL-UNNAMED`. Harmless today, but the real client's runtime JVM is now a variable Phase 7 introduces, and `Run.bat` is the place it will surface.

⚠ **7.2's THREADING CONSTRAINT, established now so it is not rediscovered mid-implementation.** The client is `client extends RSApplet`, and `RSApplet implements Runnable` with `startRunnable(this, 1)` - so **the game loop runs on its OWN thread**, not the EDT. `paint(Graphics)` is the EDT's callback, and that is where today's present happens. The split is therefore:

| thread | what it does today | what GL would add |
|---|---|---|
| game thread (`run()`) | logic, rasterisation, writes the software framebuffer | **scene submission** (`SceneRasterizer.dispatch`) - so the GL context must be current HERE |
| EDT (`paint`) | presents the framebuffer via `Renderer` | nothing - the present stays software |

⚠ **So the natural arrangement is: create the GL context lazily ON THE GAME THREAD and keep every GL call there.** `SceneRasterizer.dispatch` already runs on that thread, the readback composites into the same buffer immediately after the scene pass, and the EDT never touches GL at all. That avoids the entire AWT/GL cross-thread problem rather than managing it - and it is the same reason the offscreen choice is safe: there is no shared HWND and no second event pump to synchronise with.

#### Phase 7.2a DONE AND LIVE-GATED (2026-10-06) - the GL arm exists, compiles, and the LWJGL 2/3 collision is gone at BOTH compile and runtime

**What is in place:** `ui/GlScene` (offscreen context - hidden GLFW window, FBO with RGBA8 colour + `DEPTH_COMPONENT24` depth, and defensive degradation via `Throwable` so a machine without GL falls back to software instead of crashing), `ui/GlSceneRenderer` (a `GpuRenderer.Implementation` that initialises the context on FIRST SCENE SUBMISSION, i.e. on the game thread, and DECLINES every operation until it can actually serve one so the client falls through to software), and `RendererConfig` now installs it for the existing `gl` key. ⚠ **Deliberately output-inert: the arm is opt-in and declining is the designed path until 7.2b draws something.**

⚠⚠ **THE BLOCKER, AND WHY IT WAS A CLASSPATH BUG RATHER THAN A CODE BUG.** `ui/GlScene.java` would not compile - `GL30.GL_DEPTH_COMPONENT24` was unresolved. The cause: **`deps/lwjgl.jar` (LWJGL 2) and `deps/lwjgl3/` (LWJGL 3) BOTH ship `org.lwjgl.opengl.*`, and LWJGL 2 sat ahead on the classpath**, so `GL30` resolved to LWJGL 2's class. Settled with `javap` rather than guessed:

| jar | `GL30` declaration | `GL_DEPTH_COMPONENT24` |
|---|---|---|
| LWJGL 3.3.6 | `extends GL21` | resolves BY INHERITANCE (`GL21 -> GL20 -> GL15 -> GL14`) |
| LWJGL 2 | no `GL14` ancestor | absent - hence the compile error |

⚠ **So the source line was correct and the classpath was wrong - which is why the fix is REMOVING THE JAR rather than re-spelling the constant.**

⚠ **The removal was gated on a measurement, not on an assumption:** a repo-wide import scan shows the ONLY `org.lwjgl` imports in `src/` are `ui/GlScene.java`'s, and `ui/LwjglPresent.java` - which SIX files still reference - is an inert stub with **zero** `org.lwjgl` imports. A jar that nothing compiles against is safe to take off the classpath.

⚠ **Removed from:** `build.gradle` (`compileOnly`/`runtimeOnly`, `compileHarness`, and the `run` task), `Run.bat`, `Record.bat`, `Compile.bat`, and all THREE probes' `run.ps1`. ⚠ **The LWJGL 2 `-Dorg.lwjgl.librarypath=deps\natives` flag went with it**, because `deps\natives` holds LWJGL 2's DLLs and LWJGL 3 extracts its own from its `-natives-windows.jar`s - leaving the property set would point LWJGL 3 at the wrong natives. **The jar stays on disk, off every classpath**; deleting it outright belongs to Phase 8.1, not here.

⚠ **A runtime hazard is removed rather than merely hidden:** LWJGL 2's static GL entry points route through an LWJGL 2 context that GLFW never initialises, so under the old ordering the GL arm would have failed at its first call even if it had compiled.

**THE LIVE GATE (2026-10-06) - one session cleared 7.2a AND 6.5.6, as the operator chose.** Capture `packet-tap-20261006-001600.log`, client exit **0**:

| check | result | bar |
|---|---|---|
| wire verdict | **COMPATIBLE** | - |
| login/load prefix | **595 / 600** | >= 590 |
| whole-stream LCS | **3935 / 4041 = 97.4%** | >= 75% |
| `compareCapture` exit | **0** | 0 |
| units / bytes | 4041 (3974 R / 67 W) / 54682 | - |

⚠ **The evidence that this matters for 7.2a specifically is in the console, not the packet log:** all four CursePack startup lines are present and UNCHANGED (`CursePack: ...`, `CursePack models loaded: 9/9`, `Injected curse seq/gfx from CursePack (frames remapped)`, `667Anims: ...`), and there is **ZERO `NoClassDefFoundError`/`UnsatisfiedLinkError`** - which is the exact failure mode that removing a jar from the classpath would produce. ⚠ **Proven to be a build that CONTAINED the change rather than asserted: `RendererConfig.class` built 00:05:29 vs capture 00:17:23, and `javap` shows `new ui/GlSceneRenderer` installed.** ⚠ **Save-state restored to pristine: the session dirtied all four `bob.txt` copies (the known side-effect), `git checkout` returned them to HEAD, and `git status` is bob-clean again.

⚠⚠ **THE GATE COULD NOT SEE THE THING 7.2a ACTUALLY CHANGED, AND I DID NOT LET IT STAND IN FOR THAT.** `renderer=software`, so the session **never touched `org.lwjgl`** - it would have passed identically if the collision came straight back. A compile is no better: it proves the constant resolves *for javac*, not which JAR the *running JVM* picks. So the claim was closed with a runtime check and made a standing tool.

**`tools/gl-classpath-probe`** (`ClasspathProbe.java` + `run.ps1`, matching the repo's probe pattern) loads the classes on the EXACT classpath `Run.bat`/`Record.bat` build and asserts the LWJGL 3 arrangement specifically - `GL30` must reach `GL14` by INHERITANCE and `GL_DEPTH_COMPONENT24` must read back:

```
OK    org.lwjgl.glfw.GLFW       <- deps/lwjgl3/lwjgl-glfw-3.3.6.jar

OK    GL30.GL_DEPTH_COMPONENT24 = 33190

      GL30 extends : org.lwjgl.opengl.GL21
CLASSPATH_PROBE_OK

That is the original defect reproducible on demand, so the probe is measurably non-vacuous. ⚠ **A live session could never have distinguished any of this** - which is why the probe exists rather than another capture.

#### Phase 7.2b-1 DONE AND GPU-VERIFIED (2026-10-06) - the GL arm can DRAW

**What is in place:** `ui/GlBatcher` - the GPU triangle sink. It accumulates triangles given in **screen-pixel space** (x/y with y growing DOWN, z in `[0,1]` where 0 is nearest) plus a packed ARGB colour per vertex, into **5.2's `GpuFloatBuffer`/`GpuIntBuffer`**; uploads ONE VBO pair per frame and draws them into **`GlScene`'s offscreen FBO** with a depth test; and `readInto` converts the readback into the client's own pixel format. `GlScene` gained one package-private `framebufferId()` so the batcher can bind the target.

**Why the input is screen pixels and not a matrix, and why that is the whole reason 7.2 is staged.** The two things the scene submits do not arrive in the same space. **Ground** is handed to the software rasteriser *already projected* (see `SceneRasterizer.Implementation#drawGroundTriangle`, "already projected to screen space"), so a GPU path receives pixels directly. **Models** arrive in *model space* with the camera as `camA..camD` sin/cos plus `dx/dy/dz`, and the projection is performed *inside* `Model.method443` - so a GPU path must **reproduce that projection itself**; it cannot read the result out of the software path, because those screen-space arrays are `static` scratch valid only mid-draw (**5.1's finding**). Keeping the batcher at "draw these pixel-space triangles" makes the one piece that must be *exactly* right - upload, depth test, readback format - separable and testable ahead of the projection work.

**The four things that would otherwise be wrong INVISIBLY, each asserted rather than assumed.** A packed ARGB int `0xAARRGGBB` reaches GL as bytes `b,g,r,a`, so the vertex shader swizzles `aCol.bgra` - drop it and **red and blue swap**, which looks plausible and is wrong. `glReadPixels` returns rows **bottom-up** while the client is top-down. The client's buffers are **`0x00RRGGBB`** (see `RSImageProducer`'s `DirectColorModel(32, 0xff0000, 0xff00, 0xff)`), so the alpha byte must not be invented as `0xFF` - inventing it changes every pixel the 4.1b framebuffer hash covers. And the depth test must let the **nearer** triangle win, not the last one drawn.

**The verification, and why it is not vacuous.** `tools/gl-batch-probe` drives the **production** `GlBatcher` (not a copy) on this machine's GPU, rendering into `GlScene`'s own hidden-window FBO - so it re-proves 7.1/7.2a every run. Measured: top triangle `0x00FF0000`; the same column at y=450 clear (`0x000000`, which is the Y-flip check); bottom triangle `0x0000FF`; depth overlap `0x00FF00FF` (the NEARER one); the interpolated RED→BLUE span `0x9B0064`; and every written pixel's alpha byte exactly 0. **The readback CONTROL** - an undrawn pixel is the clear colour - makes a readback returning one constant unable to report success. ⚠⚠ **And it is MUTATION-PROVED THREE WAYS, because a probe that cannot fail is not evidence:** a copy of `ui/GlBatcher` with the Y flip removed, the alpha byte set to `0xFF`, or the swizzle dropped is compiled into an **override directory placed AHEAD of `bin/`** on the classpath (so it shadows the real class through the *same* call path the client would use), and each makes the probe **FAIL on exactly its own assertion** - `MUTATION CAUGHT`, exit 0. The alpha mutation is the clearest: it prints `ALPHA FAIL: pixel 0xffff0000 has alpha byte 0xff but the client's buffers are 0x00RRGGBB`.

**Harness: 226 → 237/237 (11 new).** The GL-**absent** degradation path is pinned headlessly, because `deps/lwjgl3` is deliberately off the harness classpath and that is also a **real production case** (`Run.bat` controls that classpath): `ensure()`, `beginFrame`, `triangle`, `flush` and `readInto` must all **DECLINE rather than throw**, a null destination must decline, `ensure()` must be **idempotent** (a failure is not retried every frame), and the `0x00RRGGBB` format plus the RED-not-blue byte-order contract are asserted as **pure calculations with no GPU**.

⚠⚠ **WHAT THIS DELIBERATELY DOES NOT DO, stated plainly: nothing is drawing the scene.** The arm still declines every operation, so the client renders **exactly as before** and no live gate applies - a session would exercise byte-identical behaviour. **And the reason is a design fact that was MEASURED rather than assumed, because it forces the next step and it rules out the cheap-looking one:** the software path **interleaves ground and models per tile** inside `WorldController.method314`, so taking over **ground alone** would put GL's ground and the software's models in different buffers with **no correct merge order** - the seam's own doc already says the scene must go over **WHOLE or not at all**. So ground-first, which looks like the small next increment, is precisely the one thing that cannot be done safely. Also confirmed while measuring: the software scene writes into the **shared `static Framebuffer.pixels`** (reached through `DrawingArea`), which is where the GL readback must ultimately land for chat, tabs and the minimap to composite on top in software.

**7.2b-2, therefore, has three parts:** (1) a **model projection**; (2) route **both** seams (`SceneRasterizer.dispatch` and `dispatchGroundTriangle`) into the batcher and **accept only when the whole scene is being drawn**, so nothing falls through to software mid-frame; (3) `readInto` the result into `Framebuffer.pixels` at the point the scene pass **ends** and **before** the UI is drawn.

#### Phase 7.2b-2a DONE AND ORACLE-VERIFIED (2026-10-06) - the model projection matches the software path exactly

**What is in place:** `ui/GlModelProjection.project(...)` - model-local vertices (5.1's accessors) to screen pixels, reproducing `Model.method443` line for line: the orientation rotation (only when `orientation != 0`), the `dx/dy/dz` translation, the yaw about `camC`/`camD`, the pitch about `camA`/`camB`, the same `>> 16` fixed point throughout, and the same `OFFSCREEN` / `NEAR_PLANE` contract. The only change is SHAPE, not arithmetic: results go into caller-owned arrays instead of the model's `private static` scratch, so they are reusable and cannot be invalidated by the next model drawn.

**Why the function had to exist at all.** The ground seam hands a rasteriser screen pixels, but a model arrives in MODEL space with the camera as `camA..camD` sin/cos plus `dx/dy/dz`, and the projection happens INSIDE `method443`. Its outputs (`anIntArray1665/1666/1667`) are `private static` scratch shared by every model and valid only mid-draw - 5.1's finding - so a GPU path can neither read nor safely borrow them.

**⚠ Two details are contract, not style, and both are reproduced rather than tidied.** First, the near-plane case writes `OFFSCREEN` into X and **leaves Y UNTOUCHED** - so a caller must read the depth to know whether Y means anything, and "tidying" it by writing a Y would make the output disagree with the software path. Second, `k2` uses `camA`/`camB` while `j2` uses `camC`/`camD`; that asymmetry is in the original, and swapping it still produces a plausible-looking projection.

**The verification is an ORACLE, not a fixture - and that distinction is the point.** Hand-computing expected coordinates would only prove the implementation agrees with the same reading of `method443` that produced it, which is exactly the trap 5.3 hit when a self-consistent but WRONG texture-id contract survived every test that agreed with itself. So the harness drives the REAL `method443`, reads the REAL `anIntArray1665/1666/1667` it leaves behind, and requires **exact integer agreement**. The software path is the authority; this either agrees with it or is wrong. The oracle needs no production change either - the arrays are private, but the harness already had `readStatic`/`writeStatic` reflection for exactly this kind of reading.

**16 checks, two scenarios, harness 237 → 252/252, warnings exactly 100.** The second scenario uses `orientation = 3`, because the orientation rotation is a separate branch applied BEFORE the camera's and would go untested under `orientation = 0`.

**⚠ The CONTROL, which is the check that makes the rest meaningful.** `method443` culls on the model's bounding box BEFORE projecting, and a culled draw leaves the PREVIOUS model's data in the shared scratch. So the arrays are filled with a sentinel first and the test requires every vertex to be rewritten: without that, a culled draw would have the test compare a stale array against a fresh projection and could call the agreement a pass.

**⚠ MUTATION-PROVED TWICE, so it is measurably not blind.** Swapping the `camA`/`camB` terms in `k2` is CAUGHT by the depth check; swapping `camD`→`camA` in the yaw is CAUGHT by the screen-X check. Both reverted.

**⚠ AND TWO BLIND SPOTS IN MY OWN FIRST VERSION, found and fixed.** It used `camA = camB = camC = camD = 46341`, which CANNOT DISTINGUISH AN AXIS SWAP - the terms are interchangeable - and `dx = dy = dz = 0`, which forces `k2 = 0` so the `camA`/`camB` axis assignment was never exercised at all. The fixture now uses four DISTINCT camera terms and non-zero offsets, chosen to be asymmetric precisely so a swap moves a number. A third limitation is recorded rather than fixed: the non-vacuity control needs a LARGE delta (`camD` 50000 → 20000), because a one-unit change is absorbed by the `>> 16` truncation and yields byte-identical output - a `+1` mutation would "pass" while testing nothing, the same trap as 6.2's zero-delta origin fixture.

**⚠ ONE UNRESOLVED INTERACTION, RECORDED RATHER THAN BURIED.** Driving a real `method443` RASTERISES, and that disturbs shared state `realModelExportDrivesTheRasteriser` (5.3) depends on: run before it, that test failed with "the flat face's draw plotted pixels". Isolating the drawing area onto scratch **and** restoring the projected-vertex scratch arrays were both tried and NEITHER fixed it; the field was not identified. So the projection test is **ordered after** it, and the code comment states that this is a **WORKAROUND for an unidentified shared-state interaction, not a fix for one** - if a test between them starts failing for no visible reason, that interaction is the first suspect.

**⚠ NOT YET WIRED, and not claimed: `GlModelProjection` has zero call sites**, like 5.1's accessors and 5.2's buffers when they landed. The client behaves identically and no live gate applies to it.

**Still needed before models can be drawn, and NOT a given: the FACE step.** `method443` does more than project - it culls backfaces and lights/texture-maps via `method483`/`method484`/`method481`. Turning projected vertices into coloured triangles is the next piece of 7.2b-2b's work.

#### Phase 7.2b-2b DONE AND ORACLE-VERIFIED (2026-10-06) - the face step is IN, and the recon's culling polarity was CORRECTED

**Read, not inferred.** The whole face path was read so the next session starts from a recipe. What it turned out to be:

**`method443` -> `method483(flag, flag1, uid)`** does three things per face:
1. **Skip** the face if `anIntArray1637 != null && anIntArray1637[face] == -1`.
2. **Backface-cull** on the PROJECTED coordinates with a signed-area test: `(x0 - x1) * (y2 - y1) - (y0 - y1) * (x2 - x1) > 0`, using `anIntArray1665` for x and `anIntArray1666` for y. ⚠⚠ **CORRECTED WHEN IMPLEMENTED: this expression is the KEEP test, not the cull test.** The bucket write at `Model.java:2490` sits INSIDE `if (area > 0)`, so a **POSITIVE** area is FRONT-facing and is drawn - the recon recorded the formula correctly but not its direction, and implementing it the other way round culls every polygon the software draws while drawing every one it culls. The screen's y axis points down in this client, which is why the sign is the opposite of the usual mathematical convention.
3. **Bucket** the face by MEAN DEPTH, `(d0 + d1 + d2) / 3 + anInt1653`, into `anIntArrayArray1672`.

⚠⚠ **Point 3 is a PAINTER'S ALGORITHM, not a depth buffer - and that is the single most useful thing this reconnaissance bought.** Faces are drawn back-to-front by depth bucket, which is exactly the job a GL depth attachment does. So the GL face step does **not** have to reproduce the bucket ordering; it has to reproduce the **culling** and the **colours**, and supply correct per-vertex depth. Reproducing the sort would have been wasted work.

**`method484(face)`** then selects the primitive from the render type `anIntArray1637[face] & 3`:
- **0** -> `Texture.method374(y0,y1,y2, x0,x1,x2, cA,cB,cC)` - flat gouraud, per-corner colours from `anIntArray1634/1635/1636`.
- **1** -> `Texture.method376(..., modelIntArray3[anIntArray1634[face]])` - a single shaded colour via the model palette.
- **2** -> `Texture.method378(...)` - textured, sampling `anIntArray1640[face]`, with texture coordinates taken from the **camera-space** arrays `anIntArray1668/1669/1670` at the indices in `anIntArray1643/1644/1645[anIntArray1637[face] >> 2]`.
- **3** -> the textured variant.
- A face flagged in `aBooleanArray1664` (a vertex sitting at the near plane) goes to `method485` instead.

⚠⚠ **The colour model is `method481(int colour, int light, int renderType)`, and it is small enough to reproduce exactly:**
- textured (`renderType & 2`): return `127 - clamp(light, 0..127)` - a **SHADE**, not a colour;
- otherwise: return `(colour & 0xff80) + clamp((light * (colour & 0x7f)) >> 7, 2..126)` - the light multiplies the colour's own embedded shade and is recombined with its high bits.

The per-corner lit colours in `anIntArray1634/1635/1636` are produced from that by `method479`, which is why 5.3's rasteriser test supplies them directly - it isolates rasterisation from lighting.

**AS BUILT (2026-10-06).** The recon above is still the map of the path; this is what was then built and how it was proved.

**Two new pieces, one of them an interface and one of them the step.** `ui/TriangleSink` is a one-method interface (`triangle(x0,y0,z0,argb0, x1,y1,z1,argb1, x2,y2,z2,argb2)`) that exists so the pipeline does not have to know what a batcher is: `ui/GlBatcher` now `implements TriangleSink` for production, and the harness supplies a `RecordingSink` instead - **which is why the entire face step is verifiable with no GL context anywhere.** `ui/GlFacePipeline.emit(model, orientation, camA..camD, dx,dy,dz, centreX, centreY, sink)` takes exactly the arguments `method443` takes, projects through `GlModelProjection`, then walks the faces.

**Every face gets exactly ONE outcome, and the outcomes are explicit rather than dropped.** `SKIPPED` (render type -1), `NEEDS_CLIPPING` (a vertex at the near plane), `CULLED` (winding), `NEEDS_TEXTURE` (render type 2/3), `NO_COLOUR` (no corner colours, or a corner colour that does not resolve), `DRAWN`. ⚠ **That is deliberate: the census test can then tell "the software culled this face" from "this face is a case the batcher cannot do yet", and a face silently disappearing from the pipeline would be a `DRAWN`-count mismatch rather than an invisible absence.**

⚠⚠ **THE ORDER OF THE TESTS IS CONTRACT, NOT STYLE.** `method483` tests the NEAR PLANE **before** it tests the winding, and a face with an `OFFSCREEN` vertex is bucketed **without ever being winding-tested** - `method485` clips and draws it either way. Testing the winding first would silently drop faces the software draws, so the clipper branch comes first and the code says so.

**Depth is mapped and then checked against the software's own depths.** `depthToZ` maps `method443`'s camera-space depth onto the `[0,1]` the batcher's depth attachment expects, and the oracle asserts the property the depth test actually needs - **a deeper vertex maps to a LARGER z, compared pairwise against the SOFTWARE's depths** - rather than re-running `depthToZ` on its own input, which would only prove it agrees with itself.

**Geometry is compared exactly, not approximately.** For every drawn face the oracle requires each submitted corner to be the software's own `anIntArray1665/1666` value **at that face's own vertex index** - so a transposed corner or a face reading another face's vertices fails rather than passing with plausible numbers.

**⚠ What is deliberately NOT in this step, listed so it is not mistaken for done:** the clipper (`method485`), texture coordinates (the camera-space `anIntArray1668/1669/1670` at the `>> 2` indices), the model-palette shade path (`method376`, `modelIntArray3`), and **lighting** - `method479`/`method481` are not reproduced, because the per-corner colours are consumed exactly as the software left them, which is the same isolation choice 5.3's rasteriser test made. **Zero call sites**, like `GlModelProjection` before it, so no live gate applies and the client behaves identically. ⚠ **RE-SCOPED BY 7.2b-2c: the lighting item above is NOT owed work** - `method479` runs upstream on the model-build path so the seam receives already-lit colours, and the only genuinely `method481`-shaped remainder is the SHADE regime carried by the TEXTURED types, which is why those are routed to `NEEDS_TEXTURE` and get settled with the texture path rather than before it.

**The order-independence probe.** The 7.2b-2a section below records an "unidentified shared-state interaction" that forced the projection test to run AFTER 5.3's rasteriser test; that is now identified (the `private static` `aBooleanArray1664`/`1663` left set by a real `method443`) and fixed in the rasteriser test itself. ⚠ **Verified here by moving the face and projection tests AHEAD of it and re-running: 281/281, with "the flat face's draw plotted pixels" passing** - so the workaround is gone rather than merely commented away.

**THE NO-REGRESSION LIVE GATE (2026-10-06 01:04) - the full standing route, on this tree.** Capture `packet-tap-20261006-010438.log`, client exit **0**:

| check | result | bar |
|---|---|---|
| wire verdict | **COMPATIBLE** | - |
| login/load prefix | **595 / 600** | >= 590 |
| whole-stream LCS | **3929 / 3998 = 98.3%** | >= 75% |
| `compareCapture` exit | **0** | 0 |
| units / bytes | 3998 (3956 R / 42 W) / 47485 | - |

⚠ **The route is what makes this worth recording**: login, walk, one attack, a ground-item pickup, the 667 content (Nex / curse / Blood reaver), and the Rock Crab area where the noted-item crash happened - and the client exited 0, so checklist items 2, 4, 5 and 6 are covered rather than assumed. All four CursePack startup lines are present and unchanged, and the floor/object maps load normally.

⚠⚠ **AND THE HONEST LIMIT, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT OF CALLING IT A NO-REGRESSION GATE: `renderer=software` and this class has ZERO CALL SITES, so nothing here was executed.** `org.lwjgl` was never loaded and no GL pixel was produced. What it proves is that a tree CONTAINING these classes still boots, plays and frames packets exactly as before - which is worth having, and is not the same as proving the classes work. Their proof is the oracle above; their functional proof has to wait for the wiring.

#### Phase 7.2b-2c DONE AND ORACLE-VERIFIED (2026-10-06) - the per-render-type colour semantics, and the premise correction that produced them

**This began as the shade-model step 7.2b-2b listed, and ended as a bug fix - because reconnaissance said the shade model is not ours to write at all.** Recorded in that order, because the correction is the larger half.

**1. WHY THE SHADE MODEL IS NOT OWED. The call graph, not a preference.**

`method479` is not on the DRAW path, it is on the model-BUILD path, and every producer of a scene model calls it:

| getter | line |
|---|---|
| `ObjectDef.java` | 595 |
| `EntityDef.java` | 307 |
| `ItemDef.java` | 506 |
| `Player.java` | 300 |
| `NPC.java` | 86 |
| `Animable_Sub3.java` | 61 |
| `Animable_Sub4.java` | 54 |

and the software's own per-face draw, `Model.method484`, indexes **the same** `anIntArray1634/1635/1636` the GL pipeline indexes. The seam (`method443`) is downstream of both. **So the GL path already receives lighting.** Re-deriving `method479`/`method481` would be dead code with zero call sites **and** a double-shade risk - the same "reproducing the sort would have been wasted work" call this plan already made once for the depth buckets.

**2. WHAT READING IT FOUND INSTEAD: `method479` does not write all three corner slots.**

`method484` reads only the slot its own primitive needs, so the software never notices. A triangle emitter must read all three. Rather than reason about which slots are written, this was **measured with sentinels** - each slot pre-filled with a value no legal `method481` output can produce, so "untouched" cannot be mistaken for "shaded to zero" - driving the **REAL** `method479` over a mixed-type fixture:

```
Shade model: render-type-0 face 0 writes ALL THREE corners    (wroteA=true wroteB=true wroteC=true)
Shade model: render-type-1 face 1 writes ONLY anIntArray1634  (wroteA=true wroteB=false wroteC=false)
```

⚠⚠ **So the pipeline had been emitting every render-type-1 face with corner A from `1634` and corners B and C read from two slots `method479` never touches - zeros on a freshly allocated model, i.e. `palette[0]`. Two dark corners the software never draws.** Silent, because nothing is wired yet; it would have surfaced as "some models have black corners" long after the wiring step.

**3. AND A SECOND DIVERGENCE THAT HIDES BEHIND FOG: the two primitives fade in OPPOSITE ORDERS.**

- `Texture.method374` (type 0, gouraud): fades the colour **CODE** first (`Fog.fadeHsl`) and lets the rasteriser look the palette up per interpolated code.
- `Texture.method376` (type 1, flat): looks the palette up **FIRST**, then fades the resulting **RGB** (`Fog.applyFlat`).

With fog off both collapse to `palette[code]` - which is precisely why this survives a reading. ⚠ **So the oracle runs with fog ON and asserts the two orders DISAGREE before it trusts their agreement** (`0xa87fb0` gouraud vs `0x402840` flat on the same input) - a non-vacuity check, so the test cannot pass by the two paths coinciding.

**4. A THIRD FINDING, STRUCTURAL: `Fog.applyFlat` reads the static `Fog.sceneDepth`, and the seam runs BEFORE `method443` assigns it.** `Model.java:2337` dispatches to the seam; `:2346` assigns `Fog.sceneDepth`. So at seam time the field still holds the **previous** model's depth. Hence a depth-explicit overload, with the old shape delegating - **one owner of the fog curve, mirroring `Fog.fadeHsl`:**

```java
public static int applyFlat(int rgb) {
    return applyFlatAt(rgb, sceneDepth);
}

public static int applyFlatAt(int rgb, int depth) {
    if (depth <= 50 || client.fogStrength <= 0) {
        return rgb;
    }
    return fadeRgb(rgb, depth);
}
```

**5. AS BUILT.** `ui/GlFacePipeline.resolveFlatColour(code, sceneDepth)` (palette, THEN fade the RGB) plus a flat emit branch keyed on `renderTypes[face] & 1 != 0`, which is what `method484` does for that type: all three vertices take the single flat colour from `colourA[face]`. Render type 0 keeps the three-corner gouraud path unchanged. **Zero call sites**, so the client is byte-for-byte the same as the gated tree.

**6. PROVED, AND MUTATION-PROVED TWICE - WITH THE FIRST ATTEMPT WRONG, WHICH IS WORTH KEEPING.**

| check | result |
|---|---|
| harness | **292 / 292** (281 -> 292, 11 new) |
| warnings | exactly **100** (pinned) |
| `clean check installBin` | BUILD SUCCESSFUL |
| lints | clean |
| `reportStaleClasses` | **212** unchanged |
| `verifyObjectCollisionSizes` | in sync |

⚠⚠ **My first mutation was wired `MUTATION_DISABLE_FLAT && ...`, which leaves the branch ENABLED when set true - so the run "passed" while testing nothing, and it looked like a pass.** With the polarity fixed:
- disabling the flat branch fails the emit check with `ffefbdab / ff2244aa / ff66ccfe` - **three different corners, i.e. the pre-fix bug reproduced on demand**;
- swapping `resolveFlatColour` to the corner order fails **both** the colour oracle and the non-vacuity check (`402840 vs 402840`).
⚠ **The switch was then REMOVED from `src/` rather than shipped: this repo mutates copies, and a production switch that must stay false is dead code.**

**⚠ What is NOT claimed: any GL output.** Nothing here executed - no GL context, no live gate applies, and that is the honest limit of a class with no call sites.

#### Phase 7.2b-2d DONE AND ORACLE-VERIFIED (2026-10-06) - the near-plane clipper, and the first test in this phase that passed while proving nothing

**`ui/GlClipper` reproduces `Model.method485` as a pure function, and `NEEDS_CLIPPING` stops being the outcome that blocks every model near the camera.**

**THE DESIGN DECISION, first, because the alternative looks obviously better and is wrong.** A face with a vertex at or behind the near plane has NO projected vertices, so there is nothing to submit. The obvious move is to submit the uncut triangle with correct camera-space depths and let the GL near plane cut it. **Rejected on arithmetic:** the batcher receives x/y **already divided by camera Z**, so clipping in that space interpolates the projected coordinates AFFINELY, whereas `method485` interpolates in CAMERA space and projects the intersection exactly at depth 50 - and `lerp(camX*512/camZ)` is not `camX(t)*512/50`. The two disagree slightly along every clipped edge, i.e. exactly where this step exists to be faithful. ⚠ **Mutation-proved: dividing `clippedScreen` by the wrong plane fails 9 checks.**

**WHAT WAS BUILT.**
- `ui/GlModelProjection` now also emits the **camera-space pair** (`anIntArray1668/1669`). Nothing else needed it; the clipper cannot work without it, and the projection is the only pure source. ⚠ The projection emits it for EVERY vertex, whereas `method443` writes it only `if (flag || anInt1642 > 0)`; that difference is safe because inside `method443` those slots are read only by `method485` (reachable only when `flag` was true) and by the textured path.
- `ui/GlClipper.clip(...)` - the rebuild, as a pure function: 0, 3 or 4 points, in `method485`'s literal emission order, into caller-owned arrays.
- `ui/GlFacePipeline` - the `CLIPPED` outcome, a `clipped()` accessor, and a clip branch that rebuilds the face and draws it the way the software does: the quad split into (0,1,2)+(0,2,3), the winding tested on the REBUILT polygon rather than the original, and render type 1 taking the face's ORIGINAL slot-A colour rather than the interpolated clip colours.

⚠ **THE CLIPPED POLYGON MIXES TWO KINDS OF POINT, SO A SINGLE DEPTH IS WRONG.** A front vertex keeps its own projected position and its own depth; an intersection sits on the plane and has depth `50 - sceneDepth` whatever the edge. The first draft had no per-point depth at all - caught while implementing, not after - and one shared value would have flattened every front corner onto the plane and lost the depth ordering between one clipped face and the next.

**THE ORACLE: the software's own rebuilt polygon, with the point COUNT read rather than assumed.** `method485` writes its polygon into `anIntArray1678/1679/1680`, which are never cleared between faces - so they are sentinel-filled first and the number of points is the run of non-sentinel entries. ⚠ **Each case is ONE face in ONE model**, because a second cut face would overwrite the scratch and the software cannot be asked which polygon belonged to which face:

| configuration | points | drawn as |
|---|---|---|
| one vertex behind | **4** | a QUAD, split into 2 triangles |
| two vertices behind | **3** | a TRIANGLE, from vertices 0 and 1 |
| front vertex first | **3** | a TRIANGLE, from the third emission branch |
| all three behind | **0** | the empty polygon - nothing drawn |

All four match **point for point in screen X, screen Y and the interpolated colour CODE**, and the submitted corners match the software's polygon corner for corner with each point's interpolated colour resolved.

⚠⚠ **AND THIS IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING: THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS TEST PASSED WHILE THE ENTIRE EMISSION PATH WENT UNTESTED.** All four configurations produced a **back-facing** rebuild, so `expectedTriangles` was **0 everywhere** and every triangle-level check agreed on **nothing**. A green run proving nothing - the exact failure mode this plan keeps writing rules about, committed here anyway. The cause is that this client's screen Y points **DOWN**, so the emission order these cases produce needs **negative-leading X** to be front-facing. Fixed by choosing the winding deliberately AND asserting the totals: **4 triangles = quad(2) + triangle(1) + triangle(1)**, so a configuration that silently stops drawing fails loudly.

⚠⚠ **A SECOND ONE, in the depth check, and it is the same shape of mistake.** The first version compared the clipper's plane depth against **itself**, so a clipper that forgot to subtract the scene depth passed. It now reconstructs the absolute value as **`depth + sceneDepth == NEAR_PLANE`**; the mutation that drops the subtraction fails 6 checks.

**A STRONGER ORACLE IN THE CENSUS TEST, AND A HOLE CLOSED.** The clip-flag comparison now reads `pipeline.clipped()` instead of an outcome - which matters, because a cut TEXTURED face reports `NEEDS_TEXTURE`, so an outcome could never be evidence about the near plane. ⚠ **And that comparison was quietly vacuous before: the census camera sits 700 units back "so no face is routed to the clipper by accident", making all-false == all-false the only thing tested.** It is now pinned by an explicit assertion, so the camera cannot drift into clipping and silently invalidate the one-triangle-per-drawn-face mapping the geometry checks depend on.

**⚠ `NEEDS_CLIPPING` SURVIVES BUT MEANS SOMETHING ELSE.** It no longer means "cut face, cannot draw" - that is handled. It now means "the clipper could not be applied": `method485` divides through a 2048-entry reciprocal table indexed unchecked, so a cut edge spanning 2048 camera-space units **throws**, and because `method443` wraps the whole draw in a bare `try/catch` that does not crash - it **silently ABORTS the model mid-draw** and leaves the frame part-rasterised. Not worth reproducing; counted instead. **`allRepresentable()`'s expression is unchanged and its meaning is now narrower: cut faces are representable, so TEXTURES are the last thing standing between this pipeline and the wiring step. ⚠ CORRECTED BY 7.2b-2e: the RESOLUTION of a textured face is now done and oracle-verified too - what remains is not a representation gap but a BATCHER gap, namely that `GlBatcher` has no atlas or sampler.**

**MUTATION-PROVED THREE TIMES**, each reverted: a wrong-edge colour interpolation (4 checks fail), a wrong clip plane (9 checks fail), a forgotten scene-depth subtraction (6 checks fail).

**GATES:** harness **292 -> 359** (67 new), warnings **exactly 100**, `clean check installBin` **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**, lints clean, `reportStaleClasses` **212** unchanged, `verifyObjectCollisionSizes` **in sync**.

**⚠ NOT CLAIMED: any GL output.** Zero call sites, so the client is byte-for-byte the tree the 2026-10-06 01:04 golden master gate already passed.

**Next: the texture path (`method378`, types 2 and 3 - where `method481`'s SHADE regime actually lives), then the scene wiring.**
