#### Phase 7.2b-2e DONE AND ORACLE-VERIFIED (2026-10-06) - the textured faces, and the two facts about them that are not guessable

**The pipeline can now DESCRIBE every face the software draws, textured ones included. What is still missing is only a SAMPLER - a batcher capability, not a resolution problem - so `NEEDS_TEXTURE` no longer means "this face cannot be represented" and now means "this sink has no texture support".**

**WHAT WAS BUILT.**
- `ui/TriangleSink` gained `supportsTextures()` and `textured(...)`: three corners of (projected x/y/z, texture u/v/w, shade code), plus the texture id - the fields `Model.method484` passes to `Texture.method378`, and nothing more.
- `ui/GlBatcher` answers `false`/`false`, because it owns no atlas and no sampler yet. That is a deliberate, recorded gap rather than an oversight.
- `ui/GlFacePipeline.submitTextured(...)` resolves the inputs, and `TEXTURED = 7` is the new outcome (`OUTCOME_COUNT = 8`).

**⚠⚠ TWO FACTS READ OUT OF `method484` RATHER THAN ASSUMED - and both are the kind that a plausible-looking implementation gets wrong.**

**1. The texture ID lives in the face's COLOUR slot (`anIntArray1640`), not in the render-type word.** The render-type word says only that the face IS textured (`& 2`) and WHICH coordinate triple to use (`>> 2`). Reading the id out of the type word is exactly what mutation (D) does, and it fails **3 checks**.

**2. The u/v/w triple is CAMERA-SPACE, not projected screen space.** The texture-coordinate vertex contributes `vertexCamX` / `vertexCamY`, and `w` is `depth + sceneDepth`. This matters because `Texture.method379` divides through `w` **per pixel**: the projected coordinates have ALREADY been divided by depth, so passing them would interpolate affinely and disagree along every textured surface, in exactly the region the step exists to make faithful. Mutation (A) reads the coordinates at the face's own corner instead of the texture-coordinate vertex and fails the check, named: `face 1 corner 0 uvw (pipeline 130.0,20.0,700.0 expected 10,20,700)`.

**THE INDIRECTION IS REAL, AND THE FIXTURE PROVES IT IS EXERCISED.** `renderType >> 2` indexes `anIntArray1643/1644/1645`, which name three vertices that are **not** the face's own corners - so the texture plane is a separate triangle entirely. The fixture uses a **non-zero** index and asserts the referenced vertices differ from the face's corners, because an index of `0` would have made the zero case pass for free. Mutation (C) hardcodes index `0` and fails with `expected 10,140,700`.

**THE PER-TYPE SPLIT, TOTALLED RATHER THAN WALKED.** Render type 3 (flat textured) hands slot A to all three corners; render type 2 (gouraud textured) hands the three distinct slots. The fixture draws one face from EACH reversed pair and asserts the totals - `textured=2 gouraud=1 flat=1` - so a type that silently stops resolving cannot pass. Mutation (B) lets type 3 read slots B and C and fails, named: `face 3 corner 1 shade 57 expected 56`.

**⚠⚠ ONE DELIBERATE ASYMMETRY, recorded because a later reader will want to "fix" it.** The texture-coordinate index and the vertices it names ARE bounds-checked, and an unusable one is `NEEDS_TEXTURE` - that is a property of the MODEL, so the pipeline can decide it. The texture ID is **NOT** bounded here: it is a GLOBAL cache index that a SINK resolves, so checking it against the model's own texture count would be a plausible-looking check that rejects valid faces. The sink is the one that gets to decline. Mutation (E) makes the pipeline claim `TEXTURED` when the sink answered `false` - i.e. report a draw that never happened - and fails the one honesty check that stops `allRepresentable()` from being a lie.

**THE SHADE REGIME, SETTLED HERE RATHER THAN EARLIER.** For `renderType & 2` the corner slot holds `127 - clamp(light)`: a SHADE, not a colour code. `submitTextured` therefore passes the RAW code through for the sink to shade with, and does NOT palette it - which is why textured faces could not be folded into the colour path and needed their own resolution step.

**THE ORACLE.** Non-vacuity is asserted FIRST - the software draw really projected, one textured triangle per textured outcome, one face per reversed pair, a non-zero index indirection - and then every attribute is compared against the software's own scratch (`anIntArray1665..1670`), the corner shades, and the colour-slot id. A sink without texture support is asserted to get `NEEDS_TEXTURE` rather than a silent drop.

**MUTATION-PROVED FIVE TIMES**, each reverted and each verified byte-exact against the tree afterwards:

| mutation | fails |
|---|---|
| coordinate read at the face's own corner | 1 check, named |
| type 3 reading slots B and C | 1 check, named |
| the `>> 2` indirection ignored | 1 check, named |
| texture id taken from the type word | 3 checks |
| `TEXTURED` claimed while the sink answered `false` | 1 check |

**GATES:** harness **359 -> 369** (10 new), warnings **exactly 100**, `clean check installBin` **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**, `ReadLints` clean.

**⚠ NOT CLAIMED: any GL output.** `GlBatcher.supportsTextures()` is still `false`, so on the production path a textured face reports `NEEDS_TEXTURE` and nothing samples anything. Zero call sites, so the client is byte-for-byte the tree the 2026-10-06 01:04 golden master gate already passed.

**Next: give `GlBatcher` real texture support - an atlas plus a sampler - so `TEXTURED` becomes reachable on the production path, then the scene rasteriser seam wiring and the batcher take-over.**

---

#### Phase 7.2b-2f DONE AND MEASURED (2026-10-06) - the texture shade policy, settled by driving the software instead of reading it

**⚠ FIRST, A DECISION THE OPERATOR MADE, because it shapes everything below:** asked what the GL textured path should aim for, the operator chose **FAITHFUL** - the GL path must LOOK LIKE the software renderer (same 64/128-square textures, same camera-space `u/w, v/w` coordinates, `NEAREST` sampling, and the software's own shade semantics), rather than a "quality mode" with linear filtering and a continuous shade. **The reason this matters beyond taste: only the faithful choice leaves an ORACLE.** A deliberately-better-looking GL path diverges from software by construction, so correctness could be judged only by eye - which is the opposite of how every other step in this phase was verified. Recorded as a decision so a later reader cannot mistake the divergence for an oversight.

**WHAT WENT IN (all pure or read-only; no GL yet):**
- **`ui/GlTextures`** (new): `texelIndex(uv, size)`, `brightnessBlock(shade)`, `extraShift(shade)`, `blockColour(block0, block)`, `shade(block0, shade[, sceneDepth])`, `available(id)`. The fog overload **REUSES `Fog.fadeHsl`** instead of re-deriving the curve, following `Fog.applyFlatAt`'s existing single-owner rule.
- **`model/Texture`**: read-only `hasTexture(id)` / `textureSize(id)` / `texturePixels(id)`. ⚠ `texturePixels` returns **the array `method371` builds and the rasteriser samples** - block 0 of `size * size` - so the texture the GPU uploads is the texture the software reads, not a re-decode. `unpack` swallows per-texture failures, so `hasTexture` is a real question, not a formality.
- **`ClientHarness`**: a new oracle, `glTexturesMatchTheSoftwareShadeBlocks`.

**⚠⚠ THE MEASUREMENT. The whole uncertainty was HOW a shade code becomes brightness, and reading could not settle it.** `method379` folds part of the shade into the texture ARRAY INDEX (`i += j1 & 0x600000`) and, in the decompiled fixed-point arithmetic, that constant reads as mis-scaled by orders of magnitude - added to a value in the tens of thousands, where the texture array is 16384 long. **A reader would conclude the line was a decompiler artifact.** The resolution is that `0x600000 >> 7 == 3 * 16384`, i.e. exactly the third of `method371`'s four blocks.

**So it was measured instead.** The four blocks of a parked texture are filled with four KNOWN label colours, the real `Texture.method378` is driven over all 128 shade codes with the shade constant across the triangle, and the painted pixel NAMES the block and the shift - nothing in the test restates the arithmetic. **Observed:**

```
shade   0-15 -> block 0 shift 0     shade  64-79 -> block 0 shift 1
shade  16-31 -> block 1 shift 0     shade  80-95 -> block 1 shift 1
shade  32-47 -> block 2 shift 0     shade  96-111 -> block 2 shift 1
shade  48-63 -> block 3 shift 0     shade 112-127 -> block 3 shift 1
```

**⇒ `brightness block = (shade >> 4) & 3`, `extra shift = shade >> 6`, and bits 0-3 never matter.**

**⚠ TWO MORE FACTS THE SAME WORK SURFACED, both recorded because they are easy to get wrong later:**
1. **The block STRIDE depends on `lowMem`** - 4096 apart in 64-square mode and 16384 apart otherwise - so a test or an uploader that hardcodes one is right only by accident. ⚠ **`client.main` calls `setHighMem()`, so the 128-square/16384 path is what actually runs in play, while the harness exercises `lowMem` (its default).**
2. **Fog changes WHICH BLOCK a textured face samples**, because `method378` fades the shade through `Fog.fadeHsl` BEFORE the block is derived, and a bare shade `0..127` presents to `fadeHsl` as hue 0 / sat 0 / luminance `shade` - which the fade pulls toward 68. Skip it and fogged textured faces come out at the wrong brightness, in a way a fog-free oracle would never catch.

**VACUITY GUARDS, asserted BEFORE any conclusion** (this phase has produced a passing-but-worthless test twice already): the four labels stay distinct under both shifts; every painted pixel agrees with every other; and the sweep must actually reach **all four blocks AND both shifts**. Without the last one, "the policy matches" would be a statement about a handful of shade codes.

**MUTATION-PROVED TWICE**, each reverted and each verified byte-exact against the tree afterwards: block shift `>> 4` -> `>> 5` fails 1 check (`first mismatch at shade 16: software block 1, GlTextures 0`); extra shift `>> 6` -> `>> 5` fails 1 check.

**GATES:** harness **369 -> 376** (7 new), warnings **exactly 100**, `clean check installBin` **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**, `ReadLints` clean.

**⚠ NOT CLAIMED - the two gaps, stated rather than glossed:**
1. **The block ARITHMETIC (`k - (k >>> n) & 0xf8f8ff`) is a VERBATIM TRANSCRIPTION and is not yet oracle-checked against the software's own built blocks.** Checking it needs `method371` to actually BUILD, which needs a real `Background` - i.e. a synthetic sprite in the client's real format, which this session did not craft. The masking is load-bearing (the subtraction borrows ACROSS channels and the mask repairs it), so doing the "obvious" per-channel darkening would be wrong, and it is precisely the kind of claim that should not rest on transcription alone.
2. **Still no GL output.** No atlas upload, no sampler, no UV attribute; `GlBatcher.supportsTextures()` is still `false` and zero call sites exist, so the client remains byte-for-byte the tree the golden master gate passed.

**Next: the other half of (A)** - upload the 51 textures into `GlBatcher` as a `GL_TEXTURE_2D_ARRAY`, add the UV and layer attributes, transcribe `ui/GlTextures` into the fragment shader (arithmetic on the PACKED int), sample `NEAREST`/`CLAMP_TO_EDGE`, flip `supportsTextures()` to `true`, and extend `tools/gl-batch-probe` with a textured case - **because the harness cannot see the shader at all, LWJGL being deliberately absent there.**

---

#### Phase 7.2b-2g DONE AND GPU-VERIFIED (2026-10-06) - the GL texture path, and the two bugs that looked exactly like working code

**This is the other half of (A). `GlBatcher.supportsTextures()` is `true`, a textured triangle reaches the GPU, and the fragment shader samples the software rasteriser's own texels.**

**WHAT WENT IN:**

- **`ui/GlBatcher` - the storage.** A `GL_TEXTURE_2D_ARRAY` of `GlTextures.TEXTURE_COUNT` (51) layers, each `GlTextures.layerSize()` square (64 or 128 by `Texture.lowMem`), format `RGBA8UI`. ⚠ **One layer per id rather than a packed atlas, and that is not laziness:** texture ids are already dense in `0..50`, so the layer index IS the id and there is no UV remapping, no padding and no bleed between neighbours; and an array requires every layer to be the same size, which holds because `method371` resamples them all to the same square. ⚠ **The texels are lifted, not re-derived:** each layer is uploaded from `Texture.texturePixels(id)`, i.e. block 0 of the array `method371` builds and `method379` samples - so `createAtlas` only reorders bytes, and the DATA path is faithful by construction rather than by re-derivation.
- **`ui/GlBatcher` - the geometry.** Two new vertex attributes: `aUvW` (location 2, three floats, the camera-space triple) and `aTex` (location 3, two floats: `shade` and `layer`). ⚠ **Both kinds of triangle still go down ONE program and ONE draw call** - an untextured vertex carries `layer = -1` and the fragment shader picks the branch - which is not only fewer state changes but is why the two paths cannot drift: same vertex format, same depth handling, same readback.
- **`ui/GlBatcher` - the shader.** The darkening is **transcribed onto the PACKED integer** (`uint rgb`, then `rgb - (rgb >> n)` masked with `0xF8F8FF`, then `>> uint(shade >> 6)`), because `method371`'s subtraction BORROWS ACROSS CHANNELS and the mask repairs it - darkening each channel in float RGB is the obvious thing to write and gives a different, subtly wrong colour. An integer texture is what makes the transcription possible at all: the texel arrives as exact bytes with no filtering or colour-space conversion in the way.
- **`ui/TriangleSink` / `ui/GlFacePipeline`**: three doc blocks corrected. They still described the production sink as having "no atlas, no UV attribute and no sampler", which stopped being true in this step.

**⚠⚠ THE INTERPOLATION DECISION, recorded because it is the one place a reader will assume the opposite.** `method379` divides by `w` per PIXEL, and the software's `u`, `v` and `w` ramps are **demonstrably AFFINE in screen space** - their per-pixel increments are constants (`l1 += k2`), which is readable off the decompiled loop. So the faithful arrangement is the RAW camera-space triple as a `noperspective` varying, **divided in the fragment shader**, which reproduces the software's `sum(l*u) / sum(l*w)` exactly. ⚠ **Using GL's default perspective correction would interpolate `u/w` instead and disagree along every textured surface** - the same class of error the clipper refuses to make, in the same direction. This is pinned by the probe: the mapping triangle's `w` is deliberately NOT constant (1, 4, 2), so the divide is load-bearing rather than a no-op, and removing it is a mutation.

**⚠⚠ TWO REAL BUGS, both of which produced a picture rather than a crash, and both now impossible to reintroduce silently:**

1. **`texture()` with an INTEGER sampler still takes NORMALISED coordinates.** Passing a texel index `(13, 9, layer)` through it normalises a second time, clamps to the edge, and **every textured fragment silently samples the bottom-right texel**. The fix is `texelFetch`, the unnormalised fetch. ⚠ **This was caught only because the probe's mapping texture encodes each texel's own `(col, row)`**, so "which texel was read" is readable directly off the painted pixel instead of being inferred from "does it look like a texture".
2. **`int(-1.0f + 0.5f)` is `0`, not `-1`.** The layer attribute was converted with a "safe-looking" rounding, which maps the untextured sentinel `-1` onto layer `0` - so **every untextured triangle took the sampler branch, sampled an empty layer and was discarded, while the textured geometry kept drawing happily.** The whole flat scene vanished. The conversion is now a plain truncation, which is exact for a texture id and correct for the sentinel; the hazard is recorded at the constant that defines it.

**⚠ A THIRD DECISION THAT IS A KNOWN, BOUNDED DIVERGENCE RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT: transparency is `discard` when the SHADED texel is zero.** The software skips a texel when `texel >>> shift == 0`, and writes unconditionally when the texture has no zero texels at all (`aBoolean1463`) - so testing the SHIFTED value is the closer of the two available tests, but a texture with NO zero texels and a texel of exactly `0x000001` and a non-zero shift would be drawn by the software as black and discarded here. It requires blue `== 1` on a mask-stable texel, so it is recorded rather than plumbed: the exact form needs `aBooleanArray1475` exposed per texture.

**⚠ The atlas is built ONCE, in its OWN guard, and failure is contained rather than fatal.** A driver that refuses integer 3D textures loses the texture path (`supportsTextures()` then answers `false` honestly, and the pipeline counts those faces as `NEEDS_TEXTURE`) while the flat path keeps drawing. ⚠ **Consequence worth knowing: `layerSize` is read at build time, so a `Texture.lowMem` change afterwards leaves the atlas at its old side** - internally consistent (the shader's `uLayerSize` uniform stays in step with the uploaded layers, so nothing corrupts), but the detail level would not follow. `client.main` calls `setHighMem()` before any GL init, so this does not arise in play.

**VERIFICATION - and the shader is the interesting half, because the harness CANNOT see it.** LWJGL is deliberately off the harness classpath, so the GLSL is invisible to every headless test. The chain is therefore two-step, and both halves are checked:

- **The harness pins the POLICY against the software** (7.2b-2f, unchanged): `GlTextures` vs the real `Texture.method378` over all 128 shade codes.
- **The GPU probe pins the SHADER against the policy**: `tools/gl-batch-probe` now parks three synthetic textures via reflection and drives the production `GlBatcher` on a real GPU. ⚠ **The expected values are COMPUTED, not hardcoded** - the shade sweep compares the painted pixel against `GlTextures.shade(...)` for four shade codes spanning all four blocks and both shifts, and the mapping check derives barycentric weights from the triangle's own screen corners, interpolates `u/v/w`, divides, floors and clamps exactly as `GlTextures.texelIndex` does. So the probe checks a MAPPING rather than restating a number.
- ⚠ **The cutout check requires the two halves to DISAGREE** (a zero texel must leave the clear colour, a solid one must not): "something was drawn" would pass even with the cutout ignored.
- ⚠ **`Texture.hasTexture` was corrected in this step to match `method371` rather than to be conservative**: `method371` returns the LOADED slot immediately when it is non-null and only otherwise builds from the `Background`, so "obtainable" means EITHER slot is present. Asking only about the `Background` would report a usable texture as missing.

**MUTATION-PROVED SEVEN TIMES, each reverted and each verified byte-exact against the tree afterwards:** `flip` (drop the readback Y flip), `alpha` (invent an opaque alpha byte), `swizzle` (drop `aCol.bgra`), `block` (`shade >> 4` -> `shade >> 5` in GLSL), `layer` (ignore the layer attribute), `divide` (drop the per-pixel UV divide), `rowcol` (read the texel from the transpose). All seven are **CAUGHT**.

**GATES:** harness **376/376** unchanged, warnings **exactly 100**, `clean check installBin` **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**, `ReadLints` clean, probe `GL_BATCH_OK` on the live GPU (`atlas 51 layers of 64x64, NEAREST, integer`).

**⚠ NOT CLAIMED - stated rather than glossed:**
1. **No live client, and none is possible yet.** `GlBatcher` still has ZERO production call sites - every reference in `src/` is a javadoc `{@link}` - so nothing reaches it from `client.java` and the client remains byte-for-byte the tree the 2026-10-06 01:04 golden master gate passed. The GPU probe is a real GPU and the production class, but it is not the game loop.
2. **The block ARITHMETIC is still not oracle-checked against the software's own BUILT blocks** (carried from 7.2b-2f). It is a verbatim transcription, and the probe now proves the SHADER agrees with the transcription - but both could be wrong together if the transcription of `method371` is wrong.
3. **No `GlSceneRenderer` wiring, no ground, no ground resolver.** (B) and (C) below are untouched.

**Next: (B) THE GROUND RESOLVER or (C) THE WIRING - the operator's order is still open.** ⚠ Note that flipping `supportsTextures()` to `true` changes what the wiring step sees: `allRepresentable()` can now be true for a textured model, but (B) remains the only piece with real unknowns.

#### Phase 7.2b-2h DONE (2026-10-06) - the ground seam's coordinate handover was wrong for non-flat tiles, and (B) is a MEASUREMENT job rather than a reading job

**Why this step exists.** (B) is "the ground resolver": derive the mapping from `drawGroundTriangle`'s nine camera-space ints into texture coordinates. The first productive move was not to derive it but to check the **seam that feeds it**, since a resolver fed wrong coordinates would have been debugged in the wrong place.

**THE DEFECT, and it is a real one.** `WorldController.method315` draws a tile quad as **two** triangles, each with its own picking block and its own rasteriser branch. For the **first** triangle, the software fallback branches on `class43.aBoolean721`:

- flat → `Texture.method378(..., i2, i3, l1, l3, i4, k4, k2, j2, j3, ...)`
- else → `Texture.method378(..., l2, l1, i3, j4, k4, i4, k3, j3, j2, ...)`

The seam passed the **flat** set unconditionally. So on a **non-flat** tile a listener received `i2` where the software uses `l2` for the same vertex's `u`, and so on for all nine - a whole corner's worth of coordinates from a different tile shape. **Fixed by repeating the software's own branch at the seam**, which makes the hand-over faithful by construction:

```java
flatGround ? i2 : l2, flatGround ? i3 : l1, flatGround ? l1 : i3,
flatGround ? l3 : j4, flatGround ? i4 : k4, flatGround ? k4 : i4,
flatGround ? k2 : k3, flatGround ? j2 : j3, flatGround ? j3 : j2
```

**⚠⚠ THE DRAFTED ARGUMENT THAT WAS REJECTED, kept because it is exactly the trap.** The first write-up claimed the two sets are interchangeable: on a flat tile all four corners share a world height, so the camera-space planes are affine in the two world coordinates, hence any three corners determine the same plane. **False as used** - the two sets differ by *variable* (`i2` vs `l2`), not by vertex ordering, so they are not one plane described twice. Had it shipped, it would have "justified" a fix that was still wrong. The seam now proves nothing and constructs everything.

**All three hooks audited, recorded rather than assumed to match:**

| hook | software fallback | seam |
|---|---|---|
| `method315` tri 1 (`:1697`) | **branches** on `aBoolean721` | now repeats the branch ✅ |
| `method315` tri 2 (`:1736`) | **no branch** - always the flat set | already matched ✅ (but ASYMMETRIC to tri 1 - flagged) |
| `method316` overlay (`:1993`) | three planes, all indexed `(l2, j3, l3)` | already matched ✅ **the only self-consistent one** |

**⚠⚠ THE BLOCKER, which is what makes this a finding rather than a fix: the plane SEMANTICS are not safely derivable by reading.** The corner table is now extracted (`A=(j1,k1)` X=`i2` Y=`l3` Z=`k2`; `B=(j1+1,k1)` X=`i3` Y=`i4`; `C=(j1+1,k1+1)` X=`l2` Y=`j4`; `D=(j1,k1+1)` X=`l1` Y=`k4` Z=`j2`), and hook 1's flat set **fails to agree with itself**: `u`/`v` are in corner order `(A,B,D)` while `w` is in `(A,D,C)` - a different *set* of vertices, not a permutation of one set. And the projection at `WorldController.java:1657-1664` appears to use some **unrotated** values (`i3`, `i4`, `j2`) for one corner while the other three use rotated ones. `method315` is decompiled with heavy variable reuse, so any inference here rests on the decompiler. **Therefore (B) must MEASURE, and the instrument already exists in this repo**: 7.2b-2f drove the real `Texture.method378` against parked textures whose brightness blocks **label themselves**, reading the mapping off the painted pixels rather than restating the arithmetic. The same approach settles these nine ints.

**Also documented at the seam** (`ui/SceneRasterizer.dispatchGroundTriangle`): the nine values are `Texture.method378`'s own `s0..s8`, three planes of three divided per pixel by `method379`, and they are **not** in one vertex order across the planes - so a consumer must mirror the software rather than normalise the layout.

**NOTHING THAT IS DRAWN TODAY CHANGED.** No rasteriser is installed in production, so `dispatchGroundTriangle` returns `false` on its first line and the software fallback runs untouched; the client is byte-for-byte the tree the golden master gate passed, `GlSceneRenderer`'s methods still `return false`, and `GlBatcher` still has zero production call sites. The fix changed what a **future** listener receives.

**GATES:** harness **376/376** unchanged, warnings **exactly 100**, `clean check installBin` **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**, `ReadLints` clean.

**⚠⚠ ENVIRONMENT FINDING, and it will bite every future session: `RunTests.bat` no longer runs on this machine.** It prefers `jdk1.8.0_202` if present (**it is**), whose `javac` defaults to **Cp1252**, while `test/ClientHarness.java` contains `⚠` in comments - so the test compile dies with **18 `unmappable character for encoding Cp1252` errors** that look exactly like a broken harness but are a JDK default. `test/ClientHarness.java` is **unmodified** (`git status --porcelain` confirms only the four intended files). Workaround used: compile the harness with the PATH `javac` (**25**, UTF-8 by default) or add `-encoding UTF-8` to the `.bat`. ⚠ **The `.bat` should be fixed rather than worked around, or the harness will look broken to the next session.**

**⚠ NOT CLAIMED:** no live client (unchanged from 7.2g - still impossible, zero call sites); **the plane semantics were still UNKNOWN at this step** (⚠ superseded by 7.2i below, which measured them and found the nine slots are the mapping itself), and the fix above is faithful to the software rather than *correct about the world*; hook 2's asymmetry (flat set on a non-flat tile) is **reported, not repaired** - changing it would change software output, which the golden master forbids.

**Next: (B) properly.** Build the measuring instrument first - park self-labelling textures and drive the real `Texture.method378`/`method379` over the three hooks so the nine-int layout is READ OFF the output, then write the resolver against what the measurement says. ⚠ Do not derive the layout from `method315` by reading; that is the step this session established is unsafe.

#### Phase 7.2b-2i DONE AND MEASURED (2026-10-06) - the ground's nine slots are measured, and the measurement REFUTED the hypothesis the probe was built on

**The instrument** (`test/ClientHarness.java`, `groundTexturePlaneRoles`) drives the REAL `Texture.method378` on a parked texture whose **every texel label is its own ARRAY INDEX**, with all three corner shades **0** - which is block 0 and shift 0, so `method379`'s darkening and its shift are **both identities** and the painted colour *is* the sampled index. **The fixed-point arithmetic is never restated by the test**; the labels are inputs on both sides and the index is the software's own output.

⚠ **Why the label is the raw INDEX and not `(col,row)`:** `method379` reads `ai1[(j & 0xfc0) + (i >> 6)]` - the row is masked to six bits but the **column is not**, so a column past 63 spills into the following row's entries. Labelling the index keeps the measurement exact under that spill instead of decoding the spill as a different texel.

⚠⚠ **THE RESULT, AND IT IS A REFUTATION RATHER THAN A CONFIRMATION.** The probe's discriminator was: perturb one slot, re-run, and see which output coordinate moved - a `u` numerator moves the **column only**, a `v` numerator the **row only**, a `w` denominator **both**, so the nine slots would partition 3/3/3 and that partition is what a resolver needs.

**Measured: 0 column-only, 0 row-only, 9 that move BOTH.**

So there is **no per-coordinate plane to isolate by perturbation**, and the nine slots are **not** per-vertex `(u,v,w)`. This is consistent with `method378`'s own structure once re-read with that question in mind: **every slot reaches the `w` ramp** - `j6` is built from `s0..s5`, and `k6` and `l6` from `s3..s8`.

⚠ **Vacuity guards ran BEFORE the conclusion was drawn**, per this phase's rule: no label is zero (a zero texel is discarded as a cut-out), all 16384 labels are distinct, the reference run paints >1000 pixels, it samples **2386 distinct indices**, and **every sampled index lands in block 0** - so the mapping is genuinely exercised and "shade 0 changed nothing" is measured rather than assumed.

⚠ **Recorded WITHOUT explanation, because none is established:** at the painted pixel nearest each of the three screen vertices the sampled **column is 0 in all three cases** while the rows differ (`13`, `40`, `54`). Left as an open question rather than guessed at.

⚠⚠ **THE SCREEN-DEPENDENCE PROBE, and it is the one that settles the question: does the sampled field depend on the screen triangle at all?** Noticed while re-reading with that question in mind: `method378`'s ramp coefficients (`l4`, `i5`, `j5`, `k5`, `l5`, `i6`, `j6`, `k6`, `l6`) are built from the slots and contain **no screen coordinates at all**, while the screen coordinates reach only the edge walk. So if the nine are already the mapping, the sampled index at a pixel cannot depend on the screen triangle - only the coverage can.

**Gates: harness 376 -> 386 (10 new), warnings exactly 100, `clean check installBin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL, `ReadLints` clean.** ⚠ `src/` was **not touched** in 7.2i, so the software path and the client are unchanged from 7.2h.

⚠⚠ **A SECOND PROBE, CORRECTED, SETTLED IT: WITH SPAN STARTS HELD FIXED THE NINE SLOTS ALONE DETERMINE THE MAPPING.** The first version moved all three vertices and gave 75890 shared / 42701 identical, which *looked* like a refutation; it was **confounded** by `method379`'s truncated span-start advance (`l1 += (k2 >> 3) * (l - textureInt1)`). Corrected to move only the RIGHT vertex - keeping the left edge, the branch, and the 8-pixel block boundaries fixed - the result is **93864 shared pixels, 93864 identical, 0 differing**. So the sampled index is independent of the screen triangle; **the nine slots are not per-vertex texture coordinates**, and the screen vertices decide only coverage.

⚠⚠ **WHAT THAT MEANS FOR THE RESOLVER, and it withdraws an assumption the wiring step would otherwise have made: the ground cannot go through the model shader shape.** A GL path must carry the nine slots (or the ramp coefficients, which `method378` derives from the slots as a **pure function of the nine**) **per triangle** and evaluate the mapping from screen position - not interpolate them as vertex attributes. This is the same `method378` the model path feeds per-vertex camera-space triples to, so **the two paths do not share one shader shape.**

⚠⚠ **AND THE CONFOUND IS ITSELF WORTH KEEPING: the rasteriser's texture mapping is NOT an exact function of `(x, y)`** - it carries a span-start-dependent truncation. So a resolver must be compared against the software with a tolerance or on identical span structure; a bit-exact per-pixel oracle will fail for a reason unrelated to the resolver. ⚠ **The confounded probe is retained in the suite as a printed observation, not a check, so the next reader sees the 75890/42701 figure and its explanation rather than rediscovering it.**

⚠⚠ **THE MODEL PATH'S SHAPE WAS THEN PUT TO THE SAME QUESTION, AND IT IS FLAGGED RATHER THAN FIXED.** `ui/GlFacePipeline` hands this same `method378` per-vertex camera-space triples (plane-major: slots 0-2 are the three vertices' `u`, 3-5 their `v`, 6-8 their `w`) and `GlBatcher`'s shader interpolates them `noperspective` and divides by the interpolated `w`. **That shape makes the mapping depend on the SCREEN TRIANGLE through the barycentric weights; the software's demonstrably does not.** Tested without needing the software's texel scale - by predicting `(u/w, v/w)` under the per-vertex shape for the very same two triangles and asking whether the prediction moves: **it moves at 93855 of 93864 shared pixels, while the software is invariant at all of them.**

⚠⚠ **HOWEVER - AND THIS CAVEAT IS WHAT KEEPS IT A FLAG RATHER THAN A BUG REPORT: the worst deviation is only 0.049 in ratio units, and the count used a 1e-6 threshold, so it largely says "not bit-identical" rather than "visibly different".** Whether it yields a wrong TEXEL depends on the texel scale, which this test does **not** pin. **The honest statement is therefore: the two shapes are structurally different - measured, not argued - and whether that is VISIBLE is not established.**

⚠⚠ **THE SCALE ALGEBRA, WORKED OUT AT THE END OF THE SESSION, AND IT REMOVES ONE POSSIBLE EXPLANATION FOR THE MODEL-PATH FLAG ABOVE.** `method379` computes `i = u_num / (w_num >> 12)` and then `col = i >> 6`, and BOTH ramps carry a `<< 14` (`l4 = ... << 14` and `j6 = ... << 14`), so the `2^14`s cancel against the `>> 12` and the `>> 6` leaves:

**`col = 64 * u_num / w_num`** (and `row = 64 * v_num / w_num`).

⚠ **So the software IS "affine numerator / affine denominator, times the texture size" - the SAME FORM the shader uses.** The earlier suspicion that the two differ by a fixed 512x scale factor is **wrong**, and the shader's `uv * size` convention is right in form. **The 64 here is the LOW-MEM texture size, so the "times size" is real and size-dependent, exactly as `GlBatcher` does it.**

⚠⚠ **THAT SHARPENS THE MODEL-PATH FLAG TO A SINGLE, ANSWERABLE QUESTION: are the two ramps equal to the barycentric interpolation of the per-vertex triples over the screen triangle?** For a per-vertex attribute they MUST be - the affine coefficients then depend on the three screen vertices. **Measured: they are not** (the field is invariant at 93864/93864 when a vertex moves). So the ramps are NOT interpolations of per-vertex attributes, and the shader's shape cannot reproduce them. ⚠ **This is now a contradiction to be resolved rather than a difference to be sized: either `method484` does NOT pass the nine that `GlFacePipeline` assumes it does, or the ramps are being read wrongly.** ⚠ **The cheap next test is in-harness and settles which: capture the nine the software ACTUALLY passes for a textured fixture face (7.2c already drives `method484` by reflection) and compare them against `GlFacePipeline`'s nine for the same face.** If they differ, the pipeline is wrong and the ground work is unaffected; if they match, the ramp reading needs revisiting before anything is built on it.

**⚠ NOT CLAIMED:** the resolver is **still unwritten**. What changed is that the SHAPE it must take is now known from measurement, and two plausible wrong shapes are excluded by measurement rather than by reading.

⚠⚠ **SUPERSEDED (same day, Phase 7.2b-2j) - READ THE NEXT SECTION BEFORE USING ANYTHING ABOVE.** The "scale algebra" derivation immediately above (`col = 64 * u_num / w_num`, therefore a ratio of two screen-affine functions) **does not describe the output**, and neither the screen-independence result nor the two-shape comparison above is interpretable the way it is written, because **every probe in this phase fed `method378` slots and screen coordinates that had no relationship to each other** while `Model.method443` always projects one from the other. See 7.2b-2j.

### Phase 7.2b-2j — The projection-consistent run, and why it matters

⚠⚠ **SUPERSEDED BY 7.2b-2k: THE REFUTATION BELOW IS WITHDRAWN - IT WAS INTEGER OVERFLOW.** The geometry used here (camera values up to ~5000) overflowed `method378`'s ramps (`l4` = -2236743680, past `Integer.MIN_VALUE`), so the ramps wrapped. On an in-range geometry the same instrument gives an iso-column residual of **0.54 px** instead of 61.4, and the code-derived ramp formula reproduces the rasteriser at **92.5% within one texel**. **Read 7.2b-2k before using anything in this section.** The section is kept because the *reasoning* that produced the wrong answer is the useful part, and because the "inconsistent inputs" observation it opens with is still true.

⚠⚠ **THE PROBES IN 7.2i WERE INPUT-INCONSISTENT, AND THAT IS NOT A DETAIL.** `Model.method443` projects a camera-space triple with `sx = textureInt1 + (u << 9) / w` and `sy = textureInt2 + (v << 9) / w` and passes **those** coordinates to `Texture.method378` alongside the nine. No probe before this one did that. With a consistent projection `u/w = (sx - textureInt1) / 512` is **already affine in screen space**, so its barycentric interpolation returns the value itself - so a set of plane coefficients that is *screen-independent* is exactly what reproduces an affine-in-screen quantity. **7.2i's conclusion therefore followed from the way its inputs were built, not from the rasteriser.**

**The run:** one triangle, projection-consistent by construction, three `w` values in ratio 1 : 2 : 2.5 so that the affine and per-vertex shapes are far apart. Results on **11849 unsaturated pixels** (13158 saturated, excluded):

| shape | pixels matching (tol 2 texels) |
|---|---|
| AFFINE — interpolate the per-vertex ratio `u/w` | **12 / 11849** |
| PER-VERTEX — interpolate `u`, `v`, `w`, divide by interpolated `w` (`GlBatcher`) | **7 / 11849** |

Implied scale `col / (64 * u/w)` spans **[0.079, 54.9]** for the affine claim. **Neither shape is what `method378` computes.**

⚠⚠ **THE CODE-DERIVED FORM IS REFUTED TOO.** `method379` computes `i = l1 / (j2 >> 12)` with `l1`, `j2` advanced by fixed per-row and per-pixel steps and only ever divided. That form has a **scale-free, parameter-free signature**: pixels sharing one measured column must lie on a **straight line**, since `Num/Den = const` is a line equation. **Measured worst residual: 61.4 px over 62 columns** — they do not. So the ramp arithmetic is **not** accounted for by the current reading, and the derivation recorded in 7.2i must not be used.

**Rig facts that are now asserted rather than assumed:** `Framebuffer.initDrawingArea(i, j, ai)` assigns `width = j`, so this rig is **503 wide by 765 tall**, while `textureInt1/2 = 382/251` are half the *height* and half the *width* — **transposed relative to the buffer**. That is why the valid positive-column x-range is only `382..502`; the first attempt ran off the right edge and silently lost most of its triangle. The existing probes' `p % width` was in fact correct (503 is the real width).

**⚠ NOT ESTABLISHED, and deliberately so:** no shape is now believed correct; nothing on the model path was changed; the resolver is still unwritten. **The 7.2i flag against `GlBatcher` is downgraded, not cleared:** the model path's divergence is real, but the software's own shape has just been shown to be *neither* candidate, so `GlBatcher` being wrong is no longer supported by these runs.

⚠⚠ **SUPERSEDED BY 7.2b-2k: the paragraph immediately above is itself now wrong.** The software's shape is **not** "neither candidate" - it is the ramp formula, confirmed at 92.5%-within-one-texel, and the reason the run said otherwise was integer overflow in the chosen geometry. See 7.2b-2k.

**Next — the missing term, with a source-verified prime suspect.** With consistent inputs **more than half the triangle saturates** at `col = 63`, which a shipped client could not do. `method484` passes the **face's** screen coordinates (`anIntArray1665/1666[j, k, l]`) but the nine from `anIntArray1668/1669/1670[anIntArray1643/1644/1645[textureIndex]]`, and `anIntArray1643/1644/1645` are a **separate triple of vertex indices parsed from the model file** (`Model.java:1134-1136`) — **not** the face's corners. This instrument **assumed** they are the same vertices. **Settle that on a real textured model first**, then re-run this instrument.

### Phase 7.2b-2k — The nine-slot mapping is pinned (and 7.2j's refutation was overflow)

✅✅ **THIS IS THE PHASE THAT HANDS THE RESOLVER ITS FORMULA.** The suspect named at the end of 7.2j was real, and chasing it overturned 7.2j's headline conclusion.

**Finding 1 — which vertices the nine come from, MEASURED not read.** `method484` takes screen coordinates from the face's own corners but the nine from `anIntArray1668/1669/1670[anIntArray1643/1644/1645[index]]`. New instrument `texturedFaceNineSourceIsMeasured`: park the face corners at vertex indices 0,1,2 and the texture triple at 3,4,5 with **different** camera values, drive the REAL `method484`, and perturb each set in turn. Result:

| perturbation | pixels changed |
|---|---|
| camera values at the FACE-CORNER indices | **0** |
| camera values at the TEXTURE-TRIPLE indices | **11140 of 11155** |
| the screen triangle (coverage control) | changes |

So the nine are the texture triple, and the two triples **need not name the same vertices** — which the existing fixture already illustrates (its face 2 has corners `{0,3,1}` and texture triple `{2,3,0}`).

⚠⚠ **Finding 2 — 7.2j's refutation was INTEGER OVERFLOW.** `method378` builds its ramps as 2x2 minors shifted left by **14, 8 or 5 bits** into an `int`. The 7.2j geometry (camera values up to ~5000) made `l4 = -2236743680`, past `Integer.MIN_VALUE = -2147483648`. The ramps wrapped, and every "shape" comparison was then a comparison against a wrapping counter. Evidence: the **same** iso-column instrument on an in-range geometry drops from **61.4 px to 0.54 px** residual. Guard added: `nineRampsFitInInt` recomputes all nine ramps in `long` and the run **asserts its own geometry cannot wrap**.

✅✅ **Finding 3 — the code-derived reading is CONFIRMED, and it is the resolver's formula.** On an in-range geometry, the formula transcribed straight from the code — build `l4,i5,j5,k5,l5,i6,j6,k6,l6` as `method378` does, then `col = ((u_num / (w_num >> 12)) >> 6)` with `u_num = l4 + j5*dy + (i5>>3)*dx` (and likewise for `v_num`, `w_num`) — reproduces the real rasteriser at:

**7206 / 10964 texels EXACT, 10146 / 10964 (92.5%) within one texel.**

The residual is `method379`'s truncated span seeding, not a semantic gap.

⚠ **The two rival shapes remain refuted, now on inputs that are provably valid:** interpolating `u/w` matches **0** of 10962; interpolating `u,v,w` and dividing (the `GlBatcher` shape) matches **12**.

**⚠ ARCHITECTURAL CONSEQUENCE, unchanged from 7.2i but now on solid ground:** the mapping is a ratio of two screen-affine functions carrying **no per-vertex interpolation**, so a GL path must carry the nine (or the nine ramps) **per triangle** and evaluate from screen position.

**Next:** (i) measure whether `GlBatcher`'s per-vertex shape is *visibly* wrong, by comparing it against this confirmed formula on real nine-slot values rather than against a shape derived the same way; (ii) build the ground/model resolver on the confirmed formula, carrying the ramps per triangle.

### Phase 7.2b-2l — The resolver: `ui.TextureRamps`, and its handoff PROVEN

✅✅ **This closes (B) as a unit.** 7.2k pinned the formula; this turns it into a real class and, more importantly, settles the one question that decided whether the formula was *usable* on a GPU.

**The class.** `Proxy Client/src/ui/TextureRamps.java` - pure, GL-free, no `Texture` reads (`textureInt1/2` are parameters, per this package's one-owner rule and for harness-testability):

| member | meaning |
|---|---|
| `of(t0..t8, textureInt1, textureInt2)` | the nine ramps, built exactly as `method378` builds them |
| `uNumerator / vNumerator / wNumerator(x, y)` | the three screen-affine numerators |
| `column / row(x, y)` | the software's own texel, clamped as `method379` clamps it |
| `overflows()` | whether any ramp wrapped a 32-bit int |
| `attributeAt(x, y)` | the three values a GL path hands the sink per vertex |

⚠ **The arithmetic is `int`, wrapping included, on purpose.** `method378` shifts each 2x2 minor left by 14, 8 or 5 bits into an `int`; a resolver that used `long` to "fix" the overflow would disagree with the software it exists to reproduce.

✅✅ **The shader handoff - the deliverable.** The mapping is a **ratio** of two screen-affine functions, so no shader can reproduce it by interpolating a ratio or a pre-divided coordinate (that is exactly the per-vertex shape 7.2k refuted). But the three **numerators are affine**, so:

> evaluate `uNum/vNum/wNum` at the triangle's three screen vertices → interpolate them linearly (`noperspective`) → divide in the fragment shader.

**Measured against the real rasteriser: 10962 of 10964 pixels EXACT, 10964 of 10964 within one texel.** The mapping is reproducible on the GPU.

**The class against the software:** **10569 of 10964 (96.4%) within one texel, 7712 exact.** The residual is `method379`'s truncated span seeding, exactly the tolerance the class doc claims - so code and documentation agree.

⚠ **Overflow is a first-class branch, not a comment.** `overflows()` reports the 7.2j geometry `true` and the in-range fixture `false`. Where the ramps wrap the software's mapping is still well-defined (it *is* the wrap), but it is **no longer affine**, so a GL path must **decline** such a triangle rather than hand the shader something it cannot reproduce.

⚠⚠ **Consequence for the MODEL path, recorded and deliberately NOT acted on.** `GlBatcher` carries raw camera-space `(u,v,w)` and divides - the **right shape, wrong attribute**. The resolver says the attribute must be the three numerators. `GlFacePipeline` does hold the camera triple, so it *can* build the same ramp form - but it must not be changed on the strength of this test, because the model path's nine come from the **texture-coordinate triple** (7.2k) and from `method443`'s own projection, not from the ground's nine values.

**Gates: harness 400 → 407 (7 new), warnings exactly 100, `clean check installBin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL, `ReadLints` clean.**
⚠ **NOT CLAIMED: nothing is WIRED.** `GlSceneRenderer`'s three methods still `return false`; `TextureRamps` has **test call sites only**; there is still not one GL pixel on screen; `src/` gained one new file and changed no existing one.

⚠⚠ **Environment trap worth more than the code, recorded because it cost real work.** `Set-Content -Encoding UTF8` in PowerShell 5.1 **decodes UTF-8 as Cp1252 on the way in**, so it silently destroyed every `⚠` in `test/ClientHarness.java` and added a BOM; and PowerShell's `-replace` is **case-insensitive**, so a "mechanical" rename also rewrote a method name. Both were caught only because the glyph count was checked afterwards (**77 restored, 0 mojibake diff lines, 1246 insertions / 0 deletions**). **Edit these files with the editor tools, not the shell.**

**Next:** (C) THE WIRING - and, before or alongside it, deciding whether `GlFacePipeline` should build the ramp form for the model path.

### Phase 7.2c-2 DONE AND HEADLESS-VERIFIED (2026-10-06) - the ground's depth, and the C2 bug that only this step could expose

✅✅ **Outcome: the ground is drawn by GL, and a scene of model + untextured ground is WHOLE and IS READ BACK.** `GROUND_DEPTH_DECLINE` is gone.

**Part 1 - the seam carries the depth.** `SceneRasterizer.Implementation#drawGroundTriangle` and `dispatchGroundTriangle` take an added trailing `int depth`, and all three `WorldController` hooks pass `Fog.sceneDepth`. ⚠ **It is read, not recomputed**, and that was the reconnaissance finding: each hook sits a few lines below its drawer's own assignment - `:1656` `Fog.sceneDepth = (k2 + j2 + k3 + j3) / 4` in `method315`, `:1963` `Fog.sceneDepth = (dx + dy) * 96 + 300` in `method316` - so the value threaded through is **the same number the software fogs that tile with**. Adding a parameter does not touch the nine or their order, which is what the seam's contract is about.

⚠ **Per-TILE, not per-vertex, and that mirrors the software.** `method315` submits a whole tile as a unit in painter order and fogs it with that one mean depth, so tile-level ordering is what the software actually does. A grazing tile could still show an artefact where a model intersects it; the per-vertex depths are in the nine's `w` plane if that turns out to matter.

**Part 2 - the ground is submitted, not declined.** Untextured tiles go to `batch.triangle` with colours from `GlFacePipeline.resolveCornerColour` - which **is** `method374`'s contract, confirmed by reading it: fade the CODE through `Fog.fadeHsl`, then look the palette up per pixel. Textured tiles go to `batch.textured` with `TextureRamps`' numerators evaluated at the tile's own projected corners, anchored at the screen origin.

⚠⚠ **THE FINDING: THE MODEL PATH WAS MAPPING A DEPTH MEASURED FROM THE WRONG ORIGIN, AND THE GROUND COULD NOT BE WIRED CORRECTLY UNTIL IT WAS FIXED.** `method443` writes `anIntArray1667[v] = camZ - k2` - relative to the MODEL'S OWN origin - and `GlModelProjection` mirrors that faithfully (correctly: `GlClipper` consumes the same relative form and the projection oracle pins it). But `k2` differs per model, so `depthToZ(vertexDepth[v])` placed every model at `z = 0.5` at its own origin, making two models at different distances **incomparable** in a shared depth buffer - a distant model would occlude a near one. The ground's depth is absolute, so the two conventions could not be mixed.

✅ **Fixed at the CALL SITES, not in the projection**, by a new `GlFacePipeline#absoluteZ(relativeDepth)` that adds `lastSceneDepth` (which IS `k2`) back before mapping. Making the projected array itself absolute would have broken `GlClipper` and the oracle that pins the projection to the software. ⚠ **Nothing has ever rendered this**, which is why it survived: the model oracle compares z WITHIN a triangle, where a per-model offset is invisible.

⚠ **A smaller finding worth keeping: the seam's colour/vertex pairing is faithful, and proving that needed the CALL SITES rather than the parameter names.** `method374` takes `(y0,y1,y2, x0,x1,x2, ...)`, so `method374(j6, l6, l5, i6, k6, k5, c0, c1, c2)` looks like a different vertex order from the seam's `(i6,j6, k6,l6, k5,l5)` - and is the SAME triangle in the SAME order. A "fix" based on the signature would have attached every colour to the wrong corner.

**The one ground decline left, named:** `GROUND_LOWMEM_DECLINE`. On low detail the software does not texturise ground at all - it darkens the codes through `WorldController.method317`/`anIntArray485` and draws them flat through `method374`, neither reachable from `ui`. ⚠ Off in the detail level `client.setHighMem()` selects, reachable via the low-detail setting, so the harness pins `WorldController.lowMem` rather than trusting its `true` default. Plus `GROUND_COLOUR_DECLINE` (code outside the palette) and `GROUND_MAPPING_DECLINE` (no atlas, wrapped ramps, or the sink declining).

**Gates: harness 456 → 464 (8 new), warnings exactly 100, `gradlew check` BUILD SUCCESSFUL, `ReadLints` clean.** ⚠ **NOT CLAIMED: no live client, and still no GL pixel on screen** - the frame is read back into a RECORDING batch, not a driver. ⚠ **And still open and unchanged: THE SHADE FOG FADE.** `method374` and `method378` both fade the shade codes through `Fog.fadeHsl(shade, Fog.sceneDepth)`, `GlTextures.shade` exists to express it, and neither the model path nor the ground path calls it - so fogged TEXTURED geometry is at the wrong brightness. It belongs in `GlFacePipeline` and must be fixed for both paths at once.

**Next:** the LIVE GATE (7.4) - the first real GL pixels, with `renderer=gl`. The headless path is complete for a whole frame; what has never run is the driver.

### Phase 7.4c DONE AND MEASURED (2026-10-06) — the ramp-overflow guard, driven against the real `method379`: it is **STRICTLY conservative at the low end and NECESSARY at the high end**, and a cost-free discriminator separates the two

✅✅ **Outcome: `TextureRamps.overflows()` is neither simply right nor simply strict, and the boundary between the two is now measured and has a name.** New instrument `rampOverflowIsMeasured` (harness, both detail levels) drives the REAL `Texture.method378`/`method379` through `groundRasterIndices` on a **controlled world scale**.

**The experiment.** 7.2j's in-range ground triangle, with all nine camera-space slots multiplied by `k`. That is a pure world scale, and the mapping is invariant under it — the ramps and the walked numerator/denominator all scale by `k^2` — so the PICTURE is unchanged while the intermediate integers grow by `k^2` and cross 2³¹. The screen triangle is computed from the **unscaled** slots, so every `k` rasterises literally the same pixels and the only variable is where the ints sit. Four things are compared per `k`, on the same pixels:

| `k` | ramps overflow? | rasteriser picture vs `k=1` | exact affine form (`TextureRamps.column`) | SHADER HANDOFF (numerators at the vertices, interpolated) | wrapped == exact |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | no | 10964/10964 | 10569/10964 (96.4%) | 10569/10964 | 10964/10964 |
| 2 | **yes** | **10962/10964** | **10569/10964** | **10569/10964** | **10964/10964** |
| 4 | yes | 4/10964 | 1993/10964 (18%) | 143/10964 (1.3%) | 0/10964 |
| 8 | yes | 0/10964 | 2797/10964 | 53/10964 | 0/10964 |
| 16 | yes | 2/10964 | 1710/10964 | 98/10964 | 1/10964 |
| 64 | yes | 3/10964 | 5984/10949 | 154/10963 | 2/10964 |

(size 64 shown; size 128 repeats it — 9998/10964 and 10022/10964 at `k = 1,2`, collapsing to 1636 and 55 at `k = 4`.)

**Finding 1 — THERE IS AN OVERFLOW THAT COSTS NOTHING, so the guard is not merely cautious but WRONG about that case.** At `k = 2` the ramps overflow and `texturedRamps()` declines, yet the rasteriser's own picture is the `k = 1` picture at 10962/10964 pixels **and the shader handoff still reproduces it at the control's own accuracy** (10569/10964). The guard's stated reason — "no longer affine, so a shader cannot reproduce it" — is FALSE there. ⚠ This settles 7.4b's open worry in the direction that matters: `overflows()` **over-declines**, and the 25 of 128 faces the live client reported were not necessarily unrepresentable.

**Finding 2 — and there is an overflow that costs the picture, so the decline is not removable either.** At `k >= 4` the rasteriser's own picture stops being the `k = 1` picture (≤ 4 of 10964 pixels survive) and the handoff collapses to ~1%. ⚠⚠ **AND THE EXACT AFFINE FORM COLLAPSES WITH IT** (18% at `k = 4`, against 96.4% at the control) — which is the part that constrains the fix: `TextureRamps.column` shares `method378`'s int arithmetic *including the wrap*, so if the wrap were merely a moved-but-still-affine mapping the exact form would keep tracking the rasteriser and only the handoff would fail. It does not. **The wrapped rasteriser is not an affine function of `(x, y)` at all**, so "narrow the guard to a cheap surrogate for `column()`" is not available as a fix.

**Finding 3 — the mechanism, read out of the printed ramps, and it is "does the wrap CANCEL".** At `k = 2` every minor is `4x` its `k = 1` value, and the two that exceed 2³¹ (`l4`, `j6`) fold back by exactly `+2^32` — a constant the **evaluation's own wrap removes again**, leaving `u = 4*u(k=1)` and `w = 4*w(k=1)` at the centroid (`-677376000 = 4 * -169344000`, `-1509504000 = 4 * -377376000`) for the whole picture. The ratio is then exact. At `k = 4` the `u` plane wraps twice and the `w` plane four times, so the representatives are no longer a common multiple and the ratio breaks.

**Finding 4 — THE DISCRIMINATOR, and it is O(1) per face.** The picture survives exactly when the **wrapped evaluation still reproduces the exact one** — the ramps recomputed in `long`, which is what the nine *mean*. Measured pixel by pixel, the two classifications agree **scale for scale at both detail levels** (`k = 2`: 10964/10964 wrapped==exact and the picture survived; `k = 4`: 0/10964 and the picture is gone). Both forms are closed in `(x, y)`, so a resolver can ask this of the triangle's screen vertices and needs no rasteriser. ⚠ Pinned as a **measured correspondence on this sweep**, not derived.

⚠ **NO PRODUCTION CODE WAS TOUCHED AND THE GUARD STILL DECLINES EXACTLY AS IT DID.** This step is measurement only: `texturedRamps()` is unchanged, no live run, no GL pixels.

**Gates: harness 489 → 513 (24 new, 12 per detail level), failures 0, `ReadLints` clean. MUTATION-PROVED: folding the exact `u` base back through an `int` cast in the measurement's own `exactRamps` turns 4 checks red (513 → 509, RESULT FAIL)** — the two "discriminator is clean" checks and the two "it tracks the picture" checks, i.e. the new checks can fail.

**Next (7.4d): WIDEN THE GUARD USING THE DISCRIMINATOR, then re-run live.** `texturedRamps()` should, on `overflows()`, build the exact (`long`) ramps as well and decline **only if the wrapped evaluation fails to reproduce them at the triangle's screen vertices** — otherwise submit the wrapped numerators, which the shader will reproduce. ⚠ The live re-run then answers the question the harness cannot: **are the 25 of 128 faces costless overflows (GL starts drawing) or picture-breaking ones?** ⚠ If they are picture-breaking, the threshold says the model's camera-space XY products are far past 131072, which a normal scene should not produce — so that outcome points at an upstream bug in the nine rather than at the guard, and would be a hunt rather than a tolerance. ⚠ Unchanged and queued behind it: **the shade fog fade** (both paths, one resolver) and **the settings restore** (`client_settings.properties` must go back to `renderer=software` by COPYING `client_settings.properties.pre-glgate`).

### Phase 8 — Cleanup (last, after the GL path is settled)
- [ ] **8.1 Delete dead weight:** `dump4/`, `jdt-bin/`, and the LWJGL 2 jars if Phase 7 supersedes them.
- [ ] **8.2 Remove superseded code:** `LwjglPresent` (and `GlPresent` if Java2D present is replaced), and any `class`/`jar` no longer referenced. ⚠️ Verify by *absence of imports and a clean compile*, not by filename.
- [ ] **8.3 Reconcile `src` and `bin`** so a stale class file can never be loaded after a rename (`Compile.bat`'s `robocopy` does not delete removed classes — this is how a renamed class silently keeps working from a stale `.class`).
- [ ] **8.4 Final golden-master run against the unmodified server.**

## Verification checklist (the gate for every phase)

1. **Compiles** with the pinned toolchain, warning count not increased.
2. **Logs into the unmodified server** and reaches the game world.
3. **Golden-master route replays identically** (Phase 0.3) — position, animation, and no desync over several minutes.
4. **Combat, item pickup/drop, and a level-up** behave as recorded (these are where cache/model/anim bugs surface).
5. **Cache-heavy actions** work: region change, teleport, NPC spawn, animation-heavy scene (the Rock Crab area where the crash happened).
6. **No new crash** in the console, and specifically the Phase 2 hardening cases hold.
7. **`ObjectCollisionSizes` still matches** the server's `Data/objectSize.cfg`.
8. For Phase 7 only: **software fallback still produces an identical framebuffer**, and alt-tab/resolution-change does not deadlock or lose the GL context.

Because `loadFrames()` runs `loadPacked` and THEN `loadLooseFrameFiles()`, and `method531` never re-reads a populated slot, **the loose file wins and the 474 content for those two ids is unreachable at runtime.** ⚠ **This is recorded as behaviour, NOT as intent** - deleting either loose file, or reordering those two startup lines, would silently revert those animations with nothing failing. #### Phase 4.1c-2c SCOPED AND DONE (2026-10-05) - THREE decision points, not two, and not the same shape as 4.1c-2b

**The ground is drawn by TWO methods, not one.** Measured per method over `WorldController.java`:

| method | mesh it draws | rasteriser calls |
|---|---|---|
| `method315` (decl L1608) | `Class43` = `Ground.aClass43_1311` - the ground/floor mesh | **7** (`method374` x4, `method378` x3) |
| `method316` (decl L1899) | `Class40` = `aClass40_1312` - the per-tile overlay mesh | **4** (`method374` x2, `method378` x2) |

11 in total, which is where the earlier figure came from - but it is **split across two methods**, and `method316` alone has only 4. Both are called from `method314` (the per-tile scene drawer) at L1204/L1207 and L1225/L1231, so one tile's draw runs the ground mesh, then the overlay mesh, then the model sites from 4.1c-2b.

**Correction to the 4.1c-2b write-up, recorded rather than quietly fixed.** Those 21 model sites are in **`method314`** (decl L1142), not `method313` (decl L955 - the queue/culling method that CALLS `method314`), and there is one further caller: a delegating wrapper at `Animable.java:18`. ⚠️ **The plan's own reconnaissance already had this right** (`method313` per-tick render 186 lines vs `method314` ground/tiles 465 lines, at L331), so the error was mine and it contradicted the plan. **4.1c-2b's implementation is unaffected - the hook sits inside `Model.method443`, so it covers every route no matter which method calls it - but the description was wrong and that is worth stating.**

⚠⚠ **The gate must be extended FIRST, and it is not optional: the textured path IS reachable in normal play.** `client.java:3812` sets `Texture.lowMem = false` under high detail, which is exactly the condition that enables the `method378` branches in both drawers. The 4.1c-2a gate pins **only** the flat path (`method374`), so `method378` - used at **5 of the 11** ground sites - would currently be covered by nothing but the live screen.

⚠ **A design constraint that makes this genuinely different from 4.1c-2b: an early return at the top of `method316` would break tile picking.** Unlike `method443`, `method316` does more than draw - inside its per-triangle loop it also runs the hover/tile-marker picking (`method318`, setting `anInt470/471` and `hoverTileX/Y` at L1949-1957) and sets `Texture.aBoolean1462`. So the hook belongs **just before the rasteriser branch, after the picking**, not at the method top - otherwise a future GPU path would silently drop tile markers and hover.

**Shape:** one hook per drawer, placed before the rasteriser branch, dispatching the already-projected triangle (coords, colours, texture id) through a `SceneRasterizer` operation and covering all three variants (`374` flat, `378` textured, `374` lowmem-shaded) with one call each. ⚠️ **Do not dispatch the whole mesh at the method top**, for the picking reason above.

**AS BUILT (2026-10-05), and what this scope got wrong.** The call counts above were right (`method315` 7, `method316` 4), but the conclusion "**one hook per drawer**" was not: ⚠⚠ **the as-built shape is THREE hooks, because `method315` draws a QUAD as two separate triangles, each with its OWN picking block (`method318` x2) and its OWN 3-way branch.** So there are five picking/drawing decision points across the two methods, of which three carry rasteriser calls.

**The design constraint above turned out to be the whole basis of the change:** all three hooks were placed **AFTER** the picking, so a future rasteriser controls WHAT IS SUBMITTED without controlling whether a tile is hovered - the property the live gate then confirmed (markers and hover still work). ⚠️ **The neutrality proof is `git diff -w`: 19 insertions / 0 deletions against a raw 59/40, i.e. the 40 raw deletions are nothing but the re-indentation of the wrapped chain.** ⚠ **My first insertion attempt produced mangled indentation and was reverted rather than committed, which is why the status block quotes the whitespace-ignoring figure.**

**Coverage after this step, stated so it is not over-read:** the scene rasteriser is seamed for **SUBMISSION** - models via `Model.method443`, ground via `method315`/`method316` - which is what a GPU path needs. It is **not** a claim that every rasteriser entry point is seamed: `WorldController.method319`/`method321`/`method305-309`/`method323-324` and `Model`'s own triangle helpers (`method472`, `method479`, `method483`, `method485`, `method480-486`) are untouched.
